# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

## [v0.42.0] - 2026-08-25

`v0.42.0-rc4` promoted unchanged -- the tree is identical apart from this
version bump, so the entries stay under the RC headings that introduced them
rather than being duplicated here. The release is the sum of `v0.42.0-rc1`
through `-rc4`; the compare links at the foot of this file walk it.

## [v0.42.0-rc4] - 2026-08-25

### Fixed
- **nine more early-closing readers, and the class that inverted the answer rather than losing it (closes #905)** -- #898 fixed one `| sort | head` that died 141 under `pipefail`; confirming it turned up nine more instances of the same mechanism, one class of which is worse than the bug that was fixed. A reader that stops reading (`grep -q` leaves on its first match, `head -n1` after one line) strands a writer that is still writing: SIGPIPE, exit 141, `pipefail` promotes 141 to the PIPELINE's status, and an `if` reads that as false. **A successful match is reported as "not found"** -- the status is not lost, it is inverted, and nothing fails loudly; the caller simply takes the other branch. Whether the race is lost is environment-dependent, measured during #898 at **0 failures in 20000 host iterations** (glibc, bash 5.1) against **6.4% inside the alpine test-tools image** (musl, coreutils 9.5, bash 5.2), so "I ran it and it was fine" is not evidence here -- and five of these ship in `dist/`, which every downstream repo executes on whatever host it has. **Class B, the inverted answer (5 sites).** `run.sh`'s already-running guard reported a LIVE container as not running and started a second one over it, instead of refusing; `exec.sh` took the same test negated and refused a container that WAS running, telling the user to start it; `detect_gpu` reported an installed `nvidia-container-toolkit` as missing, so `setup` wrote a GPU-less `.env` on a GPU host; `completions.sh` re-printed the `fpath` hint for a directory already on `$fpath`; and the base-version monitor's dedupe gate read an already-open tracking issue as absent and filed a duplicate -- weekly, in every downstream repo, until someone upgraded. **Class A, the status thrown away (3 sites).** `upgrade.sh:_get_latest_version`, `init.sh:_detect_template_version` and `gitignore.sh:_prune_retired_entries` each met the abort and answered with `|| true`, which stops the crash by discarding the status -- including a genuine failure of the pipeline -- and leaves a quieter defect: an EMPTY answer that reads as a legitimate "nothing found" (no newer release when there is one; a repo stamped with no template version; a retired entry never retracted from the downstream `.gitignore` the template was trying to clean). #898 deliberately did not take that route, and neither does this. **Class C (1 site).** `_tui_conf.sh:_detect_mig` had no suppression at all: 141 straight out of a bare assignment. The live caller is `if _detect_mig; then`, and `if` suspends errexit for the whole function body, so it misreported rather than aborting -- a MIG-enabled host told it had no slices, withholding the UUIDs the user needs for `NVIDIA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`; called outside a condition the same 141 kills setup outright (pinned at status 141, no output). **Every fix removes the dependency rather than the symptom**: each site drains its stream with a `while IFS= read -r` loop over a process substitution and compares in-shell, so no exit status depends on how two processes were scheduled. No `|| true` survives and `pipefail` is untouched. `run.sh` and `exec.sh` asked the identical question with the identical spelling, so the probe is hoisted to `lib/wrapper.sh:_wrapper_container_running`, the module that already owns what the five docker wrappers share. Dropping `grep -oP` from the two tag scans also drops a PCRE dependency that does not exist on a BSD / macOS host, where both had been silently returning `""` behind the `|| true`. **Testing is deterministic, not repeated**: two shared helpers in `test/bats/unit/test_helper.bash` pin the losing interleaving with PATH shims -- a reader that exits without reading a byte, a writer that writes only after it has gone -- so a reintroduced pipeline fails on EVERY run instead of a few percent, the technique `adr_numbering_spec` established in #898. Nine specs gained a case (`run_sh` 67, `exec_sh` 58, `setup_detect` 50, `completions` 14, `base_version_monitor` 13, `upgrade` 48, `init` 53, `gitignore` 47, `tui` 135). `upgrade_spec`'s errexit/pipefail case is rewritten rather than deleted -- it pinned a real contract (rc=0 and an empty answer on an unreachable remote, so `_check`'s guard is what reports it) while describing `|| true` as the mechanism -- and its strict shell became a script FILE, which un-skips it under `COVERAGE`: `bash -c '...'` leaves `${BASH_SOURCE}` empty at top level, so kcov's `PS4` killed it under `set -u` before the code ran, and it had been skipping in precisely the environment where this defect reproduces. **Recurrence guard, with the false-positive count measured before building it.** A naive whole-tree rule is worthless: bats test bodies run with errexit ON and pipefail OFF (measured -- `false | true` yields 0 there), so all ~65 `| head` / `| grep -q` lines under `test/bats/**` are structurally incapable of the inversion, giving ~65 candidates and 0 true positives. Scoped to `dist/` + `script/`, the shell that actually runs under `set -euo pipefail`, the same rule flagged **exactly the nine defects and nothing else** -- and replaying it over every release from v0.30.0 to v0.41.0 flags the same six lines (the nine, before the tree was reorganised) and never anything else, i.e. **zero false positives across twelve releases**. That is the opposite of #895's rejected rule (68 candidates, ~11 true positives, >80% false positive), so this one is built: new `early-close-reader` lint (`script/test/drivers/early_close_reader.sh`, `just test lint --early-close-reader`, `test.sh --early-close-reader-only`, CI job `lint-static (early-close-reader)`), with a region-delimited `# early-close-lint: allow-begin/allow-end` opt-out (no live region today) and a no-vacuous-pass scan-root check. It deliberately does NOT flag a reader that drains (`grep -v`, `grep -c`, which `build.sh` / `prune.sh` / `setup_tui.sh` use), `grep -q` with a file operand, `||`, a here-string, or a comment describing the shape. Its stated limitation: `sed 1q` and `awk 'NR==1{exit}'` also stop reading, but the tree has no instances, and widening to shapes with no instances would trade a measured zero false-positive rate for guesswork. `self_test_yaml_spec`'s `_LINT_TOOLS` completeness guard rises 9 -> 10 and pins the new name; new `early_close_reader_lint_spec.bats` (20). Closes #905.
- **eight environment variables that changed behaviour with nothing a user could read, resolved one by one (refs #895)** -- a sweep found eleven ambient reads that were neither documented where a user looks nor named as private; `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE` went to #896 and `SETUP_CONF` / the `INSTANCE_SUFFIX` residue are tracked in #893 / #881. The remaining eight resolve to document / privatise / remove, with the provenance deciding which. **Documented**: `TEMPLATE_REMOTE` -- the git remote every `.base` operation reads, i.e. the URL whose contents are then *executed* in the consumer's tree -- now has a README section ("Pointing `.base` at a different upstream", translated into all three localized READMEs) covering the SSH / private-fork case and saying plainly what trusting it means; its default URL, previously written out in `upgrade.sh` and `init.sh` with the slug repeated again in `check-base-version.sh`, is one shared constant (`dist/script/base/upstream.sh`), which the generated `main.yaml` `uses:` refs now also read. `WRAPPER_TRANSCRIPT` (which outranks the `[logging] wrapper_transcript` key the user configured and can see in `setup.sh show`) has that precedence stated in README and in `dist/.setup.conf`, and a value that is neither `true` nor `false` now aborts the verb naming the variable instead of silently handing the decision back to the conf key. The base-version monitor's `BASE_REPO` / `MONITOR_LABEL` / `BASE_VERSION_FILE` are documented in CONTEXT.md beside the monitor (including that `BASE_REPO` also names the release link the monitor publishes into a real issue, and that `MONITOR_LABEL` is half the dedupe pair). `SYSTEM_DOCKER_SOCK` is documented in `doc/test/system.md` as the supported way to point the system level at another daemon -- the issue proposed removing it as callerless, which is wrong: `_system_setup` probes it, and the two prerequisite-guard specs depend on it for the per-test isolation that fixed their race (#816). `QUIET` is documented in `build.sh` / `run.sh` `--help` in all four locales, including that it is a different knob from `setup.sh`'s `-q/--quiet`. **Privatised**: the five `TUI_*` knobs (geometry + button captions) are `_TUI_*` internals of the backend wrapper, and the geometry is now a constant, so `TUI_WIDTH=0` can no longer reach `dialog(1)` unvalidated; `VERBOSE` in `stop.sh`, an ambient input created only by a missing `local`, is declared like every sibling flag, so `-v` is the one way to switch the teardown listing on. **Removed**: the `:-1000` fallback for `HOST_UID` / `HOST_GID` in `compose.yaml` -- on any host whose developer is not uid 1000 it left the files the suite wrote into the bind-mounted checkout owned by a stranger, silently. The issue said `just test` always supplies them; it does not, and neither did CI: `just test system` and self-test.yaml's system job drive `docker compose run --rm ci-system` directly, and test.sh passed the ids with `-e` (which sets them *inside* the container) while compose's own `${HOST_UID}` interpolation reads the *caller's* environment. All three now export them, an unset value is refused by name, and a non-numeric one is refused by `_fix_permissions` before it reaches `chown`. Removing the default also makes the ids required of commands that name a service reading neither, because compose interpolates the WHOLE file whatever service is named -- `just docker build --target test-tools` (base's documented self-use of the docker namespace, and the first thing `just test system` runs) was refused, by a message naming `just test`. base's own root `justfile` therefore exports them once, which `just` propagates into every module recipe, so the whole `just` surface resolves and raw `docker compose` stays the only refused path; the paths also reachable via `just -f` (the `system` recipe, `test.sh`) keep setting them for themselves.
- **every name CI creates is now keyed to the run that created it, and every run hands its artifacts back (closes #900)** — the local half of this was already fixed: #892 keyed the test-tools tag to its build inputs and the compose project to the checkout path, and #893 resolved one `PROJECT_NAME` both the wrapper's `-p` and the emitted `compose.yaml` read. On a RUNNER both discriminators are constant. The checkout path is always `/home/runner/work/<repo>/<repo>`, and every run of one commit resolves the same content hash, so `test-tools:local` (loaded with `load: true` in five jobs), the compose project, and the acceptance job's scaffolded `e2e_test` consumer are the SAME names in every concurrent run. Nothing collides today only because `runs-on: ubuntu-latest` hands each job a fresh single-tenant VM — and this repo is moving to self-hosted runners (ADR-00000008 carries the amendment, `coverage-gate` calls itself the self-hosted coverage-floor gate, #766 is the stated prerequisite). The key is `${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}`, set **unconditionally** in ONE workflow-level `env:` block that feeds all three surfaces: `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE`, `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`, and `CI_RUN_KEY` for the acceptance scaffold's directory name (whose basename becomes `IMAGE_NAME`, hence the image tag, the container name and the project). Not a timestamp (two jobs starting in the same second collide, and it cannot be traced back to a run) and **not the commit SHA** (shared by every job of a run and identical across a re-run — precisely when a stale leftover would be reused). Costs nothing in build cache: buildx reuse is keyed by `cache-from` / `cache-to` scope, not by the image tag. **Cleanup is the other half.** A unique name per run means leftovers accumulate on a long-lived host instead of dying with the VM, and the obvious collector — `docker system prune -a` — is actively harmful on exactly that machine, because a shared host's "unused" resources include a concurrent job's in-flight cache and images. New `script/ci/reclaim.sh` removes only what a run can PROVE it created, by label (`base.ci.run=<key>`, stamped on every path that loads a test-tools image, including the pulled-and-retagged hot path) or by a name embedding `ci-<key>` (the scaffolded consumer's artifacts, which downstream tooling names but base does not build). Two layers in one `if: always()` teardown step per docker-using job: `--run` is exact and is the normal path; `--stale 12h` is the backstop for the case `--run` cannot cover at all, a runner killed before its teardown ran. **12h, not prune.sh's local defaults** (networks 10m, images 24h): an artifact belonging to a live run can be as old as the longest a job may run, and GitHub's per-job ceiling is 6h, so the window has to sit above that ceiling or the sweep deletes work in flight. The classes with no ownership marker (dangling images, unattached networks, buildx cache) are delegated to the sibling `prune.sh`, whose docker prune verbs skip in-use resources by definition. Volumes are removed by the exact layer only — age is no evidence of ownership for a resource holding state. **Downstream** rides the reusable-workflow half: `build-worker.yaml` keys its generated `WS_PATH` to the run and reclaims through the same version-matched `.worker-base` checkout the cache-scope script uses, so callers inherit it by bumping the ref they already pin — the generated `main.yaml` caller is untouched and needs no per-repo edit. ShellCheck's base-own pass was rooted one level up (`script/` rather than `script/test/`) in the same change: `script/ci/` had been outside every lint pass.
- **the ADR-numbering lint no longer dies of SIGPIPE and silently withholds the local-CI stamp (closes #898)** — `script/test/drivers/adr_numbering.sh` computed the ADR number range as `printf '%s\n' "${_nums[@]}" | sort | head -n1`. `head` closes the pipe the moment it has its line; a `sort` that has not written yet takes SIGPIPE and dies 141, `pipefail` promotes that to the pipeline's status, and the bare assignment under `set -e` kills the whole lint phase. Measured in the test-tools image (bash 5.2.37 / GNU coreutils 9.5 on musl) the race is lost in 326 of 5000 runs — 6.5%, always as `PIPESTATUS=0 141 0`. What failed was therefore not a test but the **stamp**: `ci-and-stamp.sh` reported `rc=141 action=no-stamp`, the marker was not written and `gh pr create` was denied, telling the operator local CI had not passed when every check in fact had, and teaching a retry-until-green habit. The min/max scan is now in-shell (`(( 10#${_n} < 10#${_min} ))` over the array that already exists), so the lint has no pipeline whose exit status depends on a reader that stops reading. `sort`/`tail` are gone with it; a numbering gap is still advisory-only. Separately, `_run_lint_tool` now arms an ERR trap (`-E`, since an ERR trap is not inherited by shell functions) that reports the tool, the failing command and the status through the new registered `ci_lint_driver_failed` event, decoding an above-128 status into its signal — so the next unexplained driver abort says `lint tool 'adr-numbering' stopped at ..., status 141 (killed by signal 13/SIGPIPE)` instead of surfacing as a bare number that reads like a lint finding. errexit is deliberately untouched: the driver still stops at its first failing command, since running it under `|| _rc=$?` would disable errexit inside the whole driver and let a real failure sail past. Regression cover is deterministic rather than 6.5%-of-the-time — `adr_numbering_spec` pins the losing interleaving with PATH shims (a `head` that closes without reading, a `sort` that writes late) so a reintroduced pipeline fails on every run, and `ci_spec` covers the abort report for both a signalled and an unsignalled driver failure.
- **the tag `docker compose build test-tools` writes is now the tag `docker compose run ci` reads, because neither has a default to diverge on (closes #896)** — `compose.yaml` read ONE variable with TWO different fallbacks: the build-only `test-tools` service fell back to `test-tools:local`, while the `ci` / `coverage` / `ci-system` consumers fell back to the published `ghcr.io/ycpss91255-docker/test-tools:latest`. Edit `dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools`, `docker compose build test-tools`, `docker compose run ci`, and the suite runs against the OLD published image with the fresh local build sitting unused — nothing warns. It fires exactly when someone drives compose directly, which is what a person debugging the tooling does (it happened during #869). The file's own header asserted the opposite ("the tag comes from the SAME `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE`"). **Giving all four the same default was rejected**: it removes the divergence and keeps the failure mode — a bare compose run would still quietly build or pull something instead of saying the operator left the entry point. So there is **no default at all**: each service is `${TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE:?set by 'just test' / 'just docker build --target test-tools' -- run one of those instead of driving docker compose directly}`, compose's own error form, which refuses the whole invocation and names the recipe. With no default there is nothing to diverge, by construction. This is the same rule ADR-00000003 already applied to the consumer compose ("bare `docker compose up` support is dropped"), now applied to base's own. **Consequence — every `just` path sets it.** `script/test/test.sh`'s `_run_via_compose` (the one function behind every `just test` form: bare, `lint`, `coverage`, `coverage-shard`, `bats-*`, `--bats-path`) resolves it through the existing `_resolve_test_tools_image` and EXPORTS it — compose reads the value while interpolating on the HOST, so `-e` would not have reached it. That moves the local default from the rolling published tag to the content hash of this checkout's tooling Dockerfile, and the published tag is measurably stale (the `:latest` on the development host carries neither kcov nor `docker compose`), which is the same staleness behind the #869 false green. New `_ensure_test_tools_image` therefore builds that tag when the host lacks it, through the SAME compose service the docker namespace builds — a local-only tag that is absent means "not built yet", never "pull it" — and leaves a caller-pinned value alone, because provisioning that is the caller's job. `just docker build --target test-tools` relied on the removed `test-tools:local` fallback; `dist/script/docker/wrapper/build.sh` now resolves it for a **self-managed repo** (no `.base/` subtree, ADR-00000011 sec.4, where there is no pinned base version to scope a local tag by) by asking that repo's own `script/test/test.sh --test-tools-image` rather than deriving a second tag — two derivations that agree by luck is the defect, so there is one derivation and one value. The resolved tag is forwarded on both channels a repo can read it through: the `--build-arg` a consumer Dockerfile's `FROM ${TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE}` consumes, and the exported environment a self-managed `compose.yaml` interpolates, where a `--build-arg` never reaches. Repos WITH a `.base/` subtree keep the version-scoped `.base/.version` derivation untouched. **CI verified, not assumed**: every `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE` occurrence in the tree was enumerated — `self-test.yaml` sets it explicitly in the 6 jobs that reach these services (`hadolint`, `bats-fragile`, `bats-integration`, `coverage`, `acceptance`, `system`), and `build-worker.yaml` / `publish-worker.yaml` / `release-test-tools.yaml` pin version-scoped GHCR tags through the build-arg channel into a DOWNSTREAM repo's Dockerfile, never through this compose file; the `system` job's direct `docker compose run --rm ci-system` pins it too. Nothing CI pins changed. New integration spec `compose_test_tools_image_spec.bats` (3) drives `docker compose config --images` — what compose actually resolves, not what the file's text says — comparing the build side against each consumer and pinning the refusal message; `ci_spec.bats` (84 -> 88), `build_sh_spec.bats` (54 -> 57) and `base_docker_namespace_spec.bats` cover the resolution, the ensure-built rule and the self-managed branch. Closes #896.
- **the three localized READMEs stop documenting `INSTANCE_SUFFIX` / `run.sh --instance` / `config/instances/`, a mechanism deleted three releases ago, and five other stale sections are re-translated (refs #881)** — #600 removed multi-instance support from the code and its CHANGELOG entry recorded "README unaffected (no `--instance` docs)", which was true of `README.md` and false of all three translations; the #843 localized-README pass and the #873 sync guard both went over it, because the guard compares an English section against its translation and cannot see an identifier that exists in the translation and in neither the English nor the code. Setting `INSTANCE_SUFFIX` was a silent no-op and `--instance` a hard `unknown option`, so a non-English reader with the exact problem #891 / #893 just solved -- running one repo twice, e.g. two branches in parallel -- was pointed at a removed mechanism and would reasonably conclude the tool was broken. The text is gone from `naming-scheme-three-namespaces-two-user-identities` in zh-TW / zh-CN / ja, replaced by the current English (base is single-instance, #600) **plus a pointer to what does work**: `[project] name` in `.setup.conf.local` and the [Running two worktrees at once](../../README.md#running-two-worktrees-at-once) section #893 added. That pointer is the one English-side prose change in this entry -- adding it only to the translations would have re-created the same reverse drift this issue is about. The other five: `logging-output-to-host` was missing `container_log_keep` / `container_log_days` entirely and named the shipped helper `/usr/local/lib/base/_entrypoint_logging.sh`, a path that has not existed since it became `logging.sh`; `tldr` and `adding-to-a-new-repo` pinned `v0.30.0` inside a copy-paste `git subtree add` where English uses the `vX.Y.Z` placeholder (zh-CN / ja; zh-TW was already current); `directory-structure` carried hand-counted spec totals ("56 unit specs", "5 integration specs") that English had already generalised away; `field-deployment-just-docker-setup-deploy` was found already current in all three (#870's read-only manifest default had landed since the audit). Two further stale sections turned up on re-running the same fenced-block comparison and are fixed here: the `architecture` mermaid omitted `publish-worker.yaml (opt-in)` in all three, and zh-CN's `adding-extra-stages-215` still documented the `EXEC_ARGS` env-var shim (#469) that retired with the Makefile in #546. `doc/adr/00000001-setup-conf-vs-compose.md` still names `config/instances/` in its Context; an ADR is a dated record, so its reasoning is untouched and it gains a dated amendment note (the #874 / ADR-23 shape) saying the mechanism is historical and pointing at ADR-00000025. Every section was re-translated and then stamped -- `--accept` was not used anywhere, so the guard is green because the translations are current, not because anything was silenced. Nothing was added to the sync-skip ledger.
- **two concurrent self-test runs no longer share one tooling image tag and one compose project by accident (closes #891)** — `compose.yaml` pinned `image: test-tools:local` on the build-only `test-tools` service and `just test system` pinned `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE=test-tools:local` on the consumer side, so every checkout on a host wrote and read one tag; and `script/test/test.sh` invoked `docker compose run` with no `-p`, so compose fell back to the checkout DIRECTORY BASENAME for the project name. Both collisions are silent. A sibling run rebuilding the shared tag displaces the image a live run is already using with no error at all — kcov vanished from the image mid-pass during #869 and the pass still reported green, which means a coverage run can report clean having never run kcov — and two checkouts at `~/a/base` and `~/b/base` reuse each other's containers and network, worktrees staying isolated today only by the accident of being named apart. **Image:** the local default is now `test-tools:<12 hex of sha256 of dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools>` (`test.sh --test-tools-image`). Content, not commit or run id: a per-commit tag would rebuild an image whose inputs did not change, whereas keying on content means two runs on the same inputs SHARE the image (correct, and a cache hit) while any difference produces a tag that cannot clobber — exactly what would have prevented the #869 incident, since the sibling was building different inputs. The tooling Dockerfile is the image's only input: the service passes `context: .` but the Dockerfile never reads it (every COPY is `COPY --from=<stage>`, no ADD), so hashing the context would churn the tag on every unrelated source edit and defeat the cache while changing nothing about the image; tool versions live inside the file, so an upgrade does move the tag. The digest is the whole file rather than the stage-list projection `_compute_dockerfile_hash` takes — that hash answers "did the set of compose services change?" and must ignore RUN lines, and a RUN line is precisely what dropped kcov. The build-only service now reads the SAME `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE` its `ci` / `ci-system` / `coverage` consumers read, so what the build writes and what the consumers resolve cannot diverge (that divergence is silent — the consumers would quietly pull the published image while the local build sat unused), and a missing tooling Dockerfile fails loud instead of falling back to the bare literal. **Project:** `_run_via_compose` passes `-p` explicitly, defaulting to `base-<12 hex of sha256 of the ABSOLUTE repo root path>`, and the `system` recipe exports the same name for its own `docker compose run`. Keyed to the path, not the commit, because two worktrees are routinely branched from the same commit — a commit-keyed name would collide in exactly the concurrent case this separates — and the path is stable across commits, so a checkout keeps one project instead of churning a network per commit. Hashed rather than sanitised because compose accepts only `[a-z0-9][a-z0-9_-]*` while a path may hold spaces, uppercase, punctuation, non-ASCII or a leading dot: a constant prefix plus hex digits satisfies the grammar by construction for any input. **Overrides are untouched:** `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE` and `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` still win verbatim, so the version-scoped GHCR tags pinned by `build-worker.yaml` / `publish-worker.yaml` / `release-test-tools.yaml` and `self-test.yaml`'s explicit `test-tools:local` all flow through unchanged — the derivations are LOCAL defaults only. Fail-loud guards keep the isolation from degrading silently (an unusable digest dies instead of collapsing to the bare `base-` prefix every checkout would share). Unit-tested in `ci_spec.bats` (71 -> 84) and `base_docker_namespace_spec.bats` (5 -> 8), including a hostile checkout path and a cache-reuse case; verified locally that two consecutive builds resolved `test-tools:4f04591c8021` with the second fully CACHED at an unchanged image ID, while appending one line to the tooling Dockerfile moved the tag. Closes #891.
- **one shared matcher decides which line declares a build stage, so `FROM --platform=... AS <stage>` is no longer half-visible (closes #875)** — five readers of a Dockerfile's stage list answered "which line is stage `<S>`'s FROM line" with three different regexes. Four demanded exactly one token between `FROM` and `AS`; `_bake_config_copy` used a greedy `.*`. On the standard cross-build form this repo actively invites (`TARGETARCH`, the arm64 matrix) — `FROM --platform=$BUILDPLATFORM ubuntu:24.04 AS runtime` — the strict four saw nothing and the loose one matched, and every consequence was silent: no compose service (`compose_emit.sh`'s emit loop is fed by `_parse_dockerfile_stages`, and its only warning path fires solely when the user also wrote a `[stage:<name>]` section), no drift detection (the hash projection uses the same shape, so adding or removing such a stage never moved the hash), no per-stage TUI override menu, and — the compounding one — a field bundle carrying the baked `config/app` and **none** of the `[environment]` defaults, because the ENV bake returned 1 while the config splice matched, and `docker build --target runtime` still succeeded so the bundle shipped. `_dockerfile_stage_from_line` (`lib/stage.sh`) is now the single answer, routed through by `_parse_dockerfile_stages`, `_compute_dockerfile_hash`, `_generate_runtime_dockerfile`, `_bake_config_copy` and the TUI's `_list_dockerfile_stages_available` (which stops carrying a private copy and sources `stage.sh`). Its accepted grammar is **stated, not inferred from a regex**: `FROM [--flag[=value]]... <image-ref> AS <stage>`, uppercase `FROM` / `AS`, zero or more leading `--`-prefixed flag tokens, exactly one image reference which may not start with `-`, one stage token, trailing whitespace tolerated, any `#` disqualifying. Widening for flags deliberately did **not** inherit the rest of what the greedy form tolerated: `FROM img junk AS x`, `FROM img # note AS x` and a repeated `AS` are all lines `docker build` itself rejects, and `_bake_config_copy` no longer splices into them. `_generate_runtime_dockerfile` also stops conflating two different non-results — exit **1** stays the silent "nothing to bake" (no Dockerfile, or an empty `[environment]`), while a stage the Dockerfile does not declare is now exit **2** plus a `deploy_stage_absent` WARN naming the stage and the file, so a deploy asking for a stage that does not exist is named instead of quietly producing a bundle missing its env. The regression guard is an **agreement** spec, not five more per-function tests — per-function tests are exactly the shape that let the matchers drift, since each only ever exercised its own regex: `stage_spec.bats` (84 -> 94) drives every call site off one FROM line and asserts a single verdict token per site, across the flagged, multi-flag, plain, lowercase-`as`, commented-out, stray-token and inline-`#` cases; `tui_flow_spec.bats` (105 -> 106) covers the fifth reader.

### Added
- **`just test coverage-path <spec>`: one spec under kcov, in seconds instead of minutes (refs #887)** -- the failure class whose entire evidence is "this spec is red under kcov and green without it" had no inner loop. The only instrumented entries were the whole suite and a whole shard, and `--bats-path` refuses `--coverage` (#523), so every red-green iteration on such a bug cost a full run -- which is why the last four diagnoses of that class (#613, #869, #895, #898) reached past `just` to a hand-rolled `docker run` + kcov, the one step of that work `just` could not do. #523's refusal was checked before being worked around, and it stands: `--bats-path` was built as the deliberately fast, ShellCheck-free, kcov-free loop, and it dispatches to the `ci` service, which has no kcov -- combining the two flags would not narrow a mode, it would ask one flag pair to pick two runners on two services. So this is a third mode rather than a lifted guard. It runs through the same `coverage` service a shard does, so `COVERAGE=1` is set and the kcov-fragile skip guards behave exactly as they do on a shard, and both runners now share one include/exclude set -- a reproduction that instrumented a different tree would reproduce a different thing. **It reports no coverage figure, and that is the design:** the kcov report goes to a throwaway directory inside the container and is deleted, so no path through this mode can write `coverage/cobertura.xml` (which the coverage-gate merges into the project line rate) or `coverage/timings.tsv` (which becomes the next partition's weights); one spec's covered lines over the whole tree's denominator is not a project rate, and the gate must never be handed one. The target is the path you name -- no shard partition is consulted, which is what makes the command mean the same thing locally and on CI: without `test/bats/.shard-weights` (restored from cache in CI, absent in a fresh checkout) the greedy-LPT partition differs, and during #898 a spec sat in CI's shard 1/8 and the local 4/8, so a local `just test coverage 1/8` never ran the failing specs at all. Measured on a 32-core host: `just test coverage-path test/bats/unit/lib_spec.bats` **3s** (54 tests) against `just test coverage 1/8` **154s** (13 files, 358 tests) -- and that shard does not contain `lib_spec.bats`, which locally lands in 2/8. `--filter` composes, so a single `@test` can be put under instrumentation. Conflicts with `--coverage` / `--coverage-shard` / `--bats-path` are refused by name rather than resolved silently.
- **`just test smoke`: run the shipped smoke specs in one command, faithfully (closes #885)** -- the specs under `dist/test/bats/smoke/` had no `just` entry at all. They execute only inside a downstream Dockerfile `-test` stage or base's `acceptance` CI job, and base is the template SOURCE, so it has no `Dockerfile` and no `-test` stage of its own -- the specs it *ships* had nowhere to run. Verifying one therefore meant hand-reproducing the stage with raw `docker build` / `docker run`, twice (once as root, once as the real stage's non-root user); that happened during #877, found a real bug, and left nothing reusable. It also interrupts: raw `docker` sits in the permission `ask` list, so every hand-rolled reproduction is a prompt for a step that should be one command. New `dockerfile/Dockerfile.smoke` + a build-only `smoke` compose service, driven by `just test smoke` **through the `docker` namespace** (ADR-00000011 sec.5) -- the rule `just test system` already follows, not a `docker build -f` one-liner. **Building it IS the test**: the file ends in `RUN bats /smoke_test/`, exactly as the stage does, so a failing spec fails a build rather than being reported by something that could stop reporting. **What it mirrors** is what makes the run meaningful: the `/lint` COPY set (wrappers + the `lib/` chain they source, copied **root-owned**, not `--chown`'d), the flattened `/smoke_test/` from `smoke/shared/` + `smoke/devel-test/`, `/entrypoint.sh`, `BATS_LIB_PATH`, and a **non-root `USER`** before the bats run -- a harness that ran these as root against the source tree would be green in exactly the cases the real stage is red. **What it trades away, stated rather than discovered later**: the base image. A real `devel-test` is `FROM devel`, the consumer's Ubuntu image with its apt packages, and building that means scaffolding a whole consumer repo -- the `acceptance` job's work, not a local loop's -- so the harness is `FROM ${TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE}` and anything OS- or package-specific is out of reach; the per-repo `test/bats/smoke/` overlay is likewise not copied (base ships these specs, it does not consume them), and the stage's lint half is not duplicated because `just test lint` already runs it. **Scope is `devel-test`**, every shipped smoke spec today, with no stage argument: `runtime-test` ships none and its real base is the MINIMAL runtime image, so running the shared baseline against a devel-shaped harness and labelling it `runtime-test` would be a false green -- a unit test fails the day `smoke/runtime-test/` gains a spec, which is when that decision is due. **Deliberately NOT in the default `just test`**, and asserted rather than left to habit (being outside a gate is how these specs drifted into being tautological in the first place): that gate is the fast self-test with no daemon and no image build, and these are a stage's build-time assertions, excluded from the self-test grand total for the same reason. Naming: the recipe seeds `PROJECT_NAME` as well as `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`, because the build goes through the wrapper and the wrapper passes `docker compose -p "${PROJECT_NAME}"` explicitly -- `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` never reaches it and the fallback is the checkout DIRECTORY BASENAME, so two same-named checkouts wrote one `<project>-smoke` tag (`local-base-885-smoke` before, `base-23b22f2a9700-smoke` after); the service names no image of its own for the same reason. Two spec files, because a harness has two ways to be worthless: `test/bats/unit/smoke_harness_spec.bats` (13) walks the shipped `devel-test` stage's COPY lines and fails when the harness stops reproducing one -- with a named exemption list that is itself checked against the stage, so an exemption cannot outlive the line it exempts -- and `test/bats/system/smoke_harness_spec.bats` (3) builds the real Dockerfile against a synthesized context to prove the specs really run (bats reported a plan), really run non-root (fixture specs reading `id -u` and attempting a write into `/lint`), and really gate (a deliberately failing spec fails the build). Those builds pass `--no-cache` for a reason found by the plan assertion: a CACHED layer produces no output, so the positive case reported success having executed nothing. Drive-by: `dockerfile/Dockerfile.smoke` joins `_HADOLINT_DOCKERFILES`, and the two `_run_hadolint` specs were generalised (`ci_spec.bats` 93 -> 94) so the list must name **every** Dockerfile the tree carries rather than the two literals it happened to have.
- **`i18n-orphan`: a lint for the identifier documented ONLY in a translation (closes #902)** -- the localized-README sync guard is a per-section fingerprint, i.e. "did the translation move when the English moved?", so it is structurally blind to an identifier that exists in a translation and NOWHERE else: no English counterpart, nothing to compare, nothing to flag. Two real blocks lived in that blind spot and survived both the localized-README pass and the sync guard itself -- `INSTANCE_SUFFIX` / `run.sh --instance NAME` / `config/instances/<NAME>`, documented in all three translations for months after #600 deleted it from the code (a silent no-op and a hard `unknown option`), and the zh-CN-only `EXEC_ARGS` shim retired with the Makefile in #546. Both were found by hand. Note the inversion: every other guard here -- the sync fingerprints, `derived-figures`, `stale-setup-conf`, `home-literal` -- checks that something PRESENT is correct, and none of them can express "present and should be absent". The new lint tokenizes the code spans of `doc/readme/README.*.md` (fenced blocks AND inline backticks) and flags any identifier-shaped token that `README.md` never names. Three scope decisions, each measured rather than argued. **The haystack is `README.md` alone, never the code tree**: with the code tree added, BOTH known blocks stop being reported, because the code carries negative regression assertions (`template_spec.bats` asserts `INSTANCE_SUFFIX` appears nowhere in the wrapper) and comments explaining an absence (`justfile.docker`: "no `EXEC_ARGS` env shim") that spell the retired identifier verbatim -- and an identifier present in the code but absent from English is a *different* finding, which conflating would muddy. **Code spans, not fences alone**: the fence-only shape #881 used by hand catches `EXEC_ARGS` but walks straight past `INSTANCE_SUFFIX`, whose prose ran in inline backticks. **Two token shapes, `UPPER_SNAKE` and `--long-option`, with paths deliberately excluded**: replayed over fourteen historical revisions of the translation set, the path shape fires on markdown-table debris and on real paths that simply are not spelled in `README.md` at revisions where the other two are silent, while those two produce, across that same history, exactly four distinct tokens -- `INSTANCE_SUFFIX`, `--instance`, `EXEC_ARGS`, `--env-file` -- every one of them a true finding that #881 then deleted. Blocking, not advisory, on that evidence: **zero false positives on the current tree** (345 identifiers checked across three translations) and zero at every sampled point of the translation set's five-month history, with the pre-#881 tree yielding 28 findings and nothing spurious. The rare legitimate case (an identifier that lives in a downstream repo, or a translation deliberately ahead of the English) opts out by bracketing the lines with `<!-- i18n-orphan-lint: allow-begin -- <why> -->` / `<!-- i18n-orphan-lint: allow-end -->`; an unbalanced marker fails the lint, and both a missing reference set and an empty scan die rather than pass vacuously. Runs in the `just test` lint phase and as its own `lint-static (i18n-orphan)` CI check.
- **a gitignored `.setup.conf.local` layer and a real `[project] name`, so two worktrees of one repo can run at once (closes #893; ADR-00000025)** — two checkouts pinned the identical compose project name and there was no way to change it. Verified rather than assumed: `_compute_project_name` assigned unconditionally (no `${PROJECT_NAME:-...}`), so exporting it was overwritten; `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME` lost because `_compose_project` passes `-p` explicitly and an explicit `-p` beats the environment; and `.env.local` was never read on that path. The only lever left was `IMAGE_NAME` / `DOCKER_HUB_USER`, which also moves the **image tag** — coupling two things that should be independent. **The conf chain is now three files**, lowest precedence first: the shipped `<template>/.setup.conf`, the repo's committed `<repo>/.setup.conf`, and the operator's gitignored `<repo>/.setup.conf.local`, resolved **section-replace at every step** by one `_setup_conf_layers` list every reader goes through (the per-section loader, the parse-once handle, the show/list view, the conf hash, per-stage overrides, logging collection). Section-replace rather than per-key is structural, not aesthetic: eight of the fifteen sections are `<prefix>_N` ordered lists, and a per-key merge assembles one ordered list out of two layers, offers no way to REMOVE an item, and needs the highest `N` of a layer the author cannot see; related keys also gate each other (`[network] mode` decides whether `port_N` is emitted at all). This is **not** a relapse into what #201 removed — that was a COMMITTED `setup.conf.local` inside a three-file chain whose third file was a derived snapshot nobody read, and its complaint was that `.local` *looked* gitignored while it was not; the convention adopted since makes `.local` mean exactly that, and the file is genuinely ignored now. It is also **not** multi_run's channel: ADR-00000022 isolates the Nth instance with a runtime `.env` overlay on ONE generated `compose.yaml`, while this layer acts BEFORE `compose.yaml` is generated and yields one per worktree (ADR-00000022 amended in-file with that division of labour). **`[project] name`** is a real section of the shipped template, shipping EMPTY (= "derive as before"), so upgrading changes nothing for any existing repo; it lives in `.setup.conf` as well as `.local` because `.local` is the local VARIANT of `.setup.conf` and must share its grammar, not be a second schema. **`-p` and the emitted `name:` are now one value, not two computations that agree**: `_resolve_project_name` (`lib/compose.sh`) is the only producer in `dist/` (`template_spec` fails on a second one), `apply` calls it once and records `PROJECT_NAME` in `.env.generated`, and both the wrapper's `-p` and `compose.yaml`'s `name: ${PROJECT_NAME}` read that — the form stays an interpolation so ADR-00000022's overlay contract and its guard are untouched. `setup set` / `add` / `remove` keep writing the committed file and take **`--local`** for the other; writing a section `.setup.conf.local` already defines **warns**, naming the section and pointing at `--local`, stated as a certainty (under section-replace the write cannot take effect here) but not refused, because the value is still the committed one CI and every other checkout use. `setup deploy` **refuses** while the local layer exists (PRD invariant 8: a field artifact must not silently depend on a config layer outside version control), before the preview and before any build step so `--dry-run` reports it too; `--allow-local-override` builds anyway and the bundle GENERATOR records the untracked sections in the bundle's own README, so the record reaches the person in the field rather than the build host's scrollback. Every wrapper's pre-run summary gains a `local override:` row naming the file and the sections it replaces. New PRD **invariant 9** (identity and naming are resolved once, from a file) covers this and #891/#892's image identity.
- **convention: bake self-built artifacts at `/opt`, not under `$HOME` -- stated where authors meet it, and gated (closes #799; ADR-00000024)** -- the container user is baked at BUILD time (`sys` takes `USER_NAME` / `USER_UID` / `USER_GID`, `devel` then sets `ENV HOME="/home/${USER_NAME}"`), so `$HOME` is frozen to whichever user the build received -- `just build` injects the local host's user, CI and release bake `user` (UID 1000). Anything an image bakes **under `$HOME`** is therefore coupled to the build-time username, and it only bites at deploy time: run a prebuilt / GHCR / `docker save`+`load` image under a different `USER_NAME`, or rebuild with one, and every home-relative path resolves to a different, EMPTY home tree -- the baked workspace is invisible and `source ~/some_ws/install/setup.bash` fails. An absolute `/opt/...` path is immune because there is no `$HOME` indirection left to resolve. The convention: (1) self-built artifacts (colcon workspaces, SDKs, compiled tools) install at absolute `/opt/<name>`, `$HOME` reserved for dotfiles and convenience symlinks; (2) the entrypoint / bashrc sources the **absolute** path, never `~` / `$HOME` -- a `~/<name> -> /opt/<name>` symlink in the per-user `RUN` block is encouraged for interactive discoverability but must never be what anything *sources*; (3) no concrete username in a path, use `${HOME}` / `${USER_NAME}`. It is stated in three places by audience: the shipped `dist/dockerfile/Dockerfile` commentary (what `init.sh` seeds as every repo's own `Dockerfile` -- the file an author edits to bake a workspace), a README section for readers who never open it, and ADR-00000024 for the why. **Rule 3 is gated, not just documented** -- a documented-but-ungated rule is exactly what #845 / #846 / #864 / #866 were about: new `script/test/drivers/home_literal.sh` (`just test lint --home-literal`, `test.sh --home-literal-only`) fails on a home path whose first segment is a concrete username, anywhere under `dist/` or `dockerfile/` -- the trees that reach an image. Parameterised `${USER_NAME}`, its escaped `\${USER_NAME}` variant (code that EMITS the parameterised form into `setup.conf`), an angle-bracket `<placeholder>` in prose and anything under `/opt` all pass; docs and specs are out of scope, since they legitimately spell concrete paths as fixtures and failure-mode narratives. The opt-out is region-delimited, not per-file (`# home-literal-lint: allow-begin -- <why>` / `# home-literal-lint: allow-end`), with unbalanced markers and a missing scan root both failing loudly. It joins `_LINT_TOOLS` **and** the `lint-static` CI matrix in the same change -- the completeness guard in `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` is what forces that pairing. Rules 1-2 are a judgement call no grep can make, and the lint does not pretend otherwise. **Audit of base's shipped tree: zero live violations** -- every home path in `dist/` already uses `${USER_NAME}`; the single literal is the migration NOTE in `lib/dockerfile_migrate.sh` that must name the wrong home directory to explain the mismatch it heals, now bracketed by an allow region. Downstream repos that bake a workspace (`realsense_ros2#97` first) adopt it in their own `Dockerfile` / `script/entrypoint.sh` and inherit the lint on the next `.base` upgrade; the lint does not reach a consumer's own root today. New `home_literal_lint_spec.bats` (18); `ci_spec.bats` (69 -> 70), `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` and `template_spec.bats` (153 -> 154) pin the wiring and the Dockerfile commentary. Drive-by: `sync-readme-hashes.sh` and `resolve-doc-counts.sh` were committed non-executable, so `just test sync-readme` and `just test resolve-docs` both died with exit 126; mode fixed.
- **the doc/test catalogue is generated, not hand-written: per-test rows + missing sections (closes #859)** — `doc/test/*.md` carried two kinds of content per spec file, a `### <path> (N)` heading whose count was generated and gated, and a `| Test | Description |` table of per-test rows that was neither. So the rows rotted silently under a green gate: `deploy_spec.bats` shipped **36 rows for 43 tests** while both gates reported "in sync", and `smoke/devel-test/script_help.bats` was nine rows short. Backfilling only resets the clock — the asymmetry (generated count next to hand-written rows) is the defect, so the rows are **generated too**: `sync-doc-counts.sh` emits one row per `@test`, in spec file order, and `check_test_md_drift.sh` (same generator) therefore fails on a short table. The catalogue is complete by construction; a human enriches a description, nobody has to remember to add one. **Descriptions are preserved**, keyed on the test name exactly as bats reports it (bash double-quote unescaping applied, `|` escaped into the table), so hand-written prose survives regeneration; a deleted test's row goes, and a rename is delete-plus-add (indistinguishable from it) so prose does not follow one — rename the row in the same commit to carry it. Sections are generated the same way for the same reason: four spec files (`check_test_md_drift_spec`, `base_version_monitor_spec`, `compose_emit/hostname_spec`, `logrotate_spec`) had no section at all, which the row generator could not have helped, so a missing section is now appended and filled. A section may still summarise with a `| Category | Tests |` table or prose — an explicit editorial choice for that section, stated at the top of `unit.md` along with the whole contract, not a licence for a per-test table to be partial. On the real tree the catalogue went from **988 rows to 1299**: 81 rows naming tests that no longer exist were dropped, and every remaining test now has one -- including the seven missing from `deploy_spec.bats` and the nine missing from `smoke/devel-test/script_help.bats`.
- **`script/test/resolve-doc-counts.sh` / `just test resolve-docs`: doc-count merge conflicts in one command (closes #857)** — every merge of `main` into a queued branch conflicts on `doc/test/TEST.md` and `doc/test/unit.md` because both sides bumped the same generated counters; the identical resolution was performed by hand six times in one review batch and pasted verbatim into every dispatched agent prompt, awk one-liner included. The script collapses the markers (two-way and diff3), regenerates from the MERGED spec tree, verifies, and stages. It does **not** simply adopt a side: a mechanical collapse takes whichever side it kept for content the generator does not derive, which is how TEST.md's hand-maintained "System (N) and smoke (N)" prose rode through stale three times before #843, and which the per-test rows reopened from the other end (row names are generated, descriptions are not). So both collapses are regenerated and reconciled — a description present on one side and placeholder on the other is adopted, two different descriptions for the same test are refused by name, and any remaining difference between the two regenerated sides is refused with the diff. That last check carries no list of "figures that are generated" to fall out of date: it refuses to adopt anything regeneration cannot justify. Refuses a relative root (the spec trees are symlinked into a temp dir, so a relative root silently zeroes every count), a surviving marker, and an unhappy drift gate; nothing is staged on a refusal.
- **localized-README drift guard: per-section fingerprints, maintained by a script (closes #846)** — an English README edit could silently leave `doc/readme/README.{zh-TW,zh-CN,ja}.md` behind and nothing detected it; it had already happened at the scale of an entire section (the field-deployment section was rewritten in place while the three translations kept describing the superseded deploy model, caught only by a manual review), plus two smaller instances in the same review. A headings-only structural check would NOT have caught it (the heading never changed) and hand-maintained fingerprints are worse than none (a translator who forgets to retype the hash leaves the system *claiming* it is in sync), so the mechanism is a **script-maintained per-section fingerprint**: each translated section carries an invisible `<!-- sync: <section-id> <hash> -->` HTML comment above its heading recording the hash of the English section it was translated against. The English author changes nothing; the translator never types a hash — they write the id once and run `just test sync-readme`, exactly the model `sync-doc-counts.sh` already established for derived doc figures. **Section identity** is the GitHub anchor slug of the ENGLISH heading (`field-deployment-just-docker-setup-deploy`), because the heading text itself does not survive translation and the slug is already the published name README.md's own table of contents links to; renaming an English heading therefore surfaces as UNKNOWN + MISSING rather than passing silently. **The hash input is defined, not implied** (an under-specified hash input is how this rots): the heading line plus the body up to the next ATX heading of ANY level, each line right-trimmed, leading/trailing blank body lines dropped, joined with LF and LF-terminated, SHA-256, first 12 hex characters — so a whitespace sweep or a blank-line change does not mark three files stale, a nested subsection is its own section (drift is reported at the sub-heading that actually changed), and fenced code blocks never invent headings. **Untranslated sections get a deliberate answer, not a crash**: the three translations are abridged by design, so "no marker" cannot mean "fine" — a section that deliberately has no translation is DECLARED once with `<!-- sync-skip: <id> -- <why> -->` (the region-marker opt-out idiom of the #845 stale-path lint), and the generator never invents that declaration, because auto-declaring an unclaimed section would silence exactly the drift the guard exists to catch; it reports the unclaimed ids and leaves the decision to a human. New `script/test/sync-readme-hashes.sh` (the generator, `just test sync-readme`) and `script/test/drivers/readme_sync.sh` (`_run_readme_sync`, its read-only twin) report **by FILE AND SECTION** — STALE / UNSTAMPED / MISSING / UNKNOWN / DUPLICATE / MISPLACED / AMBIGUOUS — never "this file is out of date"; missing roots or a zero-file translation glob fail loudly instead of passing vacuously. Wired into the gate beside the issue-ref / ADR-numbering / stale-setup-conf lints (the full `just test` lint phase, `just test lint`, `just test lint --readme-sync`, `just test sync-readme-check`), so CI gates on it rather than it firing only on demand. Initial stamping: **105 markers** (35 shared sections x 3 files) plus a 9-entry untranslated ledger per file — the first written inventory of what the localized READMEs do not carry (getting-help, wrapper UX cheat sheet, network mode, container init, watchdog, env-vs-workload routing, wrapper transcripts, host-detection overrides, publish-worker inputs). No translated prose was touched. `readme_sync_spec.bats` (23) covers the motivating failure (English section rewritten in place, translation untouched → reported, naming the section), the structural case, the skip declaration, marker hygiene, hash-input normalisation, generator idempotency and the fail-loud guards, plus a real-tree pair; `ci_spec.bats` (61 -> 62) pins the CI wiring. Accepted costs, stated rather than discovered later: a trivial English edit marks all three translations stale (one command to clear), and a re-stamp can silence a genuine change — unavoidable for any guard, but the re-stamp moves the hashes visibly in the diff, so "this PR moved the zh-TW hashes but did not touch the translation" is apparent in review.
- **self-contained resolved-compose field-deploy bundle + per-component tunable-config manifest (closes #832, closes #833; ADR-00000023)** — the deploy MODEL shifts from the #497 raw `docker run` `tar.xz{image, deploy.sh}` to a complete, self-contained output FOLDER run via a fully-resolved `docker compose`. `just setup deploy <stage>` now writes `deploy/<repo>-<stage>-<version>/` (gitignored; `deploy/` added to the canonical `.gitignore`/`.dockerignore` sync) containing: `image.tar.xz` (the image, tagged `<repo>:<stage>-<version>` so field iterations never collide; **version = `git describe --tags --always --dirty`** via new `_resolve_deploy_version`), a **fully-resolved self-contained `compose.yaml`** (new `_generate_resolved_compose` — no `${VAR}` interpolation, no `setup.conf`/`.env.generated` dependency, no `build:` section, the dev-host workspace bind stripped, `restart: unless-stopped` for auto-start on reboot, and the deployed stage's per-stage-resolved params via the existing `_resolve_deploy_context` + `_resolve_docker_flags` resolvers so the field never drifts from dev; it FOLLOWS THE STAGE rather than blanket-stripping GUI/X11), a **thin arg-driven `deploy.sh`** (new `_generate_deploy_launcher` — `up` docker-loads the image then `docker compose up -d`, `down`/`logs`; no inlined flags), an editable `config/`, and a generic `README` (new `dist/deploy/README` template + a `dist/deploy/cd-guard.sh` CD guard that enforces clean+tagged before an automated deploy — the deploy tool itself labels honestly and never blocks). The **tunable-config manifest** (#833, `_parse_deploy_manifest` / `_collect_deploy_binds`) is a committed, downstream-owned `config/<component>/deploy.manifest` with per-stage `[<stage>]` sections listing the container-internal paths an operator may override without a rebuild: base **delivers files, it does not parse content** — for each declared path it copies the image's baked default into the bundle `config/<basename>` and emits a `./config/<basename>:<container-path>` bind (mount-wins over the baked default, ADR-00000023 sec.2). Unlisted paths stay baked-only (fail-safe); a missing manifest is nothing-tunable (not an error); a malformed manifest, or a duplicate basename across components, fails loud. `_generate_deploy_bundle` is rewritten to orchestrate the folder (build `--target` → `save` | `xz` → per-manifest `docker create`/`cp`/`rm` extract → resolved compose + launcher + README → install), with the docker/xz/cp steps behind `_dry_run_cmd` (mocked; no real daemon). The obsolete raw-run generator (`_emit_docker_run_flags` + the old `_generate_deploy_sh`) is removed. New `deploy_manifest_spec.bats` (11) + a rewritten `deploy_spec.bats` (29) cover manifest parsing (per-stage / unlisted=baked / malformed-fails-loud / basename-collision), version resolution, the resolved-compose content (no `${VAR}`, workspace bind stripped, restart present, tunable paths bound, per-stage params, follow-stage GUI), the `deploy.sh` up/down/logs shape (ShellCheck-clean), and the bundle plan. The dev-side compose emission (`generate_compose_yaml`) is untouched (byte-identical). Per the ISTQB test pyramid (ADR-00000018) the mechanism also gains **Integration** coverage (`deploy_bundle_flow_spec.bats`, 5 -- the real generator driven end-to-end over the filesystem with a docker+xz PATH-shim, asserting the output folder / self-contained compose / tunable delivery) and **System** coverage (`deploy_bundle_e2e_spec.bats`, 2 -- a REAL deploy: `docker build` + `save | xz` -> `docker load` -> generated `deploy.sh up` (`docker compose up -d`) -> assert the container is Up -> edit the bundle `config/` and re-up to prove the mount-wins override -> `deploy.sh down`; auto-skips when docker.sock / the `docker compose` plugin / `xz` is absent). The test-tools image gains `docker-cli-compose` + `xz` (with matching release smoke checks) so the System deploy runs for real on the `ci-system` service. Two follow-ups from running the System e2e for real in CI: (1) **generate-time fail-loud** -- `_generate_deploy_bundle` now verifies each manifest tunable path resolves to a real baked FILE in the built image (the field bind mounts an editable copy *over* that baked default; a missing target otherwise surfaces as a cryptic runc "mount a directory onto a file" error at `deploy.sh up`), failing with a clear, actionable message at build time instead (never-fail-silently); (2) the `ci-system` compose service gains a same-path host mount (`/tmp/base-deploy-e2e`) so the generated `deploy.sh`'s `docker compose` bind sources -- resolved by the host daemon, not inside the socket-mounted test container -- point at a path visible identically on host and container. Implements the runtime-override half of base#826.
- **PRD invariant #8 (dev/field config split) + field-deploy ADR + ADR-3 amendment (closes #830)** — base had no stated product invariant for how config is provisioned across the development vs the deployable/field environment, and ADR-00000003's parameter-routing table was asymmetric: its env-var row had a field override channel (baked `ENV` default + optional `deploy.sh -e`) but its structured-config row was **bake-only** (a config file `COPY`-baked into the image, no field override → a deploy could not adjust config without a rebuild), and the "which stages deploy / how dev and field provision the same config" rule was unwritten. This is the doc-only anchor of the config-provisioning/field-deploy epic (the mechanism lands in the sibling issues #831/#832/#833). Three artifacts: (1) **new PRD Core Invariant #8** "Development and field are cleanly separated, and provisioned by opposite means" — dev bind-mounts config (live edit); a deployable stage bakes a self-contained default + an optional mount-to-override hook (adjust without rebuild); the developer-vs-user split follows **git-tracking** (committed = baked developer default, gitignored / not-in-repo = operator overlay); and `devel` / `*-test` stages are **never** deploy targets. (2) **ADR-00000003 amended** — its structured-config Field cell becomes "`COPY`-baked default + optional field `-v` override (mount-wins)", symmetric with the env row's `-e`, plus a dated `Amended:` note and a Consequences bullet naming the git-tracked provisioning axis and refining "compose does not travel" → "a fully-resolved, self-contained compose travels". (3) **new ADR-00000023** "Config field-override + self-contained field-deploy contract" records the git-tracked axis, baked-default + mount-wins override (the file analog of ADR-3's env `-e`), deploy-as-resolved-self-contained-compose (no `setup.conf` / `.env.generated` dependency), `deployable = not devel and not *-test`, the per-component `config/<component>/deploy.manifest` tunability channel (base delivers files, the repo entrypoint consumes them), and an explicit reconciliation with ADR-00000022 (a single-file config `-v` on the field launcher is distinct from general writable-volume topology, which stays compose-merge). `doc/adr/README.md` audit table + back-refs updated (new ADR row → elevates-invariant 8; ADR-3 row noted as amended; invariant list 1-7 → 1-8). Governs the downstream conventions #826 / #827.
- **ADR index + PRD audit: `doc/adr/README.md`, and a `> Serves:` invariant back-reference on every ADR (closes #808)** — the final #808 slice, after the PRD (#824) and the ADR-numbering guard (#823). base had 22 ADRs (an intentional `00000009` gap) with no consolidated index and no explicit link from a decision to the product invariant it upholds. This adds `doc/adr/README.md` — an index + audit table with one row per ADR carrying a verdict (keep / amend / supersede / merge / elevates-invariant), the PRD invariant (1-7) / goal / "mechanism, no invariant" it serves, and a one-line note — plus a one-line `> Serves:` back-reference near the top of each of the 21 ADR files. The intro records that the filesystem is the ADR registry (guarded by the #823 numbering lint; this README is not an ADR file so it does not perturb the lint), that `00000009` is an intentional gap (no back-fill), and that `ADR-00000020` is a single canonical record (the parallel-authored init-toggle content was folded in). Audit result: 14 keep, 1 supersede (00000004 -> 00000012, already recorded), 6 elevates-invariant (00000010/00000011 -> inv 6, 00000018 -> inv 7, 00000019 -> inv 4, 00000020 -> inv 1, 00000022 -> inv 3), 0 amend/merge in the verdict column. One recommended follow-up: add a forward-pointer on ADR-00000010 whose "docker top-level" decision was superseded-in-part by ADR-00000011 sec.1. No ADR technical content was edited (supersede/amend/merge actions are left as maintainer follow-ups).
- **product-intent PRD as base's north star (`doc/PRD.md`) (refs #808)** — base had 22 ADRs but no product-level document tying them together: core principles were scattered across ADRs + CONTEXT.md, so a new decision had no single reference and no holistic view of base's intent existed. `doc/PRD.md` is a standalone north star that holds only **invariants** -- properties that must always be true of base -- and changes only when base's product goals change, distinct from `doc/adr/` (individual append-only decisions), `CONTEXT.md` (domain facts), and the working contract. It records base's Purpose and Scope (in / deliberately-out) and seven Core Invariants, each stated as *invariant + why it is fixed + the ADRs that established/serve it*: (1) one container = one service, base owns the single-service lifecycle; (2) never fail silently; (3) multi_run-expandable by construction; (4) fail-safe defaults; (5) the two-branch default rule; (6) base is a subtree, downstream is a thin caller; (7) base holds a rigorous, industry-aligned test bar (the commitment is the invariant; the taxonomy/coverage mechanisms stay swappable). Plus a Product Shape and Roadmap section. `CONTEXT.md` cross-links it. The ADR audit (per-ADR verdict + back-reference to the invariant each serves) is the remaining #808 slice; the ADR-numbering guard already landed.
- **ADR-numbering lint: doc/adr/ stays duplicate-free + well-formed, gaps warned not failed (refs #808)** — the ADR registry is the filesystem (`doc/adr/NNNNNNNN-<slug>.md`), and nothing gated its numbering: two PRs once authored the same `ADR-00000020` in parallel, caught only by manual inspection. A new per-tool lint driver `script/test/drivers/adr_numbering.sh` (`_run_adr_numbering`, sourced by `test.sh` exactly like the issue-ref driver) reads `doc/adr/*.md` and **FAILS non-zero** on a duplicate ADR number (naming both files) or a malformed / non-8-digit filename (not matching `^[0-9]{8}-.+\.md$`), while a numbering **gap** is a warned advisory line only (exit 0) — the live tree has an intentional `00000009` gap that must not block CI. It is wired into the default `just test` lint phase and narrowable via `just test lint --adr-numbering` (the `--adr-numbering` flag on `test.sh --lint`), so CI gates on it the same way it gates the issue-ref lint. `adr_numbering_spec.bats` (7) drives the driver at its CLI over throwaway fixture `doc/adr/` trees (duplicate → fail naming the dup; malformed / short → fail; clean-set-with-a-gap → pass with the gap warned; clean contiguous → pass with no gap line) plus a real-tree guard that the live `doc/adr/` passes today with `00000009` warned-not-failed, so a future duplicate/malformed ADR fails base's own CI. `TEST.md` synced. (A PreToolUse `.claude/hooks` variant that catches a duplicate at authoring time is a possible follow-up, deliberately out of this slice.)
- **stale `config/docker/setup.conf` path lint: the relocation cannot silently regress (closes #845)** — #831 moved the per-repo override and the template default from `config/docker/setup.conf` to the repo-root `.setup.conf` dotfile, re-pointing the path constants in `lib/setup_conf.sh`, `lib/resolve.sh` and the wrapper / init / upgrade paths that ADR-00000006 requires to move in lockstep, but nothing guarded the old path from creeping back: a newly added lib that hardcodes it reads a location that no longer exists, so the repo's config knobs are silently ignored with no error and no failing test. New `script/test/drivers/stale_setup_conf.sh` (`_run_stale_setup_conf`) scans `dist/**/*.sh` — the shipped runtime tree, the only place where a stale read is a live defect — and fails with the replacement path named. Docs and CHANGELOG legitimately discuss the old path and are deliberately out of scope. The opt-out is **region-delimited, not per-file** (`# stale-path-lint: allow-begin -- <why>` / `# stale-path-lint: allow-end`), so a NEW stale reference elsewhere in an allowlisted file is still caught; the one live region is the legacy-migration block in `dist/script/base/upgrade.sh`, which must name the old path in order to relocate a downstream still carrying it. Unbalanced markers (an unterminated begin, an unmatched end) fail the lint too — a silently swallowed region would re-open exactly the hole the guard closes, and so does a missing scan root (an empty `find` root would otherwise report "clean" and silently disable the lint if `dist/` ever moves). Wired into the `--lint` dispatcher beside the issue-ref / ADR-numbering lints (`just test lint`, `just test lint --stale-setup-conf`, and the full `just test` lint phase), so it gates CI rather than only firing as an interactive Edit-time hook.
- **base compose is multi_run-expandable by construction, enforced by a guard (closes #716, ADR-00000022)** — `multi_run` (issue #25) runs the same base-generated stack in N co-located instances, but `docker compose` has no native instance axis, so any per-instance-varying field base baked into `compose.yaml` as a hardcoded literal was a wall: two instances collide on host ports / writable paths / DDS domain and multi_run cannot isolate them without a retroactive base change. This lands the forward invariant **base's compose emission never bakes a hardcoded per-instance literal** plus the machine enforcement of it. New `test/bats/unit/compose_emit/overlay_guard_spec.bats` (6) emits a compose exercising the per-instance field set and asserts each is an overlay-overridable `${VAR:-default}` / `${VAR}` interpolation, never a baked literal; its predicate self-check proves the guard *discriminates* a baked literal from an interpolation, so a future change that hardcodes a per-instance field fails base's own CI immediately (same self-validation spirit as the #800 worker preflight). ADR-00000022 records the base<->multi_run contract: per-instance isolation = a `.env` overlay (never a compose regenerate); the environment-default / per-instance-overridable resolution of the ADR-00000003 axis-A grey zone for `ports` / `network_mode`; the override channel per field kind (interpolation for `name` / `container_name` / `network_mode` / `ports`; `.env` env_file for workload env; compose-merge for volume topology; and `runtime` / `hostname` / GPU classified as host-bound-shared, NOT per-instance); and the held contract (`container_name` removable, stage identity via `build.target` not the literal `devel`).
- **`upgrade.sh` self-run guard — refuse to run inside the base template source (closes #721)** — `upgrade.sh` has the same walk-up self-location as `init.sh` (resolve the subtree root carrying `.version` + `dist/`, then use its parent as the repo root and its basename directly as the `git subtree pull --prefix=`). Run raw from inside the base checkout/worktree itself, that walk-up resolves to base's own root and the pull would target base's PARENT dir. `upgrade.sh` now sources `lib/template_guard.sh` and calls the shared `_assert_not_template_source "${SUBTREE_ROOT}" upgrade` (the same guard `init.sh` uses, added for #719) after `--help` and before any mutation: it refuses with a clear message when the subtree root carries `.git`, and is a no-op inside a real vendored `.base/` subtree. The raw compose wrappers (`build`/`run`/`exec`/`stop`/`prune`/`setup`) need no such guard — they route through compose and degrade gracefully when `.env.generated` / `setup.conf` are absent.
- **`derived-figures` lint: a figure a document repeats must match the code that defines it (refs #874)** — two figures had drifted, and each lived in more than one place, which is exactly what makes a hand fix the wrong answer: the next edit re-opens the gap in whichever copy the editor did not have open. The baseline stage blocklist was written as a five-element set including `devel-test` in README.md, in two of `stage.sh`'s own docstrings, in all three localized READMEs and in all four `setup_tui.sh` message tables (a surface a user reads at the TUI menu, not a comment) -- while `_validate_stage_name` blocklists `{sys, devel-base, devel, runtime-test}` plus the legacy `{base, test}` and deliberately lets `devel-test` through as the `test` service; `compose_emit.sh` carried a fourth spelling, `{sys, base, devel, test}`, wrong in a third way. README.md's setup.conf overview announced seven sections and listed eight while `SCHEMA_SECTIONS` declared fourteen. The new `script/test/drivers/derived_figures.sh` derives both figures from the code -- the baseline renderings out of `_validate_stage_name`'s own `return 2` case arms (read back from `declare -f`, so comment / indentation / wrapping changes cannot break the read), with every extracted name probed back THROUGH the predicate so a misread extraction fails loudly instead of pinning prose to an invented set; the section list and its count straight out of `SCHEMA_SECTIONS`, order included. It scans README.md, CONTEXT.md, the localized READMEs and `dist/**/*.sh`, flattening each file first so a set wrapped across markdown lines, across a `#` comment continuation, or across an escaped `\n` inside a `$'...'` string is still caught (all three shapes were live); brace EXPANSIONS glued to a path (`test/bats/smoke/{shared,devel-test,runtime-test}/`) and `${VAR}` expansions are not sets and are ignored. `doc/adr/` and `doc/changelog/` are deliberately out of scope -- they are dated records, not statements about today's code. Wired into `just test lint --derived-figures`, `./script/test/test.sh --derived-figures-only` and the `lint-static` CI matrix.

### Changed
- **`.setup.conf.local` is canonical in `.gitignore` / `.dockerignore` again, and `SETUP_CONF` is gone (refs #893)** — #879 had moved the leftover `.gitignore` line to the RETIRED list, which does not merely stop emitting it but actively PRUNES it from every downstream `.gitignore` on each sync. Correct while nothing read the file; exactly wrong now that every config resolution does. Both halves moved in one edit, because an entry in BOTH lists is retracted and re-appended on every sync forever, in every downstream at once — a standing spec now asserts the two lists are disjoint, and the retraction mechanism keeps its own tests against a stubbed list. The retirement was unreleased (it sat in the commits after `v0.42.0-rc3`), so no downstream ever saw the line disappear. `.dockerignore` shares the canonical list and gains the entry too: untracked machine-local config inside a build context is how an unversioned value ends up baked into an image. Separately, `SETUP_CONF` — an undocumented env var that replaced the WHOLE config resolution with one path — is **removed**. It was introduced as a test seam (its own first comment said so) and spread by five mechanical refactors that each copied it verbatim, including its inconsistent existence-checking: the two `setup_conf.sh` readers did not check the file existed, so a typo'd path silently yielded an EMPTY config, while `conf_logging.sh` and `stage.sh` did and degraded differently; it was also folded into the drift hash by concatenation even though resolution used it alone, so `SETUP_CONF_HASH` did not describe the config that was resolved. Nothing but one spec set it and ~120 spec bodies `unset` it; that spec now drives real files at the paths the resolver reads, and the 101 dead guards are gone.
- **BREAKING (manifest grammar): field-deploy tunable config is mounted read-only by default, `rw` is declared per path (closes #870)** — `_generate_resolved_compose` bound every `config/<component>/deploy.manifest` path read-write, which the manifest's own semantics never asked for: the operator edits the copy in the bundle on the HOST and the container reads it. Leaving the mount writable made "the container must not write here" prose-only, and quietly coupled it to UID alignment — the field compose sets no `user:`, so the container runs as the UID baked at build time while the bundle's files belong to whoever unpacked them on the field host. Reads work regardless (world-readable), writes fail, on the field host, at whatever moment the code first attempts one. Manifest binds are now emitted `./config/<basename>:<container-path>:ro`, and a component opts a single path into container writes with an explicit flag (`/var/lib/app/calibration.yaml rw`), which makes the exception reviewable data instead of a blanket permission and shrinks the UID question from every mount to the declared few. The flag is parsed with the same fail-loud discipline as the rest of the grammar: an unrecognised flag or a second trailing token is a malformed manifest, reported with file and line, never a silent skip and never a silent downgrade to read-only. The mode is threaded end to end (`_parse_deploy_manifest` -> `_collect_deploy_binds` -> `_generate_resolved_compose`) and an absent mode record can only tighten a mount, never loosen one; the pre-build `deploy` preview passes it too, so what an operator reviews is what gets generated. **No downstream repo ships a manifest yet** (the channel landed in #836/#833), so this settles the default before it has to be migrated. Proven at the System level rather than by asserting the flag string: `deploy_bundle_e2e_spec` now declares one default-access and one `rw` tunable in its live bundle and asserts a real `docker exec` write to the default-access mount FAILS with a read-only filesystem error leaving the host copy untouched, while the `rw` write succeeds and lands in the bundle's `config/`. English + localized READMEs and the generated bundle README say which paths are writable and why the default is not.
- **coverage floor re-based from 50 to 80 now that the rate is measured correctly (closes #855)** — `COVERAGE_MIN` was deliberately left at 50 while #853 landed, because the number it guarded did not mean what it said: the gate keyed its per-line union on the raw filename kcov emits, and kcov reports one source file under several prefix-truncated aliases, so every alias re-counted that file's lines into the denominator. With the key canonicalised, the SAME suite reads **84.72%** (`5907/6972` lines) instead of ~51% — the project rate had been understated by roughly 33 points, and a 50 floor against an 85% reality left about 35 points of dead slack in what is a required PR check, so a large regression could have landed without tripping it.
