The floor is now **80**, leaving **4.72 points** of margin below the measured rate: wide enough that ordinary per-PR churn does not false-fail the gate, narrow enough that a real regression trips it. The basis (measured rate + margin, and why the old number was what it was) is recorded in the `coverage_gate.sh` threshold docstring rather than left as a bare constant. `coverage_gate_spec.bats` (19 -> 21) now PINS the value instead of implying it: the built-in default is asserted with NO `COVERAGE_MIN` override from both sides — a report at exactly 80.00% passes and one at 79.99% fails, each reading the floor back out of the verdict line — and the pre-existing "default does not false-fail" case is re-anchored from the stale ~52.9% figure to the real 84.72%. This re-bases the number only; the v1 absolute-floor posture is unchanged and the v2 regression-vs-main-baseline gate remains the documented follow-up. ADR-00000008 amended. Refs #853, #710, #615.
- **docs: README field-deployment section rewritten for the self-contained resolved-compose bundle (refs #832 #833, #836)** — the "Field deployment" section, the env-vs-workload parameter-routing table's `Field` column, and the `deploy` command-reference row described the removed #497 model (a single `tar.xz` of `{image, deploy.sh}` launched by a raw `docker run` `deploy.sh`). They now document the current model: `just docker setup deploy [--stage S]` (or `./setup.sh deploy`) writes the gitignored `deploy/<repo>-<stage>-<version>/` FOLDER (`image.tar.xz` + fully-resolved self-contained `compose.yaml` + editable `config/` + `up`/`down`/`logs` `deploy.sh` + `README`), only field-oriented stages deploy (not `devel` / `*-test`), version = `git describe --tags --always --dirty`, image ref `<repo>:<stage>-<version>`, field flow `./deploy.sh up|down|logs` with auto-start via `restart: unless-stopped`, the `config/<component>/deploy.manifest` mount-wins operator-tunable override, and the `dist/deploy/cd-guard.sh` clean+tagged CD guard. Cross-references PRD invariant 8, ADR-00000003 (amended), and ADR-00000023.
- **BREAKING: `setup.conf` relocated out of `config/` to the repo-root `.setup.conf` dotfile (closes #831, ADR-00000006 / ADR-00000010 amended)** — `setup.conf` is `just setup`-managed, not hand-edited, yet it sat inside `config/`, which is being defined as the user's HAND-EDITABLE config surface (shell overrides + app config). It was the one tool-managed file mixed in. The per-repo override moves from `<repo>/config/docker/setup.conf` to the repo-root dotfile `<repo>/.setup.conf` (a dotfile signals "tool-managed, do not hand-hack"), and base's template default from `.base/dist/config/docker/setup.conf` to `.base/dist/.setup.conf`. This restores the original root location (reverses #262, which had nested it under `config/docker/` purely for layout uniformity — a rationale that dissolved once `config/` became the hand-editable surface). **Migration is automatic and fail-loud:** `just upgrade` (`upgrade.sh`) detects a legacy `config/docker/setup.conf`, `git mv`s it to `.setup.conf`, and warns loudly (when BOTH exist the root file wins and the legacy is kept for manual reconciliation — never silently dropped). A downstream that upgrades keeps its override active with no manual step. All path constants (`lib/setup_conf.sh` / `setup_cmd.sh` / `resolve.sh` / `wrapper.sh`, `init.sh --gen-conf`, `upgrade.sh` Region B drift-hash) re-point in lockstep per ADR-00000006's frozen-path discipline; `config/` no longer contains the tool-managed file (shell/app config layering unaffected). Unit (`setup_conf_spec`: override + fallback resolve from root `.setup.conf`, legacy path unread) + integration (`upgrade_spec`: legacy auto-migration, already-migrated no-op, both-exist conflict) cover it. README / i18n synced.
- **published ports are emitted as overlay-overridable `${PORT_<n>:-<default>}` interpolations (refs #716, ADR-00000022)** — `compose_emit.sh` previously baked each published port as a literal (`- "8080:80"`) on both the devel service and per-stage standalone blocks, so a multi_run `.env` overlay could not remap the host port per instance. Each port is now `${PORT_<n>:-<default>}` (n = 1-based index within the service's port list — `PORT_1` = first port, matching base's 1-based indexed-key convention `port_1` / `mount_1` / `arg_1`, so a human who configured `[network] port_1` overrides `PORT_1`); with the overlay var unset compose substitutes the `setup.conf` value, so single-run resolved behaviour is byte-equivalent, while a multi_run overlay sets `PORT_<n>` to isolate the instance. Downstream repos pick this up on their next `just setup`; the `#505` golden master and `gen_spec` port assertions were updated to the interpolation form.
- **language-aware `just <ns> help` recipe listing (i18n, closes #718)** — the per-namespace `help` recipe (`just docker help` / `just base help` / `just template help`, plus the `h` alias) now renders each recipe's one-line summary in the caller's language, riding base's existing colocated `_msg <category> <key>` i18n model. A new `_msg help <ns>.<recipe>` category (in the shared renderer `dist/script/docker/lib/help.sh`, which sources `i18n.sh`) carries every recipe summary across `zh-TW` / `zh-CN` / `ja` / `*` (English default), colocated per key; the effective language follows `$LANG` / `SETUP_LANG` and is overridable with `--lang`. `just --list` and bare `just <ns>` stay English by design — just's native listing cannot be intercepted (ADR-00000011 sec.6), so `just <ns> help` is the rich translated entry point. Scope matches the locked i18n boundary: `docker` / `base` / `template` are localised; `test` / `release` remain English-only.
- **`apply` resolvers parse setup.conf once instead of per-section (closes #743)** — follow-up to #742. The `apply` command's `[build]` / `[security]` / `[additional_contexts]` reads previously called `_load_setup_conf` once per section, each re-tokenizing the whole template+repo conf (three full parses). They now share a single `_setup_conf_handle` handle read via the `_conf_get_into` / `_conf_list_sorted` accessors — one tokenization for all three sections, same section-replace merge precedence and `SETUP_CONF` override. `[volumes]` deliberately keeps its dedicated single-section load: `_reconcile_workspace_path` mutates that parallel array in place and reloads it after a `mount_1` rewrite, which a handle built up-front would go stale against. Behaviour is byte-identical (guarded by the existing `setup_cmd` / `setup_detect` specs).

### Fixed
- **six surfaces where the tool taught the wrong model (closes #879)** — one audit, six places where the system's shape did not match what a competent user would assume, so the tool actively taught something false. (1) **Every user-facing surface said `setup` regenerates `.env`, and it never does.** `.env` is the hand-authored, gitignored workload overlay that `apply` scaffolds once and never rewrites; the file `apply` regenerates is `.env.generated`. Exactly one internal comment had it right. The cost runs both ways: someone who hand-edited `.env` reads "regenerate .env" and avoids the very command they are supposed to run, and someone expecting `apply` to rewrite `.env` from their conf edits waits for a change that never arrives. Corrected on every surface — the `just --list` doc comment, the localized `just docker help` listing in all four locales, the `set` / `add` / `remove` / `reset` "next:" hint, `apply`'s completion line in all four locales, the `setup.sh` / `setup_tui.sh` / `build.sh` / `run.sh` usage screens, the `.setup.conf` header, and README.md plus all three translations — and pinned by a new `env_generated_claim_spec.bats` whose cross-surface guard fails the build if any shipped surface starts claiming a bare `.env` is regenerated again. (2) **A configured `network.port_N` was discarded under the shipped default with no diagnostic.** The template ships `[network] mode = host` and compose honours `ports:` only under bridge, yet the TUI has a dedicated ports editor and `_validate_port_mapping` vets the value — the tool carefully checked something it was about to throw away. ADR-00000019 chose the host default deliberately, so the fix is a diagnostic, not a default change: one `_warn_ports_inert` helper now fires at store time (`set` / `add`, and the symmetric case of switching `mode` away from bridge with ports already configured) and again at every gate that drops the block (the devel service, each per-stage service, and the field-deploy emitter), localized in all four locales and latched once per emit so an N-stage repo prints one sentence. `--quiet` suppresses the confirmation receipt but never the diagnostic. (3) **`deploy` named two unrelated things one keystroke apart.** `setup.sh deploy` ships an image to the field; `setup_tui.sh deploy` opens the `[deploy]` section editor, which configures GPU reservation only because the section is named after Compose's `deploy:` key. The section name follows Compose and stays, and the subcommand is the documented field-deploy entry point and stays; the collision is what changed. The editor gains `gpu` as its unambiguous primary spelling (`just docker setup-tui gpu`), `deploy` remains a working alias that opens a notice naming both commands, and both usage screens spell out the distinction in all four locales. (4) **The env-boundary table promised a delivery path that does not exist.** The volatile-workload row's Field cell claimed "baked `ENV` defaults (+ optional field-side override)" for a value that lives in the `.env` overlay: baked `ENV` comes from `[environment]` (row 1, exactly as the prose three lines below already said), and nothing copies `.env` into a deploy bundle at all. An operator could put a token in `.env`, ship a bundle, and watch the container come up without it. The row now says the value is not carried and points at `[environment]`. (5) **`.setup.conf.local` was advertised and nothing read it.** It was a real override file introduced in #174 and removed again in #201; the `.gitignore` line was kept only so leftovers from that cycle would not get committed, and it now sits under "# managed by template (do not remove)", which reads as a feature — someone creates the file, gets no error, and wonders why their overrides do nothing. Restoring the override layer is a design change well past this issue, so the entry is retired: dropped from `_canonical_gitignore_entries`, recorded in a new `_retired_gitignore_entries` list, and `_sync_managed_entries` now prunes retired entries from the MANAGED half of an existing `.gitignore` / `.dockerignore` so the advertisement disappears from repos that already carry it, not just from new ones. Retraction is deliberately narrow — only named-retired entries, only below the marker — so a line the user wrote in their own half is never touched. (6) **`setup_tui.sh` exited 127 before parsing a single argument.** Exposed by the usage assertions in (3): `main` calls `_transcript_begin` as its first statement and `_transcript_detach` / `_run_{pre,post}_hook` later, but the script sourced neither `transcript.sh`, `log.sh` nor `hook.sh`. `setup_tui` is already registered as an interactive capture verb and transcript activation is explicit rather than source-time, so the sources were simply missing since they were introduced. `.env.example` (documented as the env template, never generated) is deliberately left to #868.
- **every wrapper and lib was unsourceable under the coverage shard's instrumented bash (closes #869)** — two independent defects wore one symptom, and the symptom appeared ONLY under kcov, so each cost a CI round trip and a wrong first diagnosis (#850's third attempted fix, then #867). **(1) Leaked strict mode.** kcov drives its line counter from `xtrace` with `PS4` set to a string that expands `${BASH_SOURCE}`. At the top level of a `bash -c '...'` string that array is empty, so any file that turned `nounset` on for its CALLER made the caller's next traced command die inside kcov's own PS4 — not in any line of the test, and after the `source` had already succeeded. `dist/script/docker/wrapper/setup_tui.sh` was the last file enabling `set -euo pipefail` unconditionally while documenting itself sourceable "for testing"; it now gates strict mode on being executed directly (`if [[ "${BASH_SOURCE[0]:-}" == "${0:-}" ]]`), the shape the ten sibling sourceable scripts (`setup.sh`, `init.sh`, `test.sh`, the sync/resolve tooling, the shell `setup.sh` pair) already used. That un-skips `setup_conf_spec`'s `usage()` case, whose `COVERAGE=1 && skip` was a caller-side workaround for a defect no caller-side `set +u` could reach. **(2) Unguarded self-location.** `${BASH_SOURCE[0]}` with no default aborts under the `nounset` the script itself just enabled, wherever bash does not populate the array for the running file. Blast radius was the whole chain — `dist/script/docker/lib/` alone had 8, so fixing one wrapper could not help — and the failure is not always loud: inside a command substitution only the SUBSHELL dies, the outer command still exits 0, and bash's stderr line is consumed AS THE VALUE (in #867 a guard ended up reporting a lint literally named `bash: line 3: BASH_SOURCE: unbound variable`, with `assert_success` passing). All 40 indexed reads across `dist/` + `script/` now default to `$0` (identical under direct execution, degrading instead of aborting elsewhere): the 12 libs (`_lib.sh`, `bootstrap.sh` ×3 incl. the frame-1 read, `compose.sh`, `config_summary.sh`, `conf_logging.sh`, `deploy.sh`, `help.sh`, `log.sh`, `schema.sh`, `transcript.sh`, `_tui_conf.sh`), the 8 docker wrappers, the 2 runtime helpers, the 4 `dist/script/base/` scripts, `template/new.sh`, the `terminator`/`tmux` setup pair, and 6 files of base's own `script/test/` tooling. `log.sh` / `logging.sh` / `watchdog.sh` used the `%/*` shorthand, which cannot carry a default, and became `dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]:-$0}"`. Whole-array forms (`[@]`, `[*]`, `${#...[@]}`) are deliberately untouched: bash yields an empty list for them on an unset array even under `set -u`. **Recurrence guard:** a new `bash-source-guard` lint (`script/test/drivers/bash_source_guard.sh`, `just test lint --bash-source-guard`, `test.sh --bash-source-guard-only`, CI job `lint-static (bash-source-guard)`) fails any undefaulted indexed read under `dist/` or `script/`, with a region-delimited `# bash-source-lint: allow-begin/allow-end` opt-out and a no-vacuous-pass scan-root check; `self_test_yaml_spec`'s `_LINT_TOOLS` completeness guard rises 8 -> 9 and pins the new name. **Verified under real kcov, not just locally** — `just docker build --target test-tools` first, since a cached image can predate kcov being source-built in (which is why earlier attempts reported kcov unavailable). New `sourceable_scripts_spec.bats` (8 tests): 7 of 8 red under kcov before, all 8 green after; 3 of those are green in a plain run and red only under kcov, which is the whole point. It discovers the sourceable set rather than pinning it (guard-carrying scripts + every `lib/*.sh`), floors the discovery at 12 / 25 so a broken expression cannot pass vacuously, and asserts a marker AFTER the `source` — merely asserting the source exited 0 is what let #867 pass while corrupt. New `bash_source_guard_lint_spec.bats` (18). Refs #869, #850, #867, #613.
- **the shipped smoke suite could not fail, and the shipped CD guard was both untested and not executable (closes #877)** — `dist/test/bats/smoke/devel-test/display_env.bats` is vendored into **every** downstream repo and runs at Dockerfile `-test`-stage build time. It presented as eleven assertions and contributed **one** genuinely-executed, non-tautological check. Proven by mutation, not inspection: with the wayland/x11 branches **swapped** in `dist/script/docker/wrapper/run.sh`, all eleven stayed green (seven skipped, four passed). Three failure shapes, all fixed. **(1) Tests that never ran what they were named after.** The three tests called *"run.sh calls xhost ..."* built an `xhost` mock and then `bash -c`'d an inline hand-copy of `run.sh`'s branch, asserting the copy — `run.sh` was neither invoked nor read, so deleting the branch, inverting it, or changing `+SI:localuser:` left them green. They now execute the real wrapper, via a new `run_wrapper_xhost <wrapper> [NAME=VALUE ...]` driver in the shared smoke `test_helper`: it builds a sandbox holding a symlink to the wrapper plus a symlink to its `lib/` chain and a minimal `.env.generated`, so the wrapper resolves `FILE_PATH` there, finds no `.base/` and no `.setup.conf`, skips the setup/drift lifecycle, and `--dry-run` reaches the host-ACL grant with **no docker daemon and no setup.sh**. It reports every `xhost` call and **fails rather than reporting nothing** when the wrapper exits non-zero or never calls `xhost` — otherwise a deleted branch would satisfy every `refute_output`. Chosen over extracting the branch into a sourceable helper, which would test the helper while leaving the call site (the code that runs on every `just run`) unexecuted. The five replacement assertions are two-sided (each names what must appear **and** what must not), plus a one-grant-per-invocation count and a check that the Wayland grant interpolates `USER_NAME` instead of a fixed name. **(2) Seven assertions that were unreachable everywhere, not merely on headless CI.** They were gated on `[[ -f /lint/compose.yaml ]]`, and `compose.yaml` is a derived artifact listed in `.dockerignore` — it is in no repo's build context, so `/lint/compose.yaml` cannot exist in any `-test` stage of any repo and the gate is false in every build. The first of them also re-ran the exact `grep` its own skip-gate had already run, so it could report pass or skip but never fail. Their intent moves to `test/bats/unit/compose_emit/gen_spec.bats`, where `generate_compose_yaml` is driven with the GUI resolved **both on and off** so both sides can fail: `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` env + mirrored `rw` mount under gui=true, neither under gui=false, and the GUI-independent **no-duplicate-key-within-a-service** structural check (two identical keys in one service is a hard `docker compose up` parse error) asserted for gui on **and** off, so it now runs on every CI run — plus a known-bad document fed to the detector itself, so a checker that silently never fires cannot repeat the failure mode being removed. The new detector is stricter than the `awk` it replaces: that one compared adjacent 4-space key lines, this one flags a repeated key anywhere within a service block and names the service. **(3) A shipped CD gate with four outcomes and no behavioural test.** `dist/deploy/cd-guard.sh` (ADR-00000023) refuses an automated deploy unless the tree is clean **and** HEAD sits on a tag; its only reference in `test/` grepped it for a documentation string. New `cd_guard_spec.bats` (7) drives the real script against `mktemp` git fixtures — non-repo, clean+untagged, dirty+**tagged**, tagged-but-past-the-tag, clean+tagged — asserting exit status **and** the specific refusal message, because a status-only check passes with the conditions inverted (dirty reported as untagged and vice versa), plus the stdout/stderr split. Writing it surfaced a real defect: **the guard was committed mode `100644`** while every other shipped script is `100755`, so the invocation its own usage block and all four READMEs document (`./.base/dist/deploy/cd-guard.sh`) exited 126 "Permission denied" in every consumer — and a CD pipeline treating any non-zero as "refused" would have looked like it was working. Mode fixed; the spec asserts it. Finally, the `xhost` driver is exercised from base's own gate (`smoke_helper_spec.bats`, 19 -> 28) against the wrapper at its source path, so the real branch is covered by `just test` and not only by a downstream image build — the shipped specs themselves still execute only inside a `-test` stage.
- **five silent-acceptance defects: something accepted, discarded or reported with no signal (closes #876)** — one audit, five instances of the same shape, all held to "nothing may fail silently": not merely "the code now does the right thing" but "when it cannot, the user is told". (1) **The managed `.gitignore` block ate user lines below it.** `_sync_logging_gitignore` scoped its block by SHAPE — the marker line plus every following `/<dir>/` line — and re-emitted it from the desired set, so a rule appended at the end of the file (exactly where a user appends) was deleted, folded into a large subtree-upgrade commit labelled something else, with no prompt and no diff review. The block now carries an explicit BEGIN / END marker pair, the same allow-begin / allow-end region shape the stale-path and home-literal lints use; only lines strictly between the markers are template-owned, and a begin with no end (or an end with no begin, or a second block) is refused loudly with the file left byte-identical rather than guessing the block's extent. The **dead trailing-newline guard** in `_sync_managed_entries` is fixed in the SAME change, not before it: command substitution strips the trailing newline, so `tail -c 1` could never observe one, the guard always fired, and the blank line it emitted was the only reason the newer canonical `/deploy/` entry — appended by `_sync_gitignore` immediately before this sync runs in the same `init.sh` pass — was not itself swallowed. Fixing the cosmetic blank alone would have made `/deploy/` never converge (verified by running the real sync repeatedly against a temp fixture, both ways). **Migration**: a pre-bounded block written by an older template keeps its own distinct marker text and is migrated in place, consuming only the `/<dir>/` lines the block itself would emit today; anything else below it ends the legacy block and is kept as user content, with a warning naming the lines that changed hands — leaving a stale ignore line behind is a wart, deleting a user's rule is data loss. (2) **`setup set` accepted values the resolvers then discarded.** `just setup set gui.mode of` exited 0 and printed `set [gui] mode = of`; `_resolve_gui` fell into its `auto|*` catch-all and the GUI followed host detection instead of being forced off, while the same value as `--gui of` was rejected with exit 2. Rather than registering the reported five validators by hand — which fixes today's list and reopens the gap on the next template key — `schema.sh` gains a `SCHEMA_FREEFORM` opt-out map and `schema_coverage_spec` asserts that EVERY key the shipped `setup.conf` declares (live line or commented example) resolves to a `SCHEMA_VALIDATOR` entry or is opted out with a written reason. That made the real set nine: `gui.mode`, `deploy.gpu_mode`, `deploy.gpu_capabilities`, `deploy.dri_groups`, `security.security_opt_`, plus `security.privileged` and `image.rule_1..3`. New validators: `_validate_detect_mode` (the shared auto/force/off tri-state), `_validate_gpu_capabilities`, `_validate_dri_groups`, `_validate_privileged`, `_validate_security_opt`, `_validate_image_rule`. `SCHEMA_FREEFORM` ships empty. (3) **`[stage:*]` inverted the legacy-alias precedence the global resolver uses.** `_resolve_docker_flags` resolved `deploy.gpu_runtime` first and then resolved the legacy `deploy.runtime` with THAT result as its fallback, so under `[stage:*]` the deprecated key won — the opposite of `_resolve_deploy_context`, where `gpu_runtime` is authoritative. A repo migrating `[stage:sim] runtime = nvidia` to `gpu_runtime = off` and leaving the old line behind kept emitting `runtime: nvidia` into that stage's compose service and its field bundle, and never saw the deprecation warning. Both layers now agree, and both emit `conf_runtime_key_deprecated` on the legacy key's PRESENCE rather than only when its value is consumed — otherwise the half-finished migration is invisible on exactly the path that has it. The alias itself is untouched: it remains supported until v1.0.0. (4) **Every `init.sh` error printed a logger-internal complaint.** `_error` passed the human message where `_log_dispatch` expects a registered event id, so a missing template conf, `--gen-conf` over an existing conf and a failed `--bootstrap-just` all surfaced as `FATAL: unregistered log body "..." -- add it to .../log-events.txt`: no `[init] ERROR:` framing, no timestamp, no JSON record when piped, and an instruction to edit a registry file that means nothing to a user. It now calls `_log_err init init_failed "display=$*"`, matching `upgrade.sh`'s sibling. (5) **The ShellCheck driver missed half of `script/test/`.** It named `test.sh`, `sync-doc-counts.sh` and `resolve-doc-counts.sh` one by one and swept only the `drivers/` subdirectory, leaving `check_test_md_drift.sh`, `lint_bare_stderr.sh` and the 333-line `sync-readme-hashes.sh` generator (which `mktemp`/`mv`-rewrites three tracked README files) unlinted — and the same batch that added `resolve-doc-counts.sh` to the list forgot them. One `find` sweep replaces the list, so a new gate script is linted the moment it exists. The one real finding it surfaced is fixed, not disabled (`sync-readme-hashes.sh:172`, SC2004); the single disable added is on `SCHEMA_FREEFORM` (SC2034 — declarative registry data read by the coverage spec, not by the lib), with its reason recorded above the directive.
- **the localized-README sync guard was green over stale translations, because a re-stamp could not be told from a re-translation (closes #873)** — #846 gave each translated section a fingerprint of the English section it was translated against, and `sync-readme-hashes.sh` re-stamped that fingerprint unconditionally. Nothing checked the translation body had moved too, so "update the translation, then sync" and "sync so the gate stops complaining" wrote identical bytes: the generator was a one-command way to bless exactly the drift it existed to catch. The mitigation assumed when #846 landed — that a bare re-stamp shows up in review as a hash change with no prose change — was weaker than it looked, since the initial stamping commit moved every hash at once and left no "this hash moved alone" signal to notice. An audit recomputed the fingerprints and found the guard genuinely green over two sections whose translations had never been updated. The marker now records **both sides**, `<!-- sync: <id> <english-hash> <translation-hash> -->`, the second field computed by the same algorithm over the translated heading and body (marker lines excluded from the input, or each section's hash would depend on its neighbour's and stamping would have no fixed point). That turns an unanswerable question into an arithmetic one, and the generator acts on it: if the English hash moved while the recorded translation hash still matches the translation on disk, that section is **refused** — left byte-for-byte alone, named with file / line / id, and the run exits non-zero, so the lint stays red and syncing buys nothing. The refusal is per section, which also covers the bulk case the issue raised: a run that would move every hash at once with no translation-side change is refused once per section rather than passing as a single anonymous re-stamp. #846's accepted cost is preserved rather than regressed: an English typo fix genuinely needs no re-translation, and `just test sync-readme --accept <id>[,<id>...]` is that statement — still one command, but explicit, per section, by name, and never the default. A translator updating one section is unaffected: their edit moves the translation hash, which is the evidence the generator was missing. The lint gains **UNRECORDED** (the translated prose changed and the generator was never re-run) to keep that evidence fresh — without it, an unrelated un-stamped translation edit would later be misread as proof of a re-translation — and treats a half-stamped marker as UNSTAMPED. The two stale sections are translated in the same change; the acceptance test performs the silencing case and asserts it fails, rather than asserting on the marker format, since the format was never the defect.
- **several documents stated the opposite of what the code does (closes #874)** — an audit after the restart-rescope / deployable-stage / read-only-manifest batch found the code had moved and the documents had not. **README.md promised `upgrade.sh` never touches per-repo files.** It does: two migrations run before step 1, each committing against the user's own files (`config/docker/setup.conf` -> `.setup.conf`, and `[lifecycle] restart = no` -> `unless-stopped`), and step 5 heals the repo-root `Dockerfile` + `script/entrypoint.sh` into the upgrade commit. A maintainer who read the old line got a restart policy flipped under a live field container -- auto-restarting after a host reboot -- in commits they did not author. All four READMEs now carry a "What upgrade.sh rewrites in your repo" table (when, what, the commit message it lands under), a call-out that the restart migration changes runtime behaviour and how to put it back, and the two `git` commands that show exactly what was done. **The only restart documentation said the opposite of the rescope:** default `no`, applied on `devel`, inherited by `extends: devel` -- all three false since the template ships `unless-stopped` and the emitter clears the policy for any stage failing `_is_deployable_stage`. A `#### Restart policy is deploy-scoped` section now states the rule, including that the "avoid `always` on a stage that exits 0" hazard is prevented by construction rather than by care. **README contradicted itself about `devel-test` 45 lines apart** (blocklisted in one passage, emitted as the `test` service in the next) -- now pinned by the `derived-figures` lint rather than by prose. **CONTEXT.md still defined the retired deploy model** (`deploy.sh` launcher + `tar.xz`, docker-level run flags) -- the artifact is a folder with a fully-resolved compose, and `deploy bundle` / `deploy.manifest` / `deployable stage` had no glossary entry at all despite being the vocabulary of the whole batch; that matters because CONTEXT.md's job is to stop future work re-coining terms and it is the first file a grill session reads. Also: `doc/PRD.md` invariant 8 stated the deployable rule more narrowly than `_is_deployable_stage` enforces it (an invariant that does not match its own predicate is worse than none) and now states it in full; ADR-00000023 gained in-file amendments for the read-only-by-default manifest mount (#870) and the same predicate widening; README's Directory Structure block described the pre-per-stage smoke layout and a stale ADR range; and `[Unreleased]` had duplicate `### Changed` / `### Added` subsections.
- **four static lints gated nothing on a PR because no CI job runs the `just test` lint phase (closes #866)** — the gap #864 found for one gate is the gap for the whole phase: the two lint jobs narrow to one tool (`--shellcheck-only`, `--lint --hadolint`) and every bats / coverage job sets `BATS_ONLY=1` / `COVERAGE=1`, both of which skip the phase. So the issue-ref comment lint (ADR-00000013), the ADR-numbering lint, the stale `config/docker/setup.conf` path lint (#845) and the localized README sync lint (#846) ran only for a developer who ran the full local suite — an agent, or anyone running a narrowed command, could push a violation of any of them and merge green. Two of them were added days earlier on the reasoning that an ungated rule rots. Each driver turned out to be pure bash over the checkout (verified by running it host-direct, not assumed), so each gained a `--<tool>-only` primitive and the four are wired as a **`lint-static` matrix**, one entry per lint on a plain runner: a red check is named (`lint-static (readme-sync)`), so a reviewer sees WHICH lint failed without opening a log, and `fail-fast: false` keeps one failure from hiding its siblings. `hadolint` stays out of the matrix — its binary exists only in the test-tools image. The matrix is **ungated**, like `doc-counts`: the ADR-numbering lint reads `doc/adr/` filenames and the README sync lint reads `README.md` + `doc/readme/**`, both of which `classify` scores as doc-only, so a `code_changed` gate would skip them on exactly the PR they exist to catch — and a matrix shares one job-level `if:` anyway. It is hard-mandatory in `ci-rollup` (SKIPPED means a workflow bug) and joins the release chain. The deeper fix is against recurrence: the three callers that each repeated the tool list (the full phase, the in-container `LINT_TOOL` narrowing, the host-direct primitives) now dispatch through one `_LINT_TOOLS` table in `script/test/test.sh`, and a completeness guard in `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` asserts every entry of that table is named by a job in `self-test.yaml`. Adding a lint to the phase without giving it a CI job now fails a test instead of quietly shipping a local-only rule; `doc/test/TEST.md` carries the table of which lint is enforced by which job.
- **the doc-count drift gate ran in neither `just test` nor CI (closes #864)** — `script/test/check_test_md_drift.sh` was the gate for the doc/test catalogue, but it was reachable only through `just test sync-docs-check`, which a human has to remember to type, and through an advisory PostToolUse hook in the harness repo, which fires only mid-edit in an interactive session. Neither is on the path a branch must pass, so a branch could add a `@test`, leave the catalogue stale, pass the full local gate, pass CI, and merge — the path every doc-count drift in the recent batch actually took. Making the catalogue generated (#857 / #859) removed the *cause* of the rot; this closes the other half, noticing it when it rots anyway. The gate is now a per-tool driver (`script/test/drivers/doc_counts.sh`) in the same lint phase as its siblings, the stale-`setup.conf` path lint and the localized-README sync lint, so it runs in `just test` and narrows the same way (`just test lint --doc-counts`). "In the lint phase" turned out **not** to imply "in CI": no CI job runs that phase — the two lint jobs narrow to one tool (`--shellcheck-only`, `--lint --hadolint`) and every bats job sets `BATS_ONLY=1`, which skips it — so the gate also gained a host-direct primitive (`--doc-counts-only`, pure bash + diff, no compose) and a dedicated `doc-counts` job on a plain runner, joined to `ci-rollup` and to the release chain. That job is deliberately **ungated**, unlike its sibling lint jobs: the catalogue can also be broken by hand-editing `doc/test/*.md`, which the classifier scores as a doc-only change, so a `code_changed` gate would skip the gate on exactly the PR that broke it — and it is therefore hard-mandatory in the rollup, where SKIPPED means a workflow bug. The same phase gap applies to the issue-ref, ADR-numbering, stale-`setup.conf` and localized-README lints, which still run in no CI job; that is a wider hole than this issue and is left for a follow-up. Enabling it is a behavioural no-op on a clean tree — verified green before wiring, because turning on an already-red gate is how gates get disabled again. `just test sync-docs-check` now routes through the same driver rather than invoking the script directly, so the on-demand and blocking paths cannot diverge. The harness hook stays, deliberately: it duplicates the gate but holds no rule of its own (both call the same script), and its value is latency — the relationship, and which one wins, is stated in `doc/test/TEST.md` and at the wiring site.
- **doc-count drift gate returned a confident WRONG answer on a relative scan root, and passed vacuously on an empty one (closes #848)** — `script/test/check_test_md_drift.sh <root>` runs the real generator non-destructively by copying `doc/test` into a temp dir and symlinking the spec trees (`test/`, `dist/`) in from `<root>`. A RELATIVE `<root>` was therefore recorded as a relative symlink target and resolved against the TEMP dir on that hop: every spec glob missed, every count came back 0, and the gate reported drift on every doc — not an error, a confident wrong answer. Two implementation agents in one session each hit it while verifying unrelated doc-count changes and each concluded their work had broken every count. The asymmetry is what makes it surprising: the sibling `sync-doc-counts.sh` has no temp-dir hop and takes a relative root fine, so passing `.` to both is the natural thing to do. New `_check_drift_resolve_root` resolves `<root>` to an absolute, symlink-resolved path (`pwd -P`) before anything else — `./script/test/check_test_md_drift.sh .` and the same call with an absolute root now give identical verdicts — and reports a missing root by name instead of proceeding. The same shape had a second-order risk: a scan root with no `doc/test/` used to `return 0` outright and a spec-free root compared 0 against 0, so a relocation of the spec tree would have made the gate pass vacuously rather than fail. Both are now hard failures naming the resolved root (`_check_drift_count_specs` over `test/bats/**/*_spec.bats` + `dist/test/bats/smoke/**/*.bats`), mirroring the missing-scan-root guard the #845 stale-path lint and `lint_bare_stderr.sh` already use — never fail silently. Diagnostics are block-redirected to stderr because this is a standalone `log.sh`-free tool inside the bare-stderr lint's scan scope. `check_test_md_drift_spec.bats` (3 -> 9) covers both halves: a relative root on a clean tree passes, a relative root on a drifted tree still fails and names the doc, and a nonexistent / `doc/test`-less / spec-free root each fails loudly.
- **`setup deploy` accepted a non-deployable stage; `deployable = not devel and not *-test` now has an enforcement point (closes #841)** — PRD invariant 8 and ADR-00000023 sec.4 state that development and test stages are never deploy targets, but the rule had no enforcement anywhere: `_setup_deploy` parsed `--stage` into `_stage` with zero eligibility checking and handed it straight to `docker build --target`. `devel` / `devel-test` / `runtime-test` are all real, buildable targets in the template Dockerfile, so `just setup deploy --stage devel -y` succeeded and wrote a complete field bundle -- `image.tar.xz` plus a `restart: unless-stopped` compose -- from an image that carries the whole toolchain and is designed to bind host source; `sys` / `devel-base` are build intermediates with no runnable service at all. Every other precondition in that same flow already failed loud (missing Dockerfile, malformed / duplicate manifest, no baked default, non-tty confirmation) -- the single most important one did not. `_setup_deploy` now refuses a non-deployable stage the moment the stage is known (before the repo is located, before any preview / build / bundle step), via `_log_err` in the same shape as the `deploy_no_dockerfile` path (new registered event `deploy_stage_not_deployable`). The rule is NOT re-derived: the guard calls the `_is_deployable_stage` predicate #840 hoisted into `stage.sh`, which is widened here to also reject the build intermediates `sys` / `devel-base` (and the legacy alias `base`) alongside `devel` / `test` / `*-test` -- the whole template-managed baseline. That widening is inert for the predicate's other caller (the compose `restart:` emitter): `sys` / `devel-base` / `base` are filtered out of the stage list by `_parse_dockerfile_stages` and are never emitted as services. Everything else stays deployable -- the deploy model is explicitly multi-stage and `runtime` is only the template's example name. `deploy_spec.bats`'s `--stage selects the target stage` spec asserted exactly the forbidden case (`--stage devel --dry-run` succeeds, builds `--target devel`); it is rewritten to select a second DEPLOYABLE stage, and joined by refusal specs for the baseline, the legacy aliases, a downstream-shaped `<x>-test` stage, and a `-y` run that must write no bundle at all, plus a `stage_spec.bats` unit for the widened predicate. Out of scope per the issue: `dist/deploy/cd-guard.sh` (takes no stage argument today) and `_validate_stage_name` (stage-NAME collisions, a different concern).
- **field compose dropped the watchdog and the ENV bake; `[lifecycle] restart` rescoped from devel to deployable stages (closes #840)** — three defects in the field-deploy emitter. The first two share a shape: `_resolve_deploy_context` computed a value that `_generate_resolved_compose` / `_generate_deploy_bundle` never consumed, so the dev compose honoured it and the field bundle silently did not. The third turned out to be a scoping error in the key itself, not just an unconsumed value. (1) **Watchdog env is now emitted into the field service `environment:`**, reusing `_emit_watchdog_env` exactly as the dev path does; the `environment:` header (previously emitted only under GUI) is now emitted for whichever of GUI / watchdog is present, and stays absent when neither is (no empty block). This is the field-critical one: `restart:` only recovers a container that **exits**, while the watchdog is the only mechanism that recovers a service that is **alive but wedged** (`realsense_ros1#81` documents exactly that on real hardware). Delivered through compose rather than baked `ENV` so the thresholds stay visible and adjustable in the self-contained bundle. (2) **The `[environment]` ENV bake follows the stage being built.** `_generate_runtime_dockerfile` matched the literal `AS runtime`, but `runtime` is only the template's example name -- a downstream repo deploying its own stage name silently lost every `[environment]` value from the image (and `_generate_resolved_compose` sets `env_top=""`, so compose did not re-emit them either), while the sibling `_bake_config_copy` / `docker build --target` already followed `${_stage}`. The stage is now a required 4th argument, and the `FROM ... AS <stage>` token is captured and compared literally rather than spliced into the pattern. (3) **`[lifecycle] restart` is RESCOPED from devel to deployable stages, and its default flips ON.** The key used to be emitted on the `devel` service and inherited by `extends: devel` stages, while the field bundle ignored the conf entirely and hardcoded `unless-stopped`. Both halves were wrong. A devel container IS the interactive shell -- it lives exactly as long as that shell, so `always` / `unless-stopped` on it relaunches the container the moment the developer types `exit`, forever (the hazard the template `.setup.conf` already warned about, and the exit-0 restart loop base#493 documents); a `*-test` stage exits 0 when it passes, so a restart policy loops it too. A service-shaped container is the opposite: a deployable stage and the field bundle are meant to run forever, so auto-restart -- after a crash and after a host reboot -- is exactly right. `restart:` is therefore emitted ONLY for deployable stages and the field bundle, never for `devel` and never for a `*-test` stage; ADR-00000023 sec.4's rule (`deployable = not devel and not *-test`) is hoisted into `stage.sh` as the shared `_is_deployable_stage` predicate, devel emits nothing for `extends` to inherit, and `_emit_stage_service` emits the policy itself in both the `extends: devel` and the standalone stage shapes. With dev and field no longer wanting opposite defaults, the "key absent vs explicitly `no`" distinction is gone: the template ships the default LITERALLY (`[lifecycle] restart = unless-stopped`) so an operator can see it, `_resolve_deploy_context` folds the same value in as the fallback for a conf hand-stripped of the key, and `_setup_apply` drops its private dev default. An explicitly configured value is honoured verbatim everywhere it applies (`on-failure:N` YAML-quoted; `no` emits no field at all in the dev compose -- that IS Docker's no-restart default -- and is written out literally in the fully-resolved field compose), with `_validate_restart` still guarding the apply-time trust boundary so a malformed hand-edited value cannot emit a broken `restart:` line. **Downstream migration:** `init.sh --gen-conf` copies the WHOLE template, so every existing downstream repo carries a literal `restart = no` from the old template -- a devel-scoped answer to a question that is now only ever asked about a field service, and left alone it would silently deny that service its auto-start on reboot. `upgrade.sh` gains `_migrate_lifecycle_restart_default`, which rewrites that one line to the new default (loud warning + scoped commit, following `_migrate_legacy_setup_conf`) and is gated on the PRE-PULL vendored template still shipping the old default -- so it goes permanently inert once the new template lands, and a `no` the repo chooses afterwards, or any other value it already chose (`on-failure:5`, ...), is never touched. Unit coverage in `deploy_spec.bats` (the watchdog record, the three compose emissions, the shared `environment:` header, the malformed-value fallback, the resolver default), `stage_spec.bats` (`_is_deployable_stage`; bake into a caller-named stage, siblings untouched, absent stage no-op), `compose_emit/gen_spec.bats` (restart never on devel, emitted on a deployable stage in both shapes, absent on `*-test`, `on-failure:N` quoted, `no` emitting nothing), `setup_spec.bats` (the scope end to end through `main apply` + the template contract) and `upgrade_spec.bats` (the migration and its three inert cases), plus Integration coverage in `deploy_bundle_flow_spec.bats` (watchdog + restart carried end to end into the bundle compose, and `[environment]` baked into a non-`runtime` stage) and `integration/upgrade_spec.bats` (the migration through a real upgrade, and a deliberate policy left alone).
- **doc-count drift gate was blind to level-4 (`####`) headings (closes #815)** — `sync-doc-counts.sh`'s `_sync_headings` anchored its regex on `^###`, so a level-4 `#### <spec> (N)` sub-heading (the per-leaf-lib specs under the re-split `setup` group, ADR-00000015) never matched — its `(N)` was never regenerated by `just test sync-docs`, and `check_test_md_drift.sh` (which reuses the same generator) could not detect drift on it. This already bit us once (`setup_spec.bats` 109 -> 113, hand-fixed). The regex now matches any ATX depth (`#{3,6}`), captures the hash prefix, and re-emits at the original depth; the drift checker inherits the fix. Regression test in `doc_counts_spec.bats`.
- **`_system_setup` prerequisite-guard tests raced on a shared global socket path (closes #816)** — the two `#692` guard tests in `ci_spec.bats` both operated on the single literal `/var/run/docker.sock` (one asserts the guard fires when it is absent, the other creates a socket there then removes it), so under parallel bats (`--jobs`) the "absent" assertion failed intermittently when the other test's socket appeared mid-run. `_system_setup` now probes `${SYSTEM_DOCKER_SOCK:-/var/run/docker.sock}` (production behaviour unchanged with the env unset) and each test points the override at its own `BATS_TEST_TMPDIR` path — no shared mutable global, no race.
- **local `test-tools` tag is version-scoped so it no longer collides across repos/versions on one host (closes #828)** — the local pre-built-image flow tagged the tools image with a bare, version-agnostic `test-tools:local` while CI uses the version-scoped `ghcr.io/ycpss91255-docker/test-tools:<version>`. On a shared host, two downstream repos (or two builds) pinning different base versions therefore shared the single `test-tools:local` tag — whichever built last won, and the other silently built/linted against the WRONG tool version with no error. `dist/script/docker/wrapper/build.sh` now derives the local tag from the pinned base subtree version (`.base/.version`), building/removing `test-tools:<version>` (mirroring the CI tag minus the registry prefix); it fails loud if it must build the image but cannot resolve that version (no silent bare-tag fallback). The resolved tag is forwarded to the compose build as the `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE` build-arg so the downstream Dockerfile's `FROM ${TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE}` resolves it, and `dist/dockerfile/Dockerfile` drops its bare `ARG TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE="test-tools:local"` default (now `ARG TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE`, always passed explicitly by build.sh locally / CI over the wire — an unset value fails the build loudly instead of reusing a stale image). base's own self-test workflow keeps its in-run `test-tools:local` handle (ephemeral GitHub-hosted runners never share a host, so there is no collision surface there, and its acceptance job passes `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE` explicitly). Unit-tested in `build_sh_spec.bats` (version-scoped tag derivation, forwarded build-arg, fail-loud on a missing `.base/.version`) and `template_spec.bats` (no bare-tag ARG default). Closes #828.
- **localized READMEs, TEST.md prose and the deploy regenerate hints re-aligned to the resolved-compose deploy model (closes #843)** — the #497/#832 epic rewrote the ENGLISH `README.md` field-deployment section for the self-contained bundle FOLDER and relocated the config template to `dist/.setup.conf`, but the three localized READMEs were never carried along: `doc/readme/README.{zh-TW,zh-CN,ja}.md` still documented the superseded single-`tar.xz` "immutable image + generated `docker run` launcher" model (`./setup.sh deploy -o /tmp/robot.tar.xz`, `./deploy.sh` with `-e` overrides and `DEPLOY_IMAGE=` env vars), with no mention of `up`/`down`/`logs`, the resolved `compose.yaml`, `deploy.manifest`, `restart: unless-stopped` or `cd-guard.sh` — a zh/ja reader following them operated a deploy model that no longer exists. All three sections are now ported from the English source of truth (bundle folder layout table, ordered build steps, field-machine `deploy.sh up|down|logs` usage, mount-wins `config/` overrides via `deploy.manifest`, and the CD guard), their `setup.sh` subcommand `deploy` rows and section anchors follow, their directory trees list `.setup.conf` instead of the removed `config/docker/setup.conf`, and their `[deploy]` key table shows `gpu_runtime` (legacy `runtime` still accepted). `doc/test/TEST.md`'s hand-written blockquote prose figure ("System (5)") contradicted the table in the same file (7) because `sync-doc-counts.sh` regenerated only the table rows and per-level headings; the prose System/smoke pair is now regenerated too, so the drift gate catches it instead of a human. The regenerate hints stamped into generated artifacts printed a command the CLI rejects (`just setup deploy <stage>` — `_setup_deploy` parses only `--stage` / `-o` / `--dry-run` / `-y` / `-q`, so a bare positional errors "unknown arg"); the resolved-compose header, the `deploy.sh` launcher and `dist/deploy/cd-guard.sh` now emit the `--stage` form, covered by a new `deploy_hint_spec.bats` that replays each emitted hint's own argument list through the real parser. `CONTEXT.md` no longer calls the `devel` stage a deployable artifact (PRD invariant 8 / ADR-00000023 sec.4: `devel` and `*-test` are never deploy targets). Closes #843.
- **entrypoint lib guard, scoped migration commit, stale `setup.conf` paths (closes #842)** — four small defects on the freshly-changed `setup.conf`-relocation / runtime-entrypoint surface. (1) `runtime/entrypoint.sh` sourced `/usr/local/lib/base/logging.sh` and `.../watchdog.sh` **unguarded**. The devel stage always COPYs them, but the runtime stage's COPYs are opt-in (commented out) and `init.sh` seeds this entrypoint verbatim into every repo — so a runtime image that skipped them sourced two missing files on every container start: stderr noise with the stock entrypoint, and an abort before `exec` for a consumer following the documented `set -euo pipefail` pattern. Both sources are now wrapped in `[[ -r ... ]]`, the same shape `logging.sh` / `watchdog.sh` already use for their own sibling `logrotate.sh` source. (2) `upgrade.sh`'s `_migrate_legacy_setup_conf` committed the relocation with **no pathspec**; the pre-flight only requires a clean merge state, never a clean index, so a user with unrelated staged changes got them swept into a commit labelled as the relocation. The commit is now scoped to the paths the migration actually touches (the legacy path is added only when git tracked it, otherwise it is nameable in neither HEAD nor the working tree), which also leaves the user's staged work staged. (3) The first-time bootstrap in `setup_detect.sh` kept a `mkdir -p "$(dirname "${_rwp_repo_conf}")"` under a "Ensure `config/docker/` parent dir exists" comment; post-relocation that `dirname` is the repo root, so the call was a confirmed no-op describing a layout that no longer exists — dropped. (4) User-facing help and comments still named the pre-relocation paths: the four `setup_tui.sh` usage heredocs (zh-TW / zh-CN / ja / en) told the user the TUI edits `<repo>/setup.conf`, and `setup.sh`'s header added a wrong template default (`.base/setup.conf`; the real template resolves to `.base/dist/.setup.conf`). Every shipped `dist/` mention now names `<repo>/.setup.conf` / `.base/dist/.setup.conf`. Regression coverage: `template_spec.bats` (guard present + the entrypoint really reaching `exec` with no stderr under `set -euo pipefail` with both libs absent), `upgrade_spec.bats` (relocation commit excludes pre-staged unrelated work and leaves it staged), and `setup_conf_spec.bats` (all four usage heredocs name `.setup.conf`; no shipped `dist/` text still says `<repo>/setup.conf` or `.base/setup.conf`).
- **coverage gate double-counted kcov path aliases, so adding one spec moved the project rate (closes #853)** — `_coverage_gate_run` merges the per-shard cobertura reports by per-line UNION precisely so a source file exercised from several shards is not double-counted, but the union key was the RAW `<class filename>` kcov emits — and kcov reports the same source file under several prefix-truncated aliases in one report set (`lib/_lib.sh`, `docker/lib/_lib.sh`, `script/docker/lib/_lib.sh`, `dist/script/docker/lib/_lib.sh` are all one file; 33 distinct basenames carried more than one alias on `main`). Each alias is a different key prefix, so each contributed its own full copy of the file's lines to `valid` while only the aliases that recorded hits contributed to `covered`: the denominator was inflated and the reported rate structurally understated. Worse, it was UNSTABLE — which alias a file appears under depends on which shard executed it, and shard membership is recomputed from the spec-file list, so adding a single spec reshuffled the shards and moved the rate (a docs-only PR measured 48.57% against the 50% floor while `main` at the same base measured 51.54%, reproducibly, failing a required gate for reasons unrelated to its own coverage). This is the residue of the same failure family the union was introduced to fix (#377 / #615): the union removed the shard-COUNT sensitivity but left the path-ALIAS sensitivity, turning a systematic symptom into an intermittent one. The filename is now CANONICALISED before it becomes part of the key: the canonical name is the LONGEST filename in the report set of which it is a path suffix, matched on `/` component boundaries. The aliases are pure prefix truncations of one another, so this is exact and needs no filesystem access. Basename-only keying is explicitly rejected — distinct files legitimately share a basename (`dist/script/docker/lib/deploy.sh` and the generated field launcher `deploy.sh`) and merging them would silently OVERSTATE coverage. Observations are collected on the raw filename in a single pass and re-keyed at `END`, since the canonical map is only knowable once every filename in the set has been seen; the helper stays self-contained (no `_die` / `_log_*`), so the standalone CI CLI and the sourced driver still share it. The gate additionally reports the collapsed-alias count on stderr — always, including `0` — so a future regression in kcov's path reporting moves a visible number instead of failing silently. `COVERAGE_MIN` is deliberately unchanged: the rate rises once aliases stop being double-counted, and re-basing the floor belongs to a follow-up now that the measurement is correct. Regression coverage in `coverage_gate_spec.bats` (aliases of one file counted once, two files sharing a basename kept separate, rate invariance when the same suite is resharded under other aliases, and the diagnostic count in both the aliased and unaliased case).

### Tests
- **deploy version stamp: the tagless-clone fallback is now covered, and the field-deploy e2e runs against a real tagged fixture (closes #844)** — two gaps where a regression would have passed the whole suite. (1) `_resolve_deploy_version` is `git describe --tags --always --dirty`, but the specs covered only tagged, tagged+dirty and outside-git; the middle branch — a real repo with commits and NO tags, where `--always` is what yields the short SHA — had no test, so dropping `--always` would have made `describe` fail on every untagged repo and silently degraded each such deploy to `unknown` (collapsing exactly the version-collision avoidance the stamp exists for) with the gate still green. A new `deploy_spec.bats` unit test asserts a tagless clone resolves to a non-empty, non-`unknown`, non-tag value matching a 7+ hex short SHA; verified by mutation (removing `--always` makes it the only failing test). (2) `deploy_bundle_e2e_spec.bats` built its fixture repo with `mkdir -p` and never `git init`ed it, so the ONE real end-to-end deploy test always resolved `unknown` and asserted `deploydemo:runtime-unknown` — it exercised only the degraded fallback, never the version-scoped image identity `<repo>:<stage>-<version>`. `setup_file` now commits + tags the fixture (`v1.4.2`) before reading the stamp, a new assertion pins the identity (version == the tag, image ref ends in `:runtime-v1.4.2`, never `unknown`), and the up test `docker image inspect`s the loaded reference so the versioned identity is asserted end to end rather than only in the emitted compose. The fixture basename is now run-unique — it is what becomes both the image name (`detect_image_name @basename`) and the container name — so a container leaked by a crashed earlier run can no longer satisfy or break the `docker ps --filter name=` assertions; `setup_file`/`teardown_file` additionally `docker rm -f` that exact name, and the shared same-path mount `/tmp/base-deploy-e2e` (which accumulated fixture dirs forever, since `teardown_file` removes only the current run's `TMP_ROOT`) is pruned age-bounded so a concurrent run is never touched. Test-only: no deploy behaviour changed. `just test system` verified green twice consecutively with no manual cleanup between runs.

## [v0.42.0-rc3] - 2026-07-08

### Added
- **generic single-service watchdog: supervised restart with a pluggable health check (closes #797, ADR-00000020)** — a third `[lifecycle]` capability base owns, sibling to restart (#478) and init (#792): a health-check-driven watchdog for the container's one service. It is GENERIC — the app supplies the health-check COMMAND (`watchdog_check`, whose exit status is the health signal) and base ships the supervision loop (`runtime/watchdog.sh`, a new sourced entrypoint helper next to `logging.sh`/`logrotate.sh`). EVERY knob defaults OFF: with `watchdog_check` empty (the master switch) there is no watchdog and no behavior change, so the entrypoint's `. /usr/local/lib/base/watchdog.sh` line is a safe no-op (default compose is byte-identical — the #505 golden is unaffected). After `watchdog_start_period`, the check runs every `watchdog_interval`s (bounded by `watchdog_timeout`); after `watchdog_failures` consecutive failures the `watchdog_on_fail` action fires: `restart-container` (DEFAULT, Docker-native) exits the container so the restart policy restarts it (Docker's backoff absorbs storms), or `restart-service` restarts only the in-container service in place — supervising it as a `setsid` PROCESS-GROUP LEADER and stopping/restarting the WHOLE group like `docker stop` (bounded SIGTERM → grace → SIGKILL) so the service AND every grandchild it spawned die on each restart (no orphaned subtree leaks; `init = true` reaps dead children but does not kill live orphans — the process-group kill is what prevents the leak), trapping SIGTERM to forward a graceful stop on `docker stop`, counting restarts and GIVING UP LOUDLY at `watchdog_max_restarts` — running the optional `watchdog_notify` command then falling back to exiting the container (SIGTERM to PID 1 -> tini forwards it to the service and exits, tearing the container down; the supervisor's interval waits are interruptible so a `docker stop` SIGTERM is forwarded to the service at once, never deferred until the next interval). Watchdog events always log loudly to stderr (`docker logs`), and when `[logging] local_path` is on they also write a `watchdog.log` under the log dir's `watchdog/` subdir (per-start file + stable symlink + retention, reusing `logrotate.sh`). The eight `WATCHDOG_*` knobs are registered in `schema.sh` (validators in `_tui_conf.sh`, `""` no-TUI-editor i18n opt-out), seeded commented in `setup.conf`, resolved by `_resolve_deploy_context`, and emitted YAML-quoted into each service's `environment:` by `compose_emit` only when `watchdog_check` is set. The Dockerfile COPYs `watchdog.sh` alongside `logging.sh`, and a `watchdog_copy` `dockerfile_migrate` migration propagates the sibling COPY to downstream on `just upgrade` (mirroring the #805 `logrotate_copy` migration).
- **System-level self-test that really runs the shared build worker + pushes its logic down the pyramid (closes #802)** — `build-worker.yaml` is the build-gate mechanism every repo shares, yet base only checked it *statically* (actionlint validates syntax; `build_worker_yaml_spec.bats` greps structure). It was **never actually run** in base's own CI, so a semantic break — an input that became required with no caller passing it, a broken cache change, a matrix condition that produces no jobs, a removed build step — surfaced only when some downstream ran the worker in production. Two changes close the gap. (1) The worker's extractable logic is pushed down the test pyramid (System-level logic -> Unit level, ADR-00000018): the platform -> matrix resolver (the "matrix produces no jobs" break) becomes `script/ci/build_worker/compute_matrix.sh` and the buildx cache-scope base-key derivation (with its #272 / #378 bug history) becomes `script/ci/build_worker/cache_scope.sh`, each unit-tested under `just test` (`build_worker_compute_matrix_spec.bats`, 8; `build_worker_cache_scope_spec.bats`, 4). The `compute-matrix` step and the build job's `Compute cache scope` step now fetch these scripts from base at the worker's OWN ref (`github.job_workflow_sha`, into an isolated `.worker-base`) so the logic can never drift from the worker it feeds — the same version-match pattern the #800 preflight uses — and keep only the thin GITHUB_OUTPUT plumbing. (2) The residual "actually invoke the whole workflow and really build" part becomes a **System-level, CI-only** `worker-selftest` job in `self-test.yaml`: it invokes the worker end-to-end via a local reusable-workflow call (`uses: ./.github/workflows/build-worker.yaml`) against a minimal fixture (`test/fixtures/build-worker/Dockerfile` — trivial alpine no-op stages that build in seconds; the point is to exercise the ORCHESTRATION, not build a real image), so preflight + matrix + the four-stage build all run for real and deliberately breaking the worker turns the job red. It is gated on `system_relevant` (any `.github/workflows/**` change re-runs it), joins the `ci-rollup` aggregator (SKIPPED-tolerant) and the `release` gate, so it is **required before a tag**, consistent with the existing jobs. Positioning: this is the **System** level (does the worker itself build), distinct from #800's **Acceptance**-level preflight contract test (does the consumer get told what to supply) and from the `system` job (base's own image lifecycle). Structural wiring locked by `build_worker_yaml_spec.bats` (+6) and `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (+5); `doc/test/system.md` + `unit.md` + `TEST.md` synced. Follow-up (backlog, not here): a post-tag canary rollout — bump one canary repo first, fan out the rest once it is green — a deployment-process safeguard, not a test level.
- **per-start container log files + stable symlink + configurable retention, glog-style (closes #805, ADR-00000021)** — the `[logging] local_path` container-log tee (`runtime/logging.sh`) previously wrote a single per-service file and TRUNCATED it on every container start (`: > $LOG_FILE_PATH`), so run history was wiped and the file grew unbounded within a run. It now writes a per-start real file `log/<svc>/<svc>_<ts>.log` and repoints a stable symlink `log/<svc>/<svc>.log` (= the emitted `LOG_FILE_PATH`) at it (glog-style: `tail <svc>.log` always shows the current run; earlier runs stay on disk; `docker logs` parity unchanged). Old per-start files are pruned by keep-count AND age (stricter wins), never the symlink. Two new `[logging]` keys, `container_log_keep` (default 20) and `container_log_days` (default 14), are registered in `schema.sh` with positive-integer validators (+ `""` no-TUI-editor i18n opt-out) and seeded in `setup.conf`, exactly mirroring `wrapper_transcript_keep` / `_days`; a non-positive hand-edit clamps back to the default so prune can never wipe every log. The "repoint stable symlink + prune (count+age, skip symlink)" logic is extracted into one shared `runtime/logrotate.sh` helper (`_logrotate_repoint` + `_logrotate_prune`, parameterized on symlink name / dir / keep / days) used by BOTH the wrapper transcript (`transcript.sh`'s `_transcript_prune` + `latest.log` repoint now delegate to it) and the container-log tee — no duplicated retention logic. Because the tee runs in-container while the retention values live in host-side `setup.conf`, `compose_emit` reads the two keys from the conf layer and emits them as `CONTAINER_LOG_KEEP` / `CONTAINER_LOG_DAYS` env alongside `LOG_FILE_PATH`; `logging.sh` reads the env with the same fallback + clamp. The shared helper is COPY'd into the image alongside `logging.sh` (both sources guard the load defensively so the flattened `/lint` stage degrades instead of aborting); a `logrotate_copy` `dockerfile_migrate` migration adds the sibling COPY to downstream Dockerfiles on `just upgrade`. Scope note: this changes only HOW container logs are stored — whether `local_path` defaults ON stays a separate decision, still opt-in (empty) here. New bats: `logrotate_spec` (shared helper repoint/prune, count+age, skip-symlink), rewritten `entrypoint_logging_spec` (per-start file + symlink + second-start-retains-prior + retention honors keep/days + non-positive clamp + degrade branches), `compose_logging_spec` (retention env emission + default/override), `schema_spec` (new validators), and `dockerfile_migrate_spec` (the `logrotate_copy` migration); `template_spec` pins the Dockerfile COPY. `TEST.md` synced.
- **`cache_backend` option gives the shared build worker a registry-cache "bigger fridge" (closes #801)** — the reusable `build-worker.yaml` hardwired its buildx cache to `type=gha`, which has a hard 10 GB per-repo ceiling. Source-heavy repos exceed it, so GHA evicts entries (LRU) while they are still being written and the next run recompiles cold — measured on realsense_ros2, where adding cache scopes (`cache_variant`) made it *worse*: it is a capacity problem, not a scope problem. A new `cache_backend` input (`gha` | `registry`, **default `gha`**) lets a caller opt in to storing the buildx cache in the GHCR registry (`type=registry,ref=ghcr.io/<repo>/buildcache:<scope>`), which has no 10 GB ceiling, so the second run genuinely hits. cache-from / cache-to on all four build-push steps (devel-test / devel / runtime-test / runtime) become a `cache_backend`-selected ternary whose gha branch emits the byte-for-byte identical `type=gha,scope=...` string, so every existing caller is unchanged; the registry path adds a `docker/login-action` step gated on `cache_backend == 'registry'`. Selecting `registry` requires the calling job to grant `packages: write`, now enforced up front by the #800 preflight: the engine gained an optional conditional-manifest field (`<condvar>=<value>`) so the packages requirement is gated on `cache_backend == registry` (gha callers need no packages permission and are unaffected), and the build worker's probe verifies a REAL packages: write — a GHCR blob-upload scope check, not a bare login (a read-only token can still log in). A caller that forgot the permission fails within seconds with the fix instead of 403-ing deep in a ~20-minute build. Tested at unit (`ci_preflight_spec.bats` conditional-requirement cases; `build_worker_yaml_spec.bats` cache_backend input + both ternary branches + guarded login; `worker_preflight_yaml_spec.bats` cache_backend export + real write probe), and integration against the shipped manifest (`ci_preflight_contract_spec.bats`: gha-default passes without packages, registry-without-packages fails, registry-with-packages passes); `TEST.md` synced. The `extra_stages` buildx loop honors `cache_backend` too (shell-side backend selection with the same registry/gha shapes as the four standard steps), so a caller combining `cache_backend: registry` with `extra_stages` does not silently gha-cache those stages. The engine's conditional-manifest guard fails loud (config error, exit 2) on a malformed guard field lacking `=`, rather than failing open. Follow-up: an evaluation issue on realsense_ros1 / realsense_ros2 to decide whether to switch backends.
- **`[lifecycle] init` toggle — Docker init / PID1 reaper, default ON (closes #792)** — a new `[lifecycle] init` key makes `compose_emit.sh` emit `init: true` on the service, running Docker's daemon init (`docker-init` = tini) as PID 1 to reap zombies and forward signals. This is solution 1 (compose-level), consistent with base's `just` → `docker compose` model and mirroring `[lifecycle] restart` (#478); solution 2 (baking tini as the Dockerfile ENTRYPOINT) is rejected (see the #792 decision comment), and no tini is baked into any image. **Default ON** is a deliberate, principled exception to "lifecycle knobs default off", and is the concrete example of the "default ON when enabling is transparent to a correct workload AND absence is a footgun" rule in ADR-00000020 (base owns the single-service lifecycle): init is transparent to a correct single-service workload, while running as PID 1 without reaping / signal forwarding is a footgun (`stop` hangs until SIGKILL; orphaned children accumulate as zombies), so the safe default is ON — a low-risk behavior change applied on the next compose regeneration. "Default ON" is enforced at both layers: the seeded `setup.conf` ships `init = true`, and the code-level key-absent fallback in `_resolve_deploy_context` (`deploy.sh`) resolves to `true`, so an existing downstream that regenerates (or a conf missing the key) also gets it. The value threads through `setup_cmd.sh` as `generate_compose_yaml`'s 30th positional arg; the devel service and every per-stage standalone block emit `init: true` (stages that `extends: devel` inherit it), and an explicit `init = false` omits the field. `schema.sh` registers `[lifecycle.init]` with a boolean `_validate_init` (true/false) validator (+ a `""` no-TUI-editor i18n opt-out; toggled via `setup.sh set lifecycle.init false`); the emitter re-validates as an apply-time trust-boundary guard. **Caveat** (documented in the README + seeded `setup.conf`): tini forwards signals only to its direct child (PID 2 = the entrypoint), so an entrypoint that supervises children must still `trap` + forward — reaping, however, is comprehensive. New bats: `_emit_init_line` unit (default/true → `init: true`, false omits, non-boolean dropped), `_validate_init` direct + `_schema_validate` accept/reject, and a `setup` → `compose` round-trip (default-on, false-off, template ships `init = true`, `set` rejects non-boolean); the `#505` golden master gains the devel + standalone `init: true` lines. `TEST.md` synced. (The unused, never-wired `tini` in some app repos' `runtime-base` stage becomes removable — a per-repo follow-up, out of scope here.)
- **reusable build/release workers self-validate the caller contract before doing any real work (closes #800)** — a downstream repo's CI is a thin caller that hands work to base's shared `build-worker.yaml` / `release-worker.yaml`, which need the caller's `main.yaml` to supply required inputs (`image_name` / `archive_name_prefix`) and permissions (a future registry-cache backend needs `packages: write`). When a caller omitted one, nothing surfaced it up front: CI either 403'd at the final push after ~20 minutes or silently skipped a step and stayed green — the same drift class as the v0.41 Dockerfile drift. The workers now run a preflight step BEFORE any build/archive work that never fails silently. The logic is pushed down into a pure-shell validator `script/ci/preflight.sh` that reads a declared requirement manifest (`script/ci/preflight/build.manifest` / `release.manifest` — the explicit, self-describing list of what a caller must provide) and checks each entry against an env var the worker exports from the real inputs / permission probes; any unmet item produces a plain-language, early, non-zero failure naming exactly what to add to `main.yaml`, and every gap is reported in one pass. Adding a future requirement is a single new manifest line — the preflight iterates the list, so the check follows automatically and the manifest doubles as the worker's contract documentation (`preflight.sh --list`). The GHA wiring stays thin: a `preflight` job the real build/release job gates on (`needs:` includes it), which fetches the validator + manifest from base at the worker's OWN ref (`github.job_workflow_sha`) so the validator can never drift from the worker it guards, probes GHCR login for the packages capability (paving the way for the registry-cache backend's `packages: write`), and calls `preflight.sh`. Tested at unit (`ci_preflight_spec.bats`, 13 — incl. malformed-manifest guards: an unknown requirement kind or a zero-requirement manifest is a loud config error, never a silent green), integration against the shipped manifests with a deliberately-incomplete fake caller (`ci_preflight_contract_spec.bats`, 6), and structural YAML-wiring (`worker_preflight_yaml_spec.bats`, 10) levels; `TEST.md` synced. This is purely worker-side self-validation — `main.yaml` stays thin and no re-sync/rewrite machinery is added.
- **keep `host` as the network default, ship `bridge` as a usable opt-in (closes #794, ADR-00000019)** — issue #794 originally proposed flipping the `[network]` default to `bridge` / `ipc = private` on least-privilege grounds; empirical testing (`RESULTS.md`) reversed that. Under bridge, **cross-machine ROS (both ROS 1 and ROS 2) fails unfixably** — the `172.17.x` docker network is not routable off-box, so a multi-machine robot's safety scanner / LiDAR goes silently unreachable while CI stays green. The risk asymmetry (host-default on an app container loses only a defence-in-depth layer; bridge-default on a cross-machine robot is a silent, catastrophic, safety-relevant failure) dictates the default must fail safe toward `host`, and tightening is opt-in. **Nothing is flipped**: the seeded `setup.conf` and the code-level key-absent fallbacks (`deploy.sh`, `compose_emit.sh`) all stay `host`. To make the opt-in *usable* rather than a broken trap, `compose_emit.sh` now injects `hostname: <host>` on the service when the GUI is enabled AND `network.mode = bridge` (shared `_emit_hostname_line` helper, gated on effective `gui && bridge`, host name resolved once from `HOSTNAME` with a `uname -n` fallback and threaded to both the devel service and every per-stage standalone block) — under bridge the container would otherwise get a random hostname and break the local X11 MIT-MAGIC-COOKIE (keyed to the host's hostname). Docs: a README "Network mode" subsection + a `setup.conf [network]` header note carry the opt-in recipe (`setup.sh set network.mode bridge && setup.sh set network.ipc private && setup.sh apply`) with a hard cross-machine-ROS-must-stay-host warning, and a commented `port_1` example is seeded (ports only take effect under bridge). New `compose_emit/hostname_spec.bats` (5: both emit paths + the effective-flag gate); the `#505` golden master pins `HOSTNAME` and gains the devel `hostname:` line. Deferred (need physical display hardware): verifying a GUI window actually renders under bridge, the real `just run` GUI path end-to-end, and the `--hostname` side-effect on ROS node naming.

## [v0.42.0-rc2] - 2026-07-01

### Added
- **`just` help coverage: a `help` recipe per namespace + a `--help` shim for `base update` (closes #789)** — help was uneven across the layered `just` command model (ADR-00000011): `just <ns> --help` errored cryptically (a dashed name cannot be a `just` recipe/alias, so the entry cannot intercept it), and `base update` hardcoded `upgrade.sh --check` with no `*args`, so `just base update --help` errored instead of showing usage. Within `just`'s module limits (the hierarchical `just docker build` UX is kept; switching to flat `docker-build` recipes was rejected as a breaking downstream change), this closes the gaps: (1) each shipped namespace module (`docker` / `base` / `template`) gains a `help` recipe (+ an `h` alias) so `just <ns> help` / `just <ns> h` list the namespace — bare `just <ns>` still lists via `default`, and with a `help` recipe present the dashed `just <ns> --help` now emits a `Did you mean 'help'?` hint instead of a bare error; (2) `base update` takes `*args` purely as a `--help` shim — it still always runs the check (never upgrades), but `-h|--help` is forwarded to `upgrade.sh --help` (usage) without triggering the network check. README documents the namespace-vs-recipe help contract (namespace help = `just <ns>` or `just <ns> help`; recipe help = `just <ns> <recipe> --help`; dashed `just <ns> --help` is a documented `just` dispatch limitation) and ADR-00000011 is amended to reconcile its earlier "`--help` for every recipe and namespace" claim with this actual contract. Static assertions in `justfile_spec.bats` (+2: the `help`/`h` recipe per module, the `base update` shim) and executable parity in `justfile_user_spec.bats` (+7: `update --help`/`-h` reach usage not the check, `docker`/`base`/`template` `help`+`h` listing, the dashed-`--help` hint, `template new --help` recipe usage). `TEST.md` synced.
- **per-repo base version monitor: a generated workflow that opens an upgrade-reminder issue when `base` ships a newer release (closes #777)** — there was no automated signal that a downstream repo (or the template) had fallen behind the upstream `base` release; upgrades relied on a human noticing. Because `bootstrap.sh` deletes the template's `.github/` and downstream workflows are generated by `init.sh`, "ship a workflow in the template" does not propagate — the reliable channel is the `.base` subtree plus `init.sh`. This adds a pull-based monitor: a new subtree-shipped script `dist/script/base/check-base-version.sh` reads the pinned `.version`, queries `ycpss91255-docker/base`'s `releases/latest` (so RC / prerelease tags are skipped), compares semver numerically (`0.10.0 > 0.9.7`, not lexically), and — when behind — opens a single tracking issue in the repo running it, deduped by the `base-upgrade` label + target version (a newer target opens a fresh issue). It files into its own repo with the default `GITHUB_TOKEN`, so no PAT and no central repo list are needed. `init.sh` gains an idempotent `_sync_base_monitor_workflow` (never overwrites a hand-tuned file, mirroring `_create_hook_stubs`) called from both the new-repo path (`_create_new_repo`) and the existing-repo path (`_init_existing_repo`); existing repos pick the workflow up on their next upgrade (`upgrade.sh` Step 3 re-runs `init.sh`).
