The generated `.github/workflows/base-version-monitor.yaml` is a thin weekly (`cron` Mon) + `workflow_dispatch` scheduler granting `issues: write` and deferring all logic to the subtree script, so the comparison logic refreshes automatically on every upgrade. Unit-tested in `base_version_monitor_spec.bats` (12: semver-compare boundaries + the stubbed-`gh` run flow — behind opens an issue / up-to-date no-ops / existing dup skips / empty API fails closed) and `init_spec.bats` (+7: the sync function, idempotency, and both call-site wirings); `init_new_repo_spec.bats` (+1) asserts the end-to-end new-repo generation. `TEST.md` synced.

### Changed
- **formalize the Acceptance test level: rename the `integration-e2e` CI job to `acceptance` + document the System-vs-Acceptance split (#785, ADR-00000018)** — per the ISTQB taxonomy the self-test job that scaffolds a synthesized downstream repo (via `init.sh`) and drives the delivered framework + UX (`just docker` / `just base` / `just template` verbs, help, completion, generated layout) is **Acceptance** (consumer/operator perspective, UAT + OAT), not Integration. The `.github/workflows/self-test.yaml` job `integration-e2e` is renamed to `acceptance` (job id + display name `Acceptance (<platform>)`, the `ci-rollup` `needs:` / result env var / verify-loop, and the `release` `needs:` all follow), keeping base's OWN image-lifecycle + build-gate checks classified as **System** (the `system` job / `test/bats/system/`). `doc/test/acceptance.md` is filled in (was a stub): it documents that the Acceptance level is realized as the host-driven `acceptance` CI job -- not bats specs -- because a faithful test needs the real `init.sh`-scaffolded consumer + built image + real `just`, which the mounted-`/source` bats sandbox cannot reproduce; `test/bats/acceptance/` stays an intentional empty placeholder (count 0, not in the self-test grand total). `doc/test/system.md` + `TEST.md` gain the explicit System-vs-Acceptance cross-reference, and `just docker setup-tui` is recorded as intentionally unit-only (a real drive needs a pseudo-TTY; covered by `tui_spec`). `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` retargeted `integration-e2e` -> `acceptance` (+1: the new `just template new` end-to-end assertion). `ci-rollup` / `release` wiring is preserved (one job, renamed). Refs #780, ADR-00000018.
- **retire the `behavioural` test category: re-baseline `test/bats/` to the ISTQB level taxonomy (#782, ADR-00000018)** — the self-defined `behavioural` category was not an ISTQB term and the taxonomy mixed levels (unit / integration) with a type (smoke) and static analysis (lint); ADR-00000018 re-baselines to the ISTQB levels Unit / Integration / System / Acceptance plus the shipped Smoke type. The `behavioural` runtime-test gate spec (it drives buildx to prove the smoke gate fires) is reclassified as **System level + Regression type**: `test/bats/behavioural/` is `git mv`-renamed to `test/bats/system/` and the spec header reframed accordingly. A new reserved **Acceptance** level dir `test/bats/acceptance/` ships empty (`.gitkeep` + a README) for the consumer-framework / UX checks landing in S5 (#785). The mechanism follows the rename: `script/test/sync-doc-counts.sh` now recognizes the `unit / integration / system / acceptance / smoke` set and tolerates empty level dirs (count 0, no error); the previously-referenced-but-absent `script/test/check_test_md_drift.sh` validator is implemented as the read-only twin of the generator (re-derives every count via the SAME `grep -c '^@test'` source against a temp copy, non-zero exit on drift) and wired as `just test sync-docs-check`; the test runner renames its category surface (`test.sh --behavioural` -> `--system`, `_run_behavioural` -> `_run_system`, the `template-behavioural` buildx builder -> `template-system`, the `ci-behavioural` compose service -> `ci-system`, `just test behavioural` -> `just test system`); and `self-test.yaml` renames the `behavioural` job to `system` (System Regression Test) and the `classify` output `behavioural_relevant` -> `system_relevant`, keeping the `ci-rollup` / `release` wiring intact. `doc/test/` catalogs follow: `behavioural.md` -> `system.md`, a new `acceptance.md`, and the `TEST.md` index relabelled to the ISTQB level / type vocabulary; counts regenerated via `just test sync-docs`. Tests updated to the new vocabulary: `doc_counts_spec.bats` (+3: system per-type total, empty-acceptance tolerance, TEST.md system+acceptance rows), new `check_test_md_drift_spec.bats` (3: in-sync pass, drift fail, empty-dir tolerance), `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` / `ci_spec.bats` / `base_docker_namespace_spec.bats` renamed `behavioural` -> `system`. Smoke per-stage (S3) and the Acceptance content (S5) are tracked separately. Refs #780, ADR-00000018, ADR-00000012.
- **drop `python3` from the base example `devel-base` apt list (closes #771)** — the example Dockerfile (`dist/dockerfile/Dockerfile`) installed `python3` in the `devel-base` stage, but nothing in base depends on it: there are no `.py` files and no `python3` invocation in any base script / entrypoint / runtime / config (the only other `python3` mentions are the commented-out ROS `python3-dev` / `python3-colcon-common-extensions` example lines, and the generic `assert_pip_pkg` smoke helper that downstream repos opt into with their own pip). base should ship only the standardized necessities, so the unused interpreter is removed; the deliberate shell-experience tooling (`vim` / `tmux` / `terminator` / `git` / `curl` / `wget` / `sudo` / `ca-certificates`) stays. This is a template change: it affects newly-created repos (via `/new-repo`) and base's own example / e2e build; existing hand-written downstream Dockerfile copies own their copy and are unaffected.
- **finish the setup.sh-derived spec re-split: one spec per leaf lib, retire the god-files (P1b, closes #758, ADR-00000015)** — final, behaviour-identical relocation; every moved `@test` is byte-identical (same name / body / assertions) and the unit grand total is unchanged. Per ADR-00000015 (test files mirror source; isolated unit tests move to the leaf lib's spec; integration tests stay with the orchestrator), the two remaining god-files `setup_spec.bats` (147) and `setup_emit_spec.bats` (72) are split into new flat specs mirroring `lib/`: `resolve_spec.bats` (25 — `_resolve_*` / `_detect_jetson` / `_detect_dri_groups` incl. the #760 `SETUP_DETECT_*` operator-override contract / `_compute_conf_hash`), `setup_detect_spec.bats` (49 — `detect_*` / `_is_ssh_x11` / `_setup_ssh_x11_cookie` / `detect_image_name` / `detect_ws_path` / `_reconcile_workspace_path`), `setup_conf_spec.bats` (12 — `_load_setup_conf` / `_get_conf_*` / `_rule_basename`), `env_emit_spec.bats` (4 — `write_env` / `_scaffold_env_overlay`), and `drift_spec.bats` (4 — `_check_setup_drift`); the residual `_yaml_dq` test (compose_emit-owned) moves into the existing `compose_emit/blocks_spec.bats` (27 -> 28). The remaining isolated unit tests are returned to their owning lib's spec too: `_parse_ini_section` / `_ini_tokenize` -> `conf_accessor_spec.bats` (16 -> 27, both live in `lib/conf.sh`), `_setup_known_section` -> `setup_cmd_spec.bats` (103 -> 107, `lib/setup_cmd.sh`), `_setup_ssh_x11_cookie` -> `setup_detect_spec.bats` (`lib/setup_detect.sh`). `setup_emit_spec.bats` is removed (its `apply`-pipeline / CLI-flag / i18n tests fold back into the orchestrator `setup_spec.bats`, its leaf tests move out). `setup_spec.bats` is slimmed to the orchestrator (147 -> 109): `main` dispatch, `usage`, `_setup_msg` / `_msg`, and the `apply` pipeline integration tests (driving detect -> resolve -> write_env -> compose emit end-to-end; the two `apply --no-x11-cookie` cases stay here as they assert apply-pipeline state, not the cookie helper directly). Count math: the 147 + 72 = 219 god-file tests, plus the review-relocated 19 from setup_spec, redistribute net-zero across the unit dir (`setup_spec` -38, new leaf specs +98, `setup_emit` -72, `conf_accessor` +11, `setup_cmd` +4, `compose_emit/blocks` +1). `unit.md` resynced. Closes #758.
- **re-split the setup.sh-derived unit specs to mirror their source libs (P1a of #758, ADR-00000015)** — behaviour-identical test relocation, zero `@test` body changes, unit grand total unchanged. Per ADR-00000015 (test files mirror source; a lib needing multiple sub-unit specs gets a `<name>/` folder; never split source for tests; integration tests stay with the orchestrator): (1) `compose_gen_spec.bats` / `compose_emitter_spec.bats` move into a `compose_emit/` folder as `gen_spec.bats` / `blocks_spec.bats` (mirroring `lib/compose_emit.sh`, which already had two specs); (2) `setup_subcommand_spec.bats` -> `setup_cmd_spec.bats` with `setup_section_validate_spec.bats` merged in (both cover `lib/setup_cmd.sh`'s `_setup_validate_kv` / `_setup_known_section`); (3) `setup_stage_override_spec.bats` -> `stage_spec.bats`, gathering the stage.sh-owned tests that lived elsewhere -- the 12 `_resolve_docker_flags` tests from the compose spec (the `generate_compose_yaml` golden master that merely exercises it stays in `gen_spec.bats`, as it tests the generator's output) and the 6 `_generate_runtime_dockerfile` tests from `setup_emit_spec.bats`. The shard partitioner (`drivers/bats.sh`) and the doc-count generator (`sync-doc-counts.sh`) now recurse into the per-lib sub-folders (`--recursive` on bats directory targets; `globstar` + `**/*_spec.bats` globs), so foldered specs are first-class shard / coverage units; `ci_spec.bats`'s partition tests drop their non-recursive globs to match. `_yaml_dq` and the `setup_spec.bats` slim-down are left for P1b (#758 stays open). `TEST.md` / `unit.md` resynced. Refs #758.
- **legitimize `SETUP_DETECT_JETSON` / `SETUP_DETECT_DRI_GROUPS` as documented operator detection overrides (closes #760)** — the two env-var short-circuits in `lib/resolve.sh` (`_detect_jetson` reading `SETUP_DETECT_JETSON=true|false` instead of probing `/etc/nv_tegra_release`; `_detect_dri_groups` reading `SETUP_DETECT_DRI_GROUPS` instead of `stat`ing `/dev/dri/{card*,renderD*}`) were only ever set by tests -- a test-injection seam baked into production source (the lone "source driven by tests" instance surfaced in the #758 audit, ADR-00000015). Rather than remove the seam, this promotes both to first-class, documented operator overrides of host auto-detection: real use is containerised / CI hosts where the `/dev/dri` render-group GIDs differ from the build host, or simulating a Jetson on non-Jetson hardware so `deploy.gpu_runtime` / `build.network` auto-promotion applies. Behaviour is byte-identical -- only the framing changed. Documented under README "Host-detection overrides" + a new "Host-detection overrides (env vars)" block in `setup.sh --help`; `lib/resolve.sh`'s function comments now mark each var as a supported operator knob, not a test hook. The existing detection specs are relabelled to assert the documented operator-override contract (the env var forces the result and skips the host probe) and `setup_spec` gains one dedicated assertion that the override echoes the GID list verbatim (unit 146 -> 147). The same override convention is deliberately NOT extended to `detect_gpu` / `detect_gui` (which mock at the command level and have no env seam) -- scoped to the two that already exist; the pattern is noted for future symmetry rather than expanded now.
- **extract resolvers / .env generation / drift detection, finishing the setup.sh decomposition (closes #749)** — final cleanup slice of epic #745 (ADR-00000014). Moved the last three subsystems out of `setup.sh` (808 -> 368 lines) into new libs: `lib/resolve.sh` (mode+detection resolvers `_resolve_gpu` / `_resolve_gui` / `_resolve_runtime` / `_resolve_build_network`, their detection helpers `_detect_jetson` / `_detect_dri_groups`, and `_compute_conf_hash`), `lib/env_emit.sh` (.env generators `write_env` / `_scaffold_env_overlay`), and `lib/drift.sh` (`_check_setup_drift` / `_setup_check_drift` / `_announce_template_default_fallback`) -- 12 functions. `setup.sh` is now a thin orchestrator: only the `_setup_msg*` message catalog, `usage`, and `main` (subcommand dispatch) remain (5133 -> 368 lines across the whole epic). `.env` generation lands in a NEW `lib/env_emit.sh`, not the existing `lib/env.sh` (which is the runtime `.env` reader `_load_env`) -- same emission-vs-invocation split as compose_emit.sh vs compose.sh (ADR-00000014 amended). Registered `resolve` / `env_emit` / `drift` in the `_lib.sh` chain. Pure relocation; deps resolve at call-time via the load order; the caller specs pass unchanged. `template_spec`'s structural base_path-traversal grep follows the source to `lib/setup_cmd.sh`; init_spec fixture gains `resolve` / `env_emit` / `drift`. Full `just test` green (2134 bats + lint).
- **extract the multi-stage Dockerfile + per-stage override subsystem to `lib/stage.sh` (#749c)** — third cleanup slice of epic #745 (ADR-00000014). Moved the stage subsystem out of `setup.sh` (1299 -> 808 lines) into a new `lib/stage.sh`: stage-name validation (`_validate_stage_name`), Dockerfile stage parsing + content hashing (`_parse_dockerfile_stages` / `_compute_dockerfile_hash` / `_generate_runtime_dockerfile`), the override readers (`_parse_stage_sections` / `_load_stage_overrides` / `_validate_stage_override_key`), and the append/replace resolvers (`_resolve_stage_scalar` / `_resolve_stage_list` / `_resolve_docker_flags`) -- 10 functions. Registered in the `_lib.sh` chain after `schema.sh`. Pure relocation; deps (the conf accessors, resolvers, `_setup_msg`, globals in setup.sh) resolve at call-time via the load order; the caller specs (setup_stage_override / compose_emitter / compose_gen / deploy) pass unchanged. init_spec fixture gains `stage`. Full `just test` green (2134 bats + lint).
- **extract the setup.conf accessors to `lib/setup_conf.sh` (#749b)** — second cleanup slice of epic #745 (ADR-00000014). Moved the setup.conf reader layer out of `setup.sh` (1551 -> 1299 lines) into a new `lib/setup_conf.sh`: the per-section merge loader (`_load_setup_conf`), the parse-once handle model (`_setup_conf_handle` / `_setup_load_merged_full`) feeding the `_conf_get` / `_conf_list_sorted` accessors, the scalar/list getters (`_get_conf_value` / `_get_conf_list_sorted`), and the `[image]`-rule applicators (`_rule_prefix` / `_rule_suffix` / `_rule_basename`) used by `detect_image_name` -- 8 functions. Registered in the `_lib.sh` chain after `conf.sh` (the low-level `_parse_ini_section` + handle accessors live there; this is the setup.conf-path-resolving layer above them). Pure relocation; deps (`_SETUP_SCRIPT_DIR`, `_parse_ini_section`, the conf.sh accessors) resolve at call-time via the load order; the caller specs (deploy / setup / setup_emit / setup_subcommand / tui) pass unchanged. init_spec fixture gains `setup_conf`. Full `just test` green (2134 bats + lint).
- **extract host detection + name/path resolution to `lib/setup_detect.sh` (#749a)** — first cleanup slice of epic #745 (ADR-00000014). Moved the `detect_*` host probes (user/hardware/docker-hub-user/GPU/GUI/SSH-X11) + name/path resolution (`detect_image_name`, `detect_ws_path`, `_reconcile_workspace_path`, 9 functions) out of `setup.sh` (1949 -> 1551 lines) into a new `lib/setup_detect.sh`, registered in the `_lib.sh` chain. Pure relocation; specs pass unchanged (deps resolve at call-time); init_spec fixture gains `setup_detect`. Full `just test` green.
- **extract the `setup` subcommands from setup.sh into `lib/setup_cmd.sh` (#748)** — third slice of the setup.sh decomposition (epic #745, ADR-00000014). Moved the user-facing verb block (`_setup_set` / `_setup_show` / `_setup_list` / `_setup_add` / `_setup_remove` / `_setup_reset` / `_setup_apply` / `_setup_deploy` + the `_setup_known_section` / `_setup_validate_kv` helpers -- 10 functions, ~1400 lines) out of `dist/script/docker/wrapper/setup.sh` (3348 -> 1949 lines) into a new `dist/script/docker/lib/setup_cmd.sh`, registered in the `_lib.sh` chain after config_summary; setup.sh's `main` dispatches into it. Pure relocation -- behaviour byte-identical; the subcommand specs (setup_subcommand / setup_section_validate / setup) pass unchanged. apply's deps (detection, resolvers, write_env, drift, the extracted compose_emit.sh / deploy.sh, conf/schema) resolve at call-time via the load order. init_spec's lib-symlink fixture gains `setup_cmd`. Full `just test` green.
- **extract compose.yaml emission from setup.sh into `lib/compose_emit.sh` (#747)** — second slice of the setup.sh decomposition (epic #745, ADR-00000014). Moved the ~1200-line compose renderer (the `_emit_*_block` / `_emit_*_line` helpers, the volume/device classifiers `_classify_volume_lhs` / `_collect_named_volumes` / `_yaml_dq`, `_emit_stage_service`, and `generate_compose_yaml` -- 23 functions) out of `dist/script/docker/wrapper/setup.sh` (4526 -> 3348 lines) into a new `dist/script/docker/lib/compose_emit.sh`, registered in the `_lib.sh` chain after deploy.sh. It lands in a NEW file (not the existing `lib/compose.sh`, which is the `docker compose` INVOCATION wrapper -- a distinct concern; ADR-00000014 amended). Pure relocation -- behaviour byte-identical; the compose specs pass unchanged; does not preempt the #716 emitter audit. init_spec's lib-symlink fixture gains `compose_emit`. Full `just test` green.
- **extract the deploy generator from setup.sh into `lib/deploy.sh` (#746)** — first slice of the setup.sh decomposition (epic #745, ADR-00000014). Moved the deploy-generator block (`_emit_docker_run_flags`, `_resolve_deploy_context`, `_generate_deploy_sh`, `_bake_config_copy`, `_generate_deploy_bundle`, `_expand_env_cross_refs`, ~600 lines) out of `dist/script/docker/wrapper/setup.sh` (5125 -> 4526 lines) into a new `dist/script/docker/lib/deploy.sh`, registered in the `_lib.sh` load chain after compose.sh. Pure relocation -- behaviour byte-identical; the 49 `deploy_spec.bats` tests pass unchanged and now correctly mirror `lib/deploy.sh` (resolves the test-layout audit's name/unit mismatch). The deploy functions' deps (`_setup_conf_handle`, resolvers, `_setup_msg`, `_SETUP_SCRIPT_DIR`) stay in setup.sh and resolve at call-time via the load order. init_spec's lib-symlink fixture gains `deploy`. Full `just test` green (2134 bats + lint). Compose / subcommands / infra-cleanup slices follow (#747/#748/#749).
- **`_resolve_deploy_context` parses the conf once instead of 10x (perf) (#742)** — it resolved the deploy config by calling `_load_setup_conf` once per section (build / deploy / gui / network / security / lifecycle / resources / environment / tmpfs / devices), and each call re-tokenized the whole setup.conf -- ~10 full parses per call, measured ~3.35s/call in plain bats (a real `just setup` / deploy-gen cost, not just a kcov artifact). Migrated it to the existing parse-once accessor: `_setup_conf_handle` (one section-replace merge) + the new `_conf_get_into` (outvar `_conf_get`, no `$()` fork) and `_conf_list_sorted`. Behaviour is byte-identical (the 17 existing deploy_spec tests are the regression net; the `[security]` template-baseline fallback, gpu_runtime legacy alias, and dri_groups auto-detect are preserved). Measured: deploy_spec.bats 61s -> 32s plain (-48%); this is the coverage partition floor, so the sharded coverage critical path drops too (ADR-00000008). Adds `_conf_get_into` to lib/conf.sh (+1 conf_accessor_spec test). Other per-section re-parse sites (the [build]/[volumes]/[security]/[additional_contexts] resolver, the volume resolver) are a tracked follow-up.
- **doc-only CI short-circuit now also covers `CONTEXT.md`** — the `classify` job's `code_changed` allow-list was `doc/** + README.md + LICENSE`, so a change to the root-level `CONTEXT.md` (tracked domain glossary, pure docs, not under `doc/`) tripped `code_changed=true` and ran the full heavy suite (shellcheck / hadolint / bats / coverage / integration-e2e / behavioural). Added `':!CONTEXT.md'` to the diff allow-list so a glossary-only PR skips them like any other docs change. `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` allow-list assertion updated.

### Fixed
- **two `just` recipe-correctness bugs: stale `test init`/`upgrade` recipes + leaky `docker start --help` (closes #779)** — (1) `script/test/justfile.test` still carried `init` / `upgrade` / `upgrade-check` recipes that shelled out to root `./init.sh` / `./upgrade.sh`, but ADR-00000011 §8 relocated those scripts to `dist/script/base/`, so all three recipes failed immediately (`No such file or directory`) if invoked. They are superseded by the `base` namespace (`just base init` / `just base upgrade [vX.Y.Z]` / `just base update`), so the three stale recipes (and their incomplete "relocate to the base namespace" comment) are removed; the two `template_spec.bats` unit tests that pinned them alive (grepping justfile.test for `./upgrade.sh {{args}}` and the `--check || [ $? -eq 1 ]` wrap) are replaced by a single guard asserting justfile.test references no vanished root `./init.sh` / `./upgrade.sh`. (2) `dist/script/docker/justfile.docker`'s composite `start` verb ran `./script/build.sh {{args}}` then `./script/run.sh` on separate lines, so `just docker start --help` forwarded `--help` to build.sh (which printed its help and exited 0) and then just ran run.sh anyway — a real container launch on a help request. `start` is now a shebang recipe that short-circuits on `-h` / `--help`, printing the composite-verb usage and exiting before either the build or run step. Two `justfile_user_spec.bats` tests assert the short-circuit prints usage and reaches neither backing script. `unit.md` / `TEST.md` counts resynced.
- **`just template new <name>` was broken end-to-end -- two consumer-facing defects the new acceptance test caught (#785)** — the recipe was only ever exercised through `new.sh`'s isolated unit tests (which `chmod +x` the script and use a bare sandbox registry), so the real `just template new` path shipped broken: (1) `dist/script/template/new.sh` was tracked `100644` (not executable), but the `template new` recipe runs `script/template/new.sh {{name}}` DIRECTLY, so every real consumer got `exit 126` (Permission denied) -- fixed by shipping the script `100755` like its wrapper siblings; (2) the registry idempotency guard used `grep -qF` (substring), which matched the COMMENTED example line `#   mod? deploy 'deploy/justfile.deploy'` that `init.sh` seeds into `script/local/justfile.local`, so `just template new deploy` (the literal documented example name) reported "already registered", appended no real `mod?` line, and left `just deploy` undispatchable -- fixed by matching a whole real registration line (`grep -qxF`). A new `acceptance` CI step drives the real recipe in the scaffolded consumer and asserts the produced artifact (scaffolded group dir + files, `__NAME__` substitution, registry entry, and that `just --list <name>` resolves + `just <name> hello` runs the generated recipe); `template_new_spec.bats` gains two unit regressions (the executable bit; a commented-example seed still registers). Refs #785, QA item 10.
- **bump `extractions/setup-just@v3 -> @v4` to clear the last node20 deprecation warning** — #737 cleared the GitHub-owned actions, but the `integration-e2e` job's `setup-just@v3` (third-party) still declared node20 and warned on both arch shards. v4 is the maintainer's current major; the usage is parameterless so it is drop-in. Finishes the node20 cleanup for base.
- **bump node20 GitHub Actions to node24 majors to clear the runner deprecation warning (#737)** — GitHub deprecated the Node 20 runtime and force-runs node20 actions on node24 (a shim that warns but still passes). Bumped the pinned versions that declared `node20` in their `action.yml`: `actions/upload-artifact@v4 -> @v7`, `actions/download-artifact@v4 -> @v8`, `actions/cache@v4 (save + restore) -> @v6` across `self-test.yaml`, `release-test-tools.yaml`, `publish-worker.yaml` (8 refs). `actions/checkout@v6` already runs node24. Note: `upload-artifact@v5` is STILL node20, so v6+ is required. `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` version assertions updated (@v4 -> @v7/@v8). The artifact upload<->download backend round-trip (coverage shards upload, coverage-gate downloads the `coverage-shard-*` artifacts) is verified by this PR's own CI run. Org-wide fan-out to other repos uses the same version set.
- **PR image jobs no longer rebuild the test-tools image on every PR (spurious build_local) (#734)** — the hadolint / bats-fragile / bats-integration / coverage / integration-e2e / behavioural Obtain steps decided "did this PR change `dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools`?" with `git diff --quiet "origin/<base>"...HEAD` on a `fetch-depth: 1` checkout. A shallow checkout has no reachable merge-base for the three-dot diff, so git reported EVERY file as changed -> `build_local=true` fired on EVERY PR, rebuilding kcov from source (~12s warm / ~88s cold per job, in parallel) instead of the ~4s pull-`:main`+probe hot path. Fix: compute the decision ONCE in the `classify` job (whose checkout is `fetch-depth: 0`, so the merge-base resolves) and expose it as a `testtools_changed` output; the six image jobs now read `needs.classify.outputs.testtools_changed` and drop their own fetch+diff. Single source of truth, six fragile shallow-diff copies removed. push/tag builds set it false and keep self-healing via the tool probe; a real Dockerfile-changing PR still rebuilds. `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` updated (+3: classify emits testtools_changed; image jobs gate on it; `git fetch origin` now appears once). Refs #697, the closed #732.

### Changed
- **coverage shards self-balance by REAL kcov runtime via a self-maintaining timings cache (#733)** — the #724 time-weighted partition had no timings source, so `_spec_weight` fell back to `@test` count; equal-count specs of unequal duration still imbalanced the matrix (measured 8 shards: slowest 271s vs fastest 92s, ~3x, on every PR's critical path). This wires the deferred source: each coverage shard runs `kcov ... bats --report-formatter junit`, and `_junit_to_timings` (new, in `bats.sh`) turns the per-file `<testsuite time=...>` entries into `coverage/timings.tsv` (`<seconds> <basename>`), uploaded in the shard artifact. The coverage-gate job merges every shard's timings via `coverage_gate.sh --merge-timings` (new) and, on main-branch pushes only, `actions/cache/save`s the result; every run `actions/cache/restore`s it to `test/bats/.shard-weights` (the in-repo path `_spec_weight` now reads by default, picked up by the in-container coverage run with no `-e` plumbing), so the next partition weights by real seconds. PR runs are read-only (PR-runner noise never poisons the shared weights); a cache miss / brand-new spec degrades to the `@test`-count fallback. This is the established best-practice shape (runtime-weighted greedy-LPT + cached durations: cf. CircleCI, Knapsack Pro, pytest-split; Graham 1969 bounds LPT makespan at `(4/3 - 1/(3m))` x optimal). Target: ~3x slowest-shard spread collapses toward even (critical path ~285s -> ~160s at 8 shards; hard floor = longest single spec FILE). `ci_spec.bats` (+3: `_junit_to_timings` emit + no-op, `_run_coverage` writes timings.tsv, default-path `_spec_weight`), `coverage_gate_spec.bats` (+2: `--merge-timings` max-dedup + empty), `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (+3: cache restore / merge / main-push-only save). ADR-00000008 amended. Refs #724, #725, #730.
- **coverage shards are time-balanced and integration is folded into the partition pool, not pinned to the last shard (#724)** — the coverage shard partition (`_shard_unit_files`) bin-packed UNIT specs by `@test` COUNT and `_run_coverage` appended ALL integration specs to the LAST shard. Two imbalances followed: (1) equal-count specs of unequal runtime mis-balanced the unit shards; (2) the last shard carried its unit slice PLUS the entire integration suite under kcov -- measured at 8 shards it was 326s (293s of kcov) while the other seven sat at 87-192s, i.e. the integration-laden last shard was the sole critical-path bottleneck. This change: (a) `_shard_unit_files` now partitions unit + integration specs together (one pool), so integration spreads across shards (each spec still kcov'd exactly once); `_run_coverage` drops the last-shard special case. (b) The partition weight moves from `@test` count to a per-spec `_spec_weight` -- recorded seconds from `SHARD_WEIGHTS_FILE` when present, else the `@test` count as a graceful fallback (so it is a no-op until a timings source is wired; the automated timings cache is a deliberate follow-up). Greedy LPT is unchanged. `ci_spec.bats` (+4: `_spec_weight` read/fallback, time-weighted partition, integration-in-pool) with the integration-on-last-shard tests rewritten to the new pooled behaviour. ADR-00000008 amended. Refs #725, #677, #615.
- **CI coverage shard count is now dynamic (`vars.CI_SHARDS`, default 8) instead of a hardcoded 4 (#725)** — the `coverage` matrix in `self-test.yaml` was frozen at `shard: ['1/4','2/4','3/4','4/4']`, so the slowest shard (which carried a quarter of the suite under kcov) gated the PR critical path at ~7 min. A new `compute-shards` job emits a JSON shard array `["1/N",...,"N/N"]` from `vars.CI_SHARDS` (default 8 when unset/non-numeric, clamped to [1,12] as a concurrency-budget backstop), and the coverage matrix consumes it via `strategy.matrix.shard: ${{ fromJSON(needs.compute-shards.outputs.shards) }}`. `_shard_unit_files` / `test.sh --coverage-shard N/T` and `coverage-gate` (downloads `coverage-shard-*`) already accept any total T, so no other change is needed. More shards = less per-shard work -> the slowest shard (critical path) drops roughly proportionally (4 -> 8 ~halves it). "Shard to runner count" is NOT runtime-detectable on GitHub-hosted (parallelism is bounded by the plan's concurrent-job limit), so a repo variable is the source of truth -- and it generalises: an operator moving base to self-hosted sets `vars.CI_SHARDS` to their runner fleet size without editing the workflow. `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` updated (+1: dynamic-matrix assertion replaces the hardcoded `1/4..4/4` one; new compute-shards-job assertion). The shard-array generator was validated for default/clamp/non-numeric/n=1 (all emit valid JSON). Refs #677, #615, ADR-00000008.

### Added
- **`just test sync-docs`: auto-generate the `doc/test/*.md` count figures from the specs (stop hand-editing) (#727)** — the per-spec `### <path> (N)` headings and the per-type / grand totals in `doc/test/{TEST,unit,integration,behavioural,smoke}.md` were maintained BY HAND on every PR; the `check_test_md_drift.sh` meta-repo hook only VALIDATES the per-heading counts (`grep -c '^@test'`) and does not write them back, and it never checks the totals -- so the totals silently drift. New generator `script/test/sync-doc-counts.sh` (`_sync_doc_counts`, run via `just test sync-docs`) makes the docs derived from the specs: it recomputes every `### <path> (N)` heading from `grep -c '^@test'` on that path, and recomputes each per-type total + the TEST.md grand total / type table / "not in the N figure" line as the `^@test` count over that type's spec glob (unit/integration/behavioural `test/bats/<type>/*_spec.bats`, smoke `dist/test/smoke/*.bats`). Idempotent; the drift hook stays the validating safety net. Unit-tested in `doc_counts_spec.bats` (3: heading recompute, per-type total, idempotency). Shipping the generator to downstream repos (which also carry hand-maintained doc/test counts) is a possible follow-up. Closes #727.

- **base self-uses the `docker` namespace it ships: `just docker build --target test-tools` builds base's tooling image via the shared wrapper (#713, ADR-00000011 sec.2/4/5)** — base had no first-class command to (re)build its `test-tools` image; it was only built as a side effect (a raw `docker build -t test-tools:local -f dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools .` one-liner in `just test behavioural`, or implicitly inside `test.sh`), which is exactly the staleness confusion #686/#697 hit. Rather than a bespoke build line, base now self-uses the very `docker` namespace consumers use (the unimplemented part of ADR-00000011 sec.2/4): base's root `justfile` gains `mod? docker`, and — since base is the template SOURCE with no `.base/` subtree and `init.sh` never runs on it — the wrapper symlinks are committed pointing straight into `dist/` (`script/docker/justfile.docker -> ../../dist/...`, flat `script/<verb>.sh -> ../dist/script/docker/wrapper/<verb>.sh`). A new `test-tools` service in base's hand-authored `compose.yaml` builds from `Dockerfile.test-tools`, so `just docker build --target test-tools` dispatches `docker compose build test-tools` -> `test-tools:local` (no new `--stage` flag; `--target` is the existing stage selector). The shared wrapper was made topology-agnostic so it works in base's `.base/`-less tree: `bootstrap.sh` resolves `_lib.sh` / `FILE_PATH` via an added `dist/script/docker/lib/...` candidate (additive — the consumer `.base/` candidates and the "neither found" error path are unchanged); `_wrapper_setup_sync` skips the whole setup-sync lifecycle when there is no `.base/` subtree AND no `setup.conf` (a self-managed, hand-authored compose — generating would clobber it; a general rule, consumers always carry `.base/` so it never fires for them); and `build.sh` / `compose.sh` degrade gracefully when `.env.generated` is absent (guard `_load_env`, default the project name to the repo dir, omit `--env-file` when missing). `just test behavioural` now builds test-tools through `just docker build --target test-tools` instead of the raw one-liner. base's `setup` / `setup-tui` verbs exist via the shared module but are safe no-ops in base (no `setup.conf` to operate on); `run` / `exec` / `stop` are consumer-shaped and not used by base. Unit-tested in `build_sh_base_self_spec.bats` (2: base-self lib resolution + `--target test-tools` compose dispatch with setup-sync skipped) and `base_docker_namespace_spec.bats` (5: root `mod? docker`, the committed symlinks resolving into `dist/` with no `.base/` hop, the `test-tools` compose service, and `just test behavioural` routing through the namespace); the `--env-file`-now-conditional change to `_compose_project` split `lib_spec`'s compose-dispatch test into present + absent cases (45 -> 46); existing `build_sh_spec` (53), `wrapper_lib_lookup_spec` (5), `wrapper_compose_dispatch_spec` (6), and `justfile_spec` (11) stay green (no regression). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2100 -> 2108: unit 2015 -> 2023). Lands in v0.42.0. Closes #713. Refs #686, #697, ADR-00000011.
### Fixed
- **coverage-gate merge is now a per-line UNION, not a SUM of root counters (was shard-count-dependent) (#730)** — `coverage_gate.sh` merged the per-shard kcov cobertura reports as `SUM(lines-covered)/SUM(lines-valid)` over each report's root `<coverage>` element. But every shard's kcov runs with `--include-path=<repo>`, so each shard's cobertura covers the WHOLE source tree, and a source file exercised by specs in multiple shards (shared libs) appears in MULTIPLE reports -- summing double-counts that shared source, so `valid` inflates faster than `covered` and the merged rate DRIFTS DOWN as the shard count grows. Latent at 4 shards (read ~52.9%, passed the 50% floor); exposed by #725's dynamic shard count, where 8 shards read **42.42%** (`15101/35596`) on the SAME suite and FALSE-FAILED the floor. Fixed: `_coverage_gate_run` now computes a per-line UNION across all reports -- a source line counts as covered if ANY shard executed it (`hits>0`); `valid` = distinct source lines keyed by `<class filename>` + `<line number>` -- so the rate is shard-count-invariant. Verified on the real 8-shard artifacts: union = **51.42%** (`8953/17413`). Self-contained awk (no XML lib, no kcov in the gate job); the now-unused root-counter parser was removed. `coverage_gate_spec.bats` (+1, 11 -> 12): fixtures gained per-line `<line>` elements (distinct filenames stay disjoint so the existing line-weighted assertions hold), plus a SHARED-source test proving two shards over the same file union to 60% instead of the double-counted 30%. Closes #730. Refs #725, #710, #615, ADR-00000008.
- **`just --list` module descriptions fixed: one clean adjacent doc comment per `mod?` (#720)** — `just --list` (bare `just`, and `just <module>`) renders a module's description from the comment lines IMMEDIATELY above its `mod?` (no blank-line gap), using only the LAST line of a contiguous block. The consumer entry `dist/script/justfile` and base's root `justfile` both violated this: `mod? docker` sat below a blank line (after the file header) so it rendered with NO description; `mod? base` sat below a multi-line paragraph whose last line was the mid-sentence fragment "the script relocation land in"; and base's root `mod? docker/test/release` were a comment-less run so all three rendered empty. Every consumer inherited the broken output via the subtree. Fixed by giving each top-level `mod?` exactly ONE clean one-line `#` doc comment directly above it (longer rationale stays in the file header, separated by a blank line so `just` does not grab it). A regression invariant is unit-tested in `justfile_spec.bats` (+2: each top-level `mod?` in the consumer entry AND base's root justfile has its line-above a `#` comment and its line-two-above NOT a comment -- i.e. a single adjacent one-liner, catching both the blank-gap-empty and multi-line-fragment defects). Note: `just --help` printing `just`'s own binary help (not the recipe list) is expected, not a defect; language-aware help is tracked separately in #718. `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2111 -> 2113: unit 2025 -> 2027; `justfile_spec` 11 -> 13). Closes #720. Refs #718.
- **`init.sh` refuses to self-run inside the base template source itself (#719)** — `init.sh` / `upgrade.sh` self-locate the subtree root by walking up to the dir carrying `.version` + `dist/` (ADR-00000011 sec.8), then scaffold/symlink into that root's PARENT. Run RAW from inside the base repo itself (`./dist/script/base/init.sh`, not via a `.base/` subtree), the walk-up resolves base's OWN root as the subtree root, so `REPO_ROOT` becomes base's PARENT directory and `init.sh` silently scaffolds a full repo there (Dockerfile, symlinks into `../base/dist/...`, compose.yaml, `.env`, ...) and exits 0 with "Done!" — a silent footgun (base itself is untouched, but the parent dir is polluted). The common entry `just base init` cannot fire in base (base's root justfile has no `base` namespace), so only the raw deep-script path was unguarded. New shared guard `lib/template_guard.sh` (`_assert_not_template_source`) refuses when the resolved subtree root carries `.git`: a vendored `.base/` subtree never carries `.git` (the consumer's `.git` lives at the repo root, OUTSIDE the subtree), whereas the base checkout/worktree has `.git` AT the subtree root (`-e` catches a `.git` dir or worktree file). The discriminator does not hardcode the subtree basename, so a renamed subtree prefix keeps working; rejected alternatives were a git-remote URL match (breaks on fork/CI/repo-rename) and a CONTEXT.md sentinel (shipped INTO `.base/`, cannot distinguish). `init.sh` sources the guard and calls it right after the walk-up, before any file is created. The integration fixtures that `cp -a /source/.` into `.base/` (which copies the source checkout's `.git`) now strip `.base/.git` so they match a genuine subtree. Unit-tested in `template_guard_spec.bats` (2: refuses with `.git`, passes without) and integration-tested in `init_new_repo_spec.bats` (+1: in-`.git` raw run refused, no scaffold). A new event `base_self_run_refused` is registered. Wiring `upgrade.sh` to the same helper (the symmetric raw-path hole) is tracked in #721; a base-root convenience bootstrap symlink (`.base/init.sh`) was considered and rejected (see ADR-00000011 sec.8). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2100 -> 2103: unit 2015 -> 2017; new `template_guard_spec` 2; integration 85 -> 86, `init_new_repo_spec` 50 -> 51). Closes #719. Refs #721, ADR-00000011.

### Changed
- **shipped-tree directory renamed `downstream/` -> `dist/`; domain glossary locked (#714)** — the `downstream/` directory name was overloaded: it described BOTH base's shipped tree AND "a downstream repo" (the repo that vendors base). The shipped tree is renamed to `dist/` (= *distribution* — the tree base distributes to downstream repos via the `.base/` subtree), so "downstream" now unambiguously means the repo. Pure path rename of the DIRECTORY only: every `downstream/...` path reference (init.sh symlink targets + the subtree-root marker check, upgrade.sh Region B config-drift paths + marker check + Step-3 init.sh call, the wrapper bootstrap candidate paths, the consumer `dist/script/justfile` entry, the `mod?`/`shellcheck source=` directives, Dockerfile COPYs, `self-test.yaml` release-archive + classify globs, the `dockerfile_migrate` lib, the `issueref` lint roots, ALL bats specs + fixtures, the per-type test catalogs, README ×4 directory trees, and the CONTEXT.md tree) moves to `dist/...`; the standalone term **downstream repo** is preserved everywhere (it now only ever means the repo). The glossary is recorded in `CONTEXT.md` (base / downstream repo + consumer synonym / `dist/` / origin / base-own tooling / tooling image (test-tools) / consumer image (devel|runtime) / template repo / `just template` / base's "Dockerfile / config" / `@<path>`). `dockerfile_migrate` gained a new **migration 0** that rewrites a consumer Dockerfile's `.base/downstream/...` lint-stage COPY sources (e.g. `COPY .base/downstream/script/docker/lib /lint/lib`) to `.base/dist/...` so the consumer build keeps resolving after the rename, and migration 4 was widened to `(downstream/|dist/)?` so a consumer Dockerfile on either historical path heals to the `dist/` target. **Consumer migration:** an existing consumer on `.base/downstream/` upgrading to this base gets `.base/dist/` after the subtree pull; its dangling `script/*.sh -> .base/downstream/...` symlinks are dropped and re-pointed at `.base/dist/...` by the init resync (`init.sh`'s `_symlink` `rm -f`s the stale link then `ln -sf`s the new one), and its Dockerfile COPY sources are healed by migration 0. Verified on a real `.base/downstream/` (v0.42.0-rc1) fixture: after the subtree pull the symlinks re-point to `.base/dist/...` and resolve, the Dockerfile heals, and `just docker build` succeeds. NOTE: the v0.42.0-rc1-shipped `upgrade.sh` dies at its Step-3 `init.sh` call after the pull (the script path it hard-codes was relocated by #654 — ADR-00000006 Region A, "no rollback", inherent to the relocation, not introduced here); recovery is re-running the relocated base tooling (`bash .base/dist/script/base/init.sh` + the Step-5 migration pass), exactly as the production v0.41.x->v0.42.0 fanout already requires. ADR-00000006 (frozen path contract now `.base/dist/...`, supersedes `.base/downstream/`) and ADR-00000011 sec.4 (origin lives in `dist/script/<ns>/`) amended. `TEST.md` section-header paths repointed `downstream/...` -> `dist/...`; `dockerfile_migrate_spec` +3 for migration 0 (grand total 2097 -> 2100: unit 2012 -> 2015). Lands in v0.42.0-rc2; the 17-consumer fanout runs after the v0.42.0 tag. Closes #714. Refs #625, #654, ADR-00000006, ADR-00000011.

## [v0.42.0-rc1] - 2026-06-25

### Fixed
- **CI: self-test Obtain steps probe the pulled `:main` for required tools and rebuild on a miss, ending the stale-`:main` race against `release-test-tools` (#697)** — a push to `main` that changes `dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools` triggers `release-test-tools.yaml` (republishes `ghcr.io/ycpss91255-docker/test-tools:main` WITH the new tools) and `self-test.yaml` (whose jobs `docker pull` that same `:main` tag in the 3-layer Obtain step) CONCURRENTLY. The self-test run pulled the STALE `:main` (pre-new-tool, e.g. pre-kcov after #686) before the republish finished, so the coverage shards fast-failed with `kcov: command not found` (~14s) — leaving the merge commit's main CI red and breaking any open PR that pulled `:main` in that window. The Obtain step's layer-2 (pull-succeeded) path now PROBES the pulled image for the tools this run needs via a single, easy-to-extend `REQUIRED_TOOLS="kcov bats shellcheck hadolint"` list (`docker run --rm test-tools:local sh -c 'command -v <tool>'`); on any miss it emits `build_local=true` so the existing buildx Build step rebuilds from `dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools`. This makes the Obtain path self-correcting against a stale / old / racing `:main` regardless of cause, while keeping layer-1 (PR touched Dockerfile -> build) and layer-3 (pull failed -> build) intact. Applied to all five `build_local`-pattern Obtain steps (`hadolint` / `bats-unit` / `bats-integration` / `coverage` / `behavioural`) since they pull the same tag and race identically. Unit-tested in `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (+4: `bats-unit` + `coverage` Obtain probe for kcov and rebuild on a miss, the `REQUIRED_TOOLS` list is extensible, and all five `build_local` Obtain steps carry the guard). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2072 -> 2076: unit 1987 -> 1991; `self_test_yaml_spec` 58 -> 62). Closes #697. Refs #686, #615.
- **compose.yaml `environment:` entries are now emitted as quoted YAML scalars; `network.mode` / `ipc` / `pid` gained schema validators + apply-time guards (#698)** — a strengthened adversarial test audit found two asymmetric unhardened sinks. (1) The `_validate_env_kv` validator accepts a free-form VALUE half (rejecting only embedded newlines, #687), but the compose `environment:` list emitted each entry UNQUOTED (`echo "      - ${_ev}"` on both the devel and per-stage standalone paths, via `_expand_env_cross_refs` which does no quoting). A validator-accepted value carrying a YAML-structural character — a colon-space (`MSG=a: b` parses as the mapping `{"MSG=a": "b"}`), a leading flow indicator (`* & [ { !`), or an inline ` #` comment — was therefore silently mis-parsed / truncated, corrupting the container environment. The Dockerfile baked-ENV sink (#688) and `deploy.sh` (`%q`) had already hardened the same accepted-value class; the compose env sink was the asymmetric, unprotected, untested one. A new `_yaml_dq` helper wraps each entry as a YAML double-quoted scalar (escaping `\` then `"`), mirroring the existing ports / cgroup / max-size quoting; both env emit sinks route through it. (2) The `SCHEMA_VALIDATOR` registry registered `network.network_name` but NOT `network.mode` / `network.ipc` / `network.pid`, so they were free-form default-accept — arbitrary values flowed into `.env` (`NETWORK_MODE=…`) and, on the per-stage standalone path, into a LITERAL `network_mode:` / `ipc:` / `pid:` line in compose.yaml. Added enum validators (`_validate_network_mode` host|bridge|none|container:*, `_validate_ipc_mode` host|private|shareable|none|container:*, `_validate_pid_mode` host|private|container:*) registered with their existing `SCHEMA_I18N` prompt keys, plus apply-time guards on the standalone emit path that drop a bogus hand-edited override back to the env-var ref (mirroring `_emit_restart_line`). Unit-tested in `schema_spec.bats` (validator accept/reject/empty-clear) + `setup_emit_spec.bats` (`_yaml_dq` escape ordering, +1) + `setup_section_validate_spec.bats` (env quoting for colon-space / flow-indicator / inline-comment / quote-backslash + bogus-mode literal suppression, +6); existing `compose_gen_spec` / `compose_emitter_spec` golden assertions retargeted to the quoted env form. `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2055 -> 2062: unit 1970 -> 1977). Closes #698. Refs #687, #688.
- **conf.sh INI writers + upgrade.sh rollback: destructive error paths guarded (#700)** — a strengthened adversarial test-audit round found two verified destructive-failure modes. (1) `_upsert_conf_value` ran `_tmp="$(mktemp "${_file}.XXXXXX")"` with the result UNCHECKED; if `mktemp` fails (read-only dir / no inodes) `_tmp` is empty, the per-line `>> ""` appends no-op, and the final `mv "" "${_file}"` could clobber/truncate the user's `setup.conf`. The writer now verifies `mktemp` succeeded (non-empty + file exists) and on failure emits `conf_upsert_tmp_failed` + returns 1 WITHOUT touching the original file. (2) `_write_setup_conf` did not use a temp file at all: it truncated the destination in place with `: > "${_dst}"` then appended line-by-line, so any append failing after the truncate left `setup.conf` truncated with no rollback. It now writes to a sibling temp file and atomically `mv`s it over the destination only after the full rewrite succeeds (guarded `mktemp` -> `conf_write_tmp_failed` on failure, destination left unchanged). (3) `_rollback_subtree_pull` ran `git reset --hard "${_pre_head}" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true` then unconditionally reported `repo restored to pre-upgrade state` — the `|| true` swallowed a FAILED reset (bogus / gc'd / empty pre_head) yet still claimed the repo was restored over a still-broken tree. The rollback now checks the reset's exit status and, on failure, emits a distinct `rollback FAILED -- manual recovery required` message (`upgrade_rollback_failed`) and exits non-zero instead of the false 'restored' line. Unit-tested in `conf_accessor_spec.bats` (+2: each writer leaves the original file intact when its temp file cannot be created) and `upgrade_spec.bats` (+1: a failed reset is surfaced, not masked). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2055 -> 2058: unit 1970 -> 1973). Closes #700. Refs #625, #687.

- **bare-`read` EOF set-e crash fixed in the two unfixed #700 siblings + conf.sh writer atomic-`mv` failure now guarded (#702)** — a final review after the #698-#700 round found TWO siblings of the #700 bare-`read` EOF class still unfixed and untested, plus a `mv`-failure gap the #700 temp+atomic-mv rework left open. (1) `prune.sh`'s `--volumes` confirm prompt (the more-destructive sibling of the #699-fixed worktree-orphans prompt) and `build.sh`'s `--reset-conf` confirm prompt both ran a bare `read -r _reply` under `set -euo pipefail`: on closed/non-tty stdin (`prune --volumes </dev/null`, a CI pipe) `read` returns non-zero at EOF and `set -e` aborted ON the read line BEFORE the `case` could map an empty reply to a clean abort, so the user got no `Aborted volume prune` / `[build] aborted.` diagnostic and a confusing exit code. Both now use `read -r _reply || _reply=""` (matching the #700 prune-image/worktree fix), so EOF maps to an empty reply the default `case` treats as an explicit (safe) abort with the intended message. (2) `_write_setup_conf` and `_upsert_conf_value` ended with a bare `mv "${_tmp}" "${_file}"` whose failure (e.g. destination on a read-only mount) left the `setup.conf.XXXXXX` orphan temp behind with no actionable error — contradicting the comment that claimed a failed mv was surfaced. Both now `mv … || { rm -f "${_tmp}"; _log_err conf <event>; return 1; }`, removing the orphan temp, logging the new `conf_write_mv_failed` / `conf_upsert_mv_failed` events, and leaving the original file untouched. EOF specs added for `--volumes` (`prune_sh_spec.bats`, +1) and `--reset-conf` (`build_sh_spec.bats`, +1); mv-failure specs added for both writers (`conf_accessor_spec.bats`, +2, `COVERAGE=1`-skipped as kcov-fragile mv stubs). The stale `prune_sh_spec.bats` comment that falsely claimed the volumes prompt already proved the EOF pattern is corrected. `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2068 -> 2072: unit 1983 -> 1987; `prune_sh_spec` 40 -> 41, `build_sh_spec` 52 -> 53, `conf_accessor_spec` 13 -> 15). Closes #702. Refs #700, #699, #625.

### Changed
- **coverage gate is now self-hosted (kcov-total merge via `ci-rollup`); Codecov removed for GitLab portability + no external-SaaS data leakage (#710)** — this repo is moving to the company GitLab where Codecov is unavailable and uploading coverage to an external SaaS leaks data; separately #709 found `codecov/project` is a Pro-only status, so the #615/ADR-00000008 project gate never enforced on the free plan. The `codecov/codecov-action` upload step, the `CODECOV_TOKEN` usage, the no-op `flags: coverage-shard-N`, the `.codecov.yaml` config, and the `codecov` badge (4-locale READMEs) are all removed — no coverage data leaves CI. Replaced by a CI-agnostic floor gate, `script/test/drivers/coverage_gate.sh`: each coverage shard now uploads its kcov report (HTML + cobertura) as a CI artifact (`actions/upload-artifact`, keyed by `strategy.job-index`); a new `coverage-gate` job downloads every shard artifact (`actions/download-artifact`, `pattern: coverage-shard-*`) and MERGES the per-shard cobertura reports into one line-weighted project rate (`SUM(covered)/SUM(valid)` across shards — NOT the average of the per-shard rates, which would mis-weight the unequal denominators since integration runs on the last shard only) and exits non-zero when it is below `COVERAGE_MIN`. `COVERAGE_MIN` defaults to **50** (percent, env-overridable), set just below the current measured ~52.9% so it does not false-fail today and is meant to ratchet up; v2 (regression-vs-main-baseline, the full #615 intent) is a documented follow-up, not built here. `coverage-gate` joins `ci-rollup`'s `needs:`, so a sub-floor rate blocks merge with NO branch-protection change. Visibility without SaaS: kcov HTML + cobertura are kept and the gate appends a coverage summary table to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`; the GitLab cobertura/`coverage:`-regex mapping is documented in ADR-00000008 so the move is mechanical (GitHub Pages HTML publish noted as a follow-up). The gate is unit-tested in `coverage_gate_spec.bats` (+11: floor pass/fail, the sum-not-average merge math with unequal denominators, missing/empty/malformed report handling, the default-floor non-false-fail, and the step-summary emit); `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (+2: each shard uploads a CI artifact + no codecov reference anywhere + the `coverage-gate` job runs the gate); `lint_bare_stderr_spec.bats` (+1: the standalone log.sh-free `coverage_gate.sh` is excluded from the bare-stderr lint, mirroring the bootstrap pre-sourcing allowlist). ADR-00000008 amended; `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2083 -> 2097: unit 1998 -> 2012; `self_test_yaml_spec` 63 -> 65; `lint_bare_stderr_spec` 5 -> 6; new `coverage_gate_spec` 11). Closes #710; resolves #709; relates #678, #615, #677, #686.
- **test docs split by type: `doc/test/TEST.md` is now a thin index, per-type spec catalogs live in sibling files (#695)** — the single ~2000-line `TEST.md` mixed the grand-total line plus per-spec sections for ALL test types into one constant merge-conflict hotspot (every test PR edited the same total line). It is now an INDEX carrying the self-test grand total (`2083` = 1998 unit + 85 integration) plus a one-line description and relative link per type; the per-spec sections moved into `doc/test/unit.md` (1998), `doc/test/integration.md` (85), `doc/test/behavioural.md` (5, previously only inline), and `doc/test/smoke.md` (38, previously only inline). Each per-type doc carries its OWN count header; the index carries the grand total. Behavioural + smoke are now first-class documented but stay OUT of the self-test grand total (behavioural needs host docker / is opt-in; smoke specs are Dockerfile `test`-stage build-time assertions) — matching the prior counting scope. Recomputing every section count from `grep -c '^@test'` surfaced FIVE previously-undocumented unit specs (`conf_logging_spec` 6, `hook_spec` 8, `runtime_smoke_spec` 8, `tui_mount_assembler_spec` 9, `wrapper_lib_lookup_spec` 5 = 36) whose absence made the old header's `1998 unit` overstate the documented total by 36; all five are now documented so `unit.md` actually sums to 1998. The per-file `### <path> (N)` section-header format is preserved verbatim across the split so the existing parsers keep matching the headings. README (4 locales) test links + directory trees repointed; no dead links. The TEST.md drift check lives in the docker_harness meta repo (external to this repo) and currently scans `doc/test/TEST.md` only — it must be updated there to scan `doc/test/*.md` to keep the count-vs-`^@test` guard live across the split (the headings it parses are unchanged). Closes #695. Refs #625, #686, #677.

### Tests
- **`prune.sh --worktree-orphans` interactive confirmation gate covered + a `read` EOF set-e crash fixed (#699)** — the strengthened adversarial test audit found the destructive `docker rmi` loop in `_run_worktree_orphans_prune` was gated behind an interactive `read -r _reply` prompt that only fires when neither `-y` nor `--dry-run` is given, yet every existing worktree-orphans spec passed `-y`/`--dry-run`, so that prompt branch was NEVER exercised — unlike the sibling `--volumes` prompt, which has both an abort-on-`n` and a `-y`-skip test. Added three `prune_sh_spec.bats` cases mirroring the volumes pair for the MORE destructive image removal: (1) piped `y` confirms and the candidate lands in `DOCKER_RMI_LOG`; (2) piped `n` aborts with exit-1 + "aborted by user" and leaves `DOCKER_RMI_LOG` empty; (3) closed stdin (`</dev/null`, no `-y`). Case (3) was RED — a real bug: under `set -euo pipefail`, `read` returns non-zero on EOF and aborted the script AT the `read` line BEFORE the `case` could map an empty reply to abort, so a piped/CI invocation died with exit 1 and NO diagnostic. **Source fix (`prune.sh`):** `read -r _reply || _reply=""` maps EOF to an empty reply, so the default `case` arm treats a closed/non-tty stdin as an explicit (safe) abort with the same "aborted by user" message. The confirm-`y` and abort-`n` paths were already correct and are now locked as regression guards. `TEST.md` synced (`prune_sh_spec` 37 -> 40; grand total 2055 -> 2058, unit 1970 -> 1973). Refs #699, #388, #625.
- **wrapper exit-code propagation + error-path coverage gaps closed (#690)** — a 3-round adversarial test-quality audit surfaced three verified holes in the docker-wrapper specs; all three turned out to be coverage gaps (no production behaviour change). (1) Every wrapper guards its main work with `_run_pre_hook <wrapper> "$@" || exit $?`, but only `hook_spec.bats` unit-tested the *helper* — no wrapper spec proved the wrapper actually aborts (and skips the docker op) on a failing pre-hook, leaving the fragile `set -e` + `||`-chain idiom unguarded against a refactor that drops/reorders it. Added a real-mode pre-hook abort test per wrapper (`run_sh_spec` / `build_sh_spec` / `exec_sh_spec` / `stop_sh_spec` / `prune_sh_spec`): a `script/hooks/pre/<wrapper>.sh` that `exit 7` makes the wrapper exit `7` AND the subsequent compose/prune verb (`up` / `build` / `exec` / `down` / `network prune`) never runs. (2) `run.sh`'s `_app_cleanup` EXIT-trap overrides the wrapper exit with a failing post-run hook's rc (`exit "${_post_rc}"`) while still tearing down; the existing test only covered the `--detach` path, so the foreground override was untested. Added a foreground (`run.sh -- <cmd>`) test where `script/hooks/post/run.sh` `exit 9` makes `run.sh` exit `9` while `compose down --remove-orphans` still runs (recorded to a file since `_app_cleanup` silences the real-mode `down` output). (3) `exec.sh` forwards the in-container command's exit code (`return "${_exec_rc}"`) but the spec's docker stub always exited 0 for non-`ps` calls, so non-zero forwarding was never exercised. Added an `_exec_rc_fixture` (mirrors `run_sh_spec`'s `_exit_code_fixture`) + tests forwarding `42` / `0` / `7`, a post-exec hook override, and a pre-exec hook abort. No source change. `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1982 -> 1994: unit 1897 -> 1909; `run_sh_spec` 63->65, `build_sh_spec` 51->52, `exec_sh_spec` 52->57, `stop_sh_spec` 26->27, `prune_sh_spec` 36->37). Closes #690. Refs #625.

### Removed
- **`--instance` / per-instance overlay machinery removed from base -- base is single-instance only (#600)** — base now operates a SINGLE container/instance per repo, mirroring `docker` (no project concept) vs `docker compose` (owns `-p`); multi-instance orchestration belongs to the compose layer. Dropped: the `--instance NAME` flag from `run.sh` / `exec.sh` / `stop.sh` (usage text in all four locales), `stop.sh`'s `-a` / `--all` multi-instance teardown and its no-instances messaging, `INSTANCE_SUFFIX` (`_compute_project_name` no longer takes an instance arg or exports the suffix), the `config/instances/<name>.{yaml,env}` overlay convention with `lib/compose.sh::_compose_project_with_overlay` + `_validate_instance_name` (#465) and `run.sh::_compose_dispatch` (all compose calls revert to the plain `_compose_project` path), the `INSTANCE_SUFFIX:-` interpolation in the setup.sh-generated `compose.yaml` `name:` / `container_name:` lines, and the `run_instance_overlay` log event. The default `just docker run` / `run.sh` compose invocation (project + container name) is byte-identical to before. Breaking for any downstream that used `run.sh --instance` (migrate to the compose layer); blast radius is low. Specs pruned/retargeted across `lib_spec` / `run_sh_spec` / `exec_sh_spec` / `stop_sh_spec` / `template_spec` / `compose_gen_spec` / `setup_spec`; `compose_overlay_spec.bats` deleted. README unaffected (no `--instance` docs). Closes #600. Refs #465.

### Added
- **input-rejection coverage for the setup.conf validators: direct `_validate_restart` tests + a shape-only numeric contract lock (#687)** — closing the remaining (pure-coverage, no behaviour change) gaps from the adversarial test-quality audit. `_validate_restart` had ZERO direct tests — it was exercised only indirectly via the `_edit_section_lifecycle` TUI happy paths, so its reject regex (`^[1-9][0-9]*$` for the N in `on-failure:N`) could silently loosen; `tui_spec.bats` gains direct tests pinning accept (`no` / `always` / `unless-stopped` / `on-failure` / `on-failure:5`) and reject (`on-failure:0`, leading-zero `on-failure:01`, empty `on-failure:`, `on-failure:-1`, `on-failure:x`, unknown bare policy, empty). The numeric validators (`_validate_port_mapping` / `_validate_gpu_count` / `_validate_log_max_file`) are deliberately shape-only (they constrain form, delegating range/bound checks to docker at `compose up` time); `schema_spec.bats` adds a test that PINS that contract — in-shape-but-out-of-range integers (`0:0`, `70000:80`, a gpu-count that overflows, a huge `max_file`) are accepted while shape violations stay rejected — so a future tightening or loosening becomes observable rather than silent. Refs #687.
- **transcript / logging error-path + edge-case test coverage, plus a transcript retention read-side guard fix (#691)** — closed five verified gaps from the adversarial test-quality audit. (1) `log.sh`'s `_log_json_escape` (the only thing keeping JSON well-formed under dirty attribute keys/values/body) had ZERO targeted coverage — every JSON test fed metachar-free values, so an escaper regression would pass them all; `log_spec.bats` (+5) now pins each escape (`"`, lone-backslash double, newline/tab/CR, substitution order) plus a live `_log_info` attr value carrying quote+backslash+tab that must stay a single well-formed JSON object. (2)–(4) `transcript_spec.bats` (+6): `_transcript_prune` keep=0 wipe-all behaviour is now pinned AND its consequence fixed — `_transcript_begin`'s read-side guard for `wrapper_transcript_keep` / `wrapper_transcript_days` was `^[0-9]+$`, which ACCEPTED `0` even though the validator (`_validate_wrapper_transcript_keep`) requires `^[1-9][0-9]*$`; a hand-edited `setup.conf` with `wrapper_transcript_keep = 0` therefore survived the never-revalidated Apply/read path and reached prune, deleting every transcript. **Source fix (`lib/transcript.sh`):** the read-side guard now mirrors the validator (`^[1-9][0-9]*$`) so an out-of-range `0` falls back to the documented default (20 / 14) instead of wiping logs. Also added: a non-zero wrapper exit is now asserted to be BOTH recorded (`transcript_complete exit_code=7`) and propagated to the caller (the EXIT handler's `return "${_rc}"`), and all three degrade-to-no-op failure branches (`tee` missing / `mkdir` fail / raw-file unwritable) are exercised to prove they WARN + return 0 + let the wrapper continue. (5) `entrypoint_logging_spec.bats` (+2): the `mkdir`-fail (`cannot create`) and `tee`-missing (`tee binary missing`) warn-and-continue branches of `runtime/logging.sh` — previously only the truncate-fail branch was tested — are now covered with root-proof triggers (parent-is-a-file / stub PATH). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1982 -> 1995: unit 1897 -> 1910). Refs #691, #625.
- **stripped transient issue refs from code comments + an `issueref` lint that blocks regressions (#576, ADR-00000013)** — the historical convention threaded inline issue numbers (`#440`, `#216 / #429`, `# #546: ...`) through code comments; an inline `#N` is a frozen pointer that rots (close / rename / supersede) while `git blame -> commit -> PR -> issue` does not. ADR-00000013 records the convention: code comments in `.sh` / `justfile*` / `compose.yaml` / `Dockerfile*` / `.bats` carry NO bare transient issue numbers, but KEEP `ADR-0000xxxx` references and all what/why prose; the accepted tradeoff is one extra `git blame` hop for comment conciseness. A new per-tool driver `script/test/drivers/issueref.sh` (`_run_issueref`, following the `drivers/shellcheck.sh` / `drivers/hadolint.sh` conventions) enforces it: an awk comment-state machine flags a bare `#NNN` (2-4 digits) in the COMMENT portion of an in-scope file, while NOT flagging a `#NNN` inside a string literal / `${#var}` expansion, `@test "..."` description strings (test identities mirrored in `TEST.md`), word-prefixed cross-repo refs that name their tracker (`base#321`, `docker_harness#53`, `moby/buildkit#3403`), hadolint/shellcheck directive codes (`DL3007` / `SC1090`), or version tags (`v0.41.0`). Wired into `test.sh`: the full `just test` lint phase, `just test lint` (no flag = ShellCheck + Hadolint + issueref), and a new `--issueref` narrowing flag. The sweep removed the refs across 104 files (946 comment lines) with no behaviour change (only comment text differs; `@test` names / DL-SC codes / ADR refs / non-comment lines verified untouched). Unit-tested in `issueref_lint_spec.bats` (12: flagged forms — leading / trailing / paren / .bats-helper — and must-keep forms — string literal / ADR / DL-SC / version / cross-repo / single-or-5-digit / `${#arr}` / heredoc usage prose). Out of scope (untouched): commit messages, PR bodies, CHANGELOG, `doc/adr/` prose, and downstream repos' own comments. `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1968 -> 1980: unit 1883 -> 1895). Refs #576, ADR-00000013.
- **`init.sh` host preflight warns when the `just` runner is missing (+ opt-in `--bootstrap-just`, no vendored binary) (#607, ADR-00000005/00000010/00000011)** — `init.sh` produced a repo whose entire user-facing entry is `just <ns> <verb>` but performed no host preflight, so on a fresh host it reported `Done!` while the first `just` call failed `command not found`. `init.sh` now probes `command -v just` at the end of `main()` (runs on BOTH the new-repo and existing-repo paths, after the scaffolding/setup that lays down the `justfile` symlink) and, on a miss, emits ONE advisory `WARN` (registered log event `init_just_missing`) carrying the install pointer (`apt`/`brew`/`cargo`/the official `https://just.systems/install.sh` one-liner, mirroring README "Prerequisites", plus the `--bootstrap-just` hint). It is deliberately **non-fatal**: init still completes and the symlinks + wrappers are already created, so a later `just` install works immediately (and each recipe has a raw `./script/<verb>.sh` fallback meanwhile). The opt-in `init.sh --bootstrap-just` flag runs the OFFICIAL prebuilt-binary installer into `~/.local/bin` (registered event `init_bootstrap_just`); it is never auto-invoked, no-ops when `just` is already on PATH, and prints a PATH reminder when `~/.local/bin` is not on PATH afterward. The binary is **not vendored** into the subtree (arch-specific, `--squash` would inject it into every downstream history, a committed binary never updates — #607 Notes). Unit-tested in `init_spec.bats` (+6: warn-on-absent / json event body / install-hint content / silent-when-present / bootstrap no-op-when-present / bootstrap installer pipeline) + integration in `init_new_repo_spec.bats` (+2: full init warns + exits 0 + still lays down the justfile + wrapper symlinks when `just` is absent; silent when present). `TEST.md` synced; README deferred to #656. Refs #607, #557, ADR-00000005, ADR-00000010, ADR-00000011.
- **`--help` reachable + `--lang` for the human-facing namespaces at every just level (#655, epic #625, ADR-00000011 §6)** — completes the ADR-00000011 §6 command-model promise: help is reachable and the language is selectable at the recipe, namespace, and entry levels, within the locked just-1.52 mechanism (the #655 grill: `just <recipe> --help`/`--lang` ARE forwarded to the backing script as `{{args}}`, but `just <ns> --help`/`--lang` ERROR because just parses the flag as part of the recipe path — so namespace help is the BARE `just <ns>` module `default` and namespace-level language comes from `SETUP_LANG`/`$LANG`, not a flag). **Recipe `--help`:** the docker wrappers already shipped 4-locale usage + `--lang` (#565/#222); `test.sh` already had English usage; `template/new.sh` GAINED `-h`/`--help` (it previously only printed a one-line usage on a missing name). **Namespace bare help:** the `docker` / `base` / `release` module `default` recipes were listing the ROOT entry's namespaces instead of their own verbs, because inside a `mod?`'d module `justfile()` resolves to the root entry — switched to `source_file()` so `just docker` / `just base` / `just release` now list THEIR OWN recipes (exit 0). **`--lang` plumbing (i18n scope = human-facing only):** `docker` was done; `base` (`init.sh` / `upgrade.sh` / `completions.sh`) + `template` (`new.sh`) now accept `--lang <code>`, validate it via i18n.sh's `_sanitize_lang` (non-fatal warning + fallback to `en` on a bad value), and resolve the default via `_resolve_lang` (honors `SETUP_LANG`/`$LANG`) — their own messages stay English-only pending the localized-string pass, so this slice wires the flag uniformly without a premature i18n rewrite. `completions.sh` / `new.sh` (previously standalone) source the shared `i18n.sh` rather than duplicating the validator. The machine/CI namespaces `test` and `release` stay **English-only** (no `--lang`; `test.sh` rejects it as an unknown option). Also closed a lint blind spot the change surfaced: `dist/script/base/*.sh` (completions.sh) was not in `test.sh`'s shellcheck list — now added. Unit-tested in a new `help_lang_spec.bats` (19; each backing script direct: `--help` exits 0 + prints usage, the i18n scripts accept/validate `--lang`, `test.sh` is English-only) + `justfile_user_spec` (+5: bare `just docker`/`just base` list their verbs, recipe `--help`/`--lang` forwarding) + `justfile_spec` (+2: test/release own a default + are English-only) + `ci_spec` (base shellcheck path) + `template_new_spec`/`justfile_user_spec` setup mirror i18n.sh. `TEST.md` synced; README deferred to #656. Refs #655, #625, ADR-00000011.
- **Schema i18n-index + locale coverage (#591, deferred from #562, epic #559)** — `lib/schema.sh` gains the `SCHEMA_I18N` associative array, the i18n column #562 lacked: it maps each canonical `<section>.<key>` (same keying as `SCHEMA_VALIDATOR`) to the `_tui_msg` message key its setup-TUI editor shows as the key's label/help. Keys with no interactive editor (the transcript knobs `logging.wrapper_transcript*`, config/env-only since #608) map to an explicit `""` opt-out. A new `_schema_i18n_key <section> <key> [<fallback>]` accessor resolves a key (normalising per-service logging sections + numbered list suffixes) to its message key, echoing `<fallback>` for free-form / opted-out keys; `setup_tui.sh`'s generic `_edit_list_section` now routes its per-entry prompt through it (behaviour-preserving — registered list keys resolve to the same literal they passed, free-form `tmpfs_` / `security_opt_` fall back), so the index is consumed in production and cannot silently drift from the menu. `schema_coverage_spec.bats` adds the deferred coverage (4 -> 8 tests): the index is complete (every validator key has a row), has no orphan rows, every mapped key is present in all four locale tables (en / zh-TW / zh-CN / ja) — a missing translation in any locale fails CI — plus the accessor's own behaviour. No translations were missing; all 21 mapped keys already existed in every locale. Refs #591, #562, #559.
- **`just base init` + opt-in `just base completions` (#653, epic #625, ADR-00000011)** — two additive recipes on the `base` namespace introduced by #652. `just base init [args]` forwards to `./.base/init.sh` so a repo can re-wire its base-owned symlinks + `.gitignore` without remembering the raw script path. `just base completions install|uninstall [--shell bash|zsh|fish|all]` is an opt-in shell tab-completion installer (`dist/script/base/completions.sh`): it writes the **dynamic** clap_complete loader — bash gets the literal `eval "$(JUST_COMPLETE=bash just)"`, fish/zsh get the live `JUST_COMPLETE=<shell> just` completer output — into each shell's standard auto-load directory (`${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/bash-completion/completions/just`, `${XDG_CONFIG_HOME:-~/.config}/fish/completions/just.fish`, `${XDG_DATA_HOME:-~/.local/share}/zsh/site-functions/_just`). Because the loader is dynamic the completions always reflect the live justfile, including the layered `docker::` / `base::` namespaces. It **never** edits a shell rc; `--shell` defaults to detecting the current shell from `$SHELL` (unknown -> error asking for an explicit `--shell`), `all` does all three, install overwrites and uninstall is a safe no-op when absent. For zsh it prints (stdout, never edits `~/.zshrc`) a one-line `fpath+=(<dir>); autoload -U compinit; compinit` hint when the site-functions dir is not already on `$fpath`. `init.sh` wires the `script/base/completions.sh` symlink alongside `justfile.base` (base-owned, flows on upgrade). The `init.sh` / `upgrade.sh` scripts stay at the `.base/` root in this slice; their relocation to `dist/script/base/` is #654. Unit-tested in `completions_spec.bats` (11; sandboxed HOME + XDG + stubbed `just`) + `justfile_user_spec` (executable forwarding, +2) + `justfile_spec` (static recipe declarations, +1) + `init_new_repo_spec` (completions.sh symlink). README is deferred to the #656 holistic refresh. Refs #625, ADR-00000011.
- **lnav format for the wrapper transcript (#609)** — adds `script/docker/lib/transcript.lnav-format.json`, a **regex**-type [lnav](https://lnav.org) format that parses the plain-text transcript's `<ISO ts> [service] LEVEL: msg` lines (timestamp ordering + level filtering / highlighting for DEBUG / INFO / WARN / ERROR / FATAL; raw docker output falls through as body). Targets `log/<verb>/*.log` via `file-pattern` and coexists with the existing `log.lnav-format.json` (JSON, `*.jsonl`). Load once with `lnav -i .base/script/docker/lib/transcript.lnav-format.json`. Also adds `log/` to base's **own** root `.gitignore` + `.dockerignore` (the canonical sync targets consumer repos, not base itself) and untracks the transcript files that earlier `git add -A` runs accidentally committed under `script/docker/log/` — so base never ships its own run transcripts via the subtree. Unit-tested in `test/unit/transcript_lnav_spec.bats` (8): structural (grep — the CI image has no jq/lnav) + functional, where the embedded regex (extracted + JSON-unescaped) must match real transcript lines and all 5 levels via `grep -P` and reject raw docker lines. README (lnav usage) synced. Closes #609. Refs #606.
- **Interactive-verb orchestration capture in the wrapper transcript (#608)** — `just run` (attached), `exec`, and `setup-tui` now capture their **orchestration phase** to the transcript (including `exec`'s not-running precheck error) and then call `_transcript_detach` before handing the terminal to the interactive docker/TUI process, so the interactive session itself is not captured. `run -d` (non-interactive) keeps the full capture from #606. New `_transcript_detach` finalizes the transcript early (the run's exit code is not yet known) with a `transcript_detached` closing line; the shared `_transcript_finalize` is extracted so the EXIT handler and detach reuse one teardown. The three interactive verbs are added to a `_TRANSCRIPT_INTERACTIVE_VERBS` set (activation gates on full-OR-interactive via `_transcript_is_capture_verb`); `run.sh`'s foreground-cleanup `trap _app_cleanup EXIT` is migrated to the `_atexit` registry so it coexists with the transcript finalize. Unit-tested in `transcript_spec.bats` (+6: interactive/capture classification, detach orchestration-only / no-detach full-capture / detach no-op, wiring guards). Closes #608. Refs #606, ADR-00000007.
- **Wrapper transcript for non-interactive verbs (#606)** — `just build` / `setup` / `stop` / `prune` / `upgrade` now tee their combined output to a plaintext transcript at `log/<verb>/<UTC-ts>-<traceid8>.log` (ANSI stripped; the terminal keeps colour), with a per-verb `latest.log` symlink, a closing `transcript_complete exit_code=… duration=…s` line, and retention (keep the most recent `wrapper_transcript_keep`=20 AND drop older than `wrapper_transcript_days`=14, stricter wins). New `lib/transcript.sh` activates **only in execution** — each verb's `main()` calls `_transcript_begin` (never at `_lib.sh` source time, so unit specs that source a wrapper don't tee). It is the producer of #605's `_LOG_IS_TTY` cache (frozen before the tee). stdout and stderr each get their **own** tee so the terminal keeps them separate (a caller's `2>err` is not collapsed) while both land in the one transcript file. Transcript meta lines are written to the file only (never the terminal), so wrapper output is byte-identical to before. EXIT is owned via a small `_atexit <fn>` registry (build's `--clean` cleanup migrated off `trap … EXIT`); the finalize closes the tees, waits them (no truncation), strips ANSI, repoints `latest.log`, and prunes. Kill switch: `[logging] wrapper_transcript=false` (complete no-op). Interactive verbs (`run` / `exec` / `setup-tui`) produce no file in this slice (deferred to #608). Failure-safe: if `log/` can't be created or `tee` is absent it WARNs and runs on. `log/` is added to the `.gitignore` **and** `.dockerignore` canonical sets (one edit to the shared source, #604) so downstream picks it up on upgrade. 3 setup.conf `[logging]` keys + validators (schema-registered), 3 granular failure log-events. Unit-tested in `test/unit/transcript_spec.bats` (17) + integration (`log/` in both ignore sets). Closes #606. Refs #438, #605, #604, ADR-00000007.
- **`.dockerignore` canonical sync mechanism (#604)** — base had no `.dockerignore` sync at all: the file existed only at base's own root (hand-maintained) and was never templated, so downstream repos shipped derived artifacts (`.env` / `compose.yaml` / `coverage/` ...) into their Docker build context. Mirrors the existing `.gitignore` canonical-sync (#172): a new `_canonical_dockerignore_entries` (delegates to `_canonical_gitignore_entries` — a derived artifact not worth committing is not worth shipping in the build context, so the two share one source and never drift) plus `_sync_dockerignore`, both in `lib/gitignore.sh`. The append-missing / managed-block-marker / idempotency mechanics are factored into a shared `_sync_managed_entries`; `_sync_gitignore` and `_sync_dockerignore` are now thin wrappers over it (behaviour-preserving for `.gitignore`). `init.sh` invokes the sync on both the new-repo path (creates `.dockerignore` with a managed block) and the existing-repo path (append-missing into the user's file).
