Per-repo build-context specifics (`script/` / `test/` / `config/`) stay hand-maintained **above** the managed block and are never touched; re-running is idempotent. `log/` is intentionally **not** added here (reserved for #606; it will land in both files from a single edit to the shared canonical source). Unit-tested in `test/unit/dockerignore_spec.bats` (11) + integration in `gitignore_sync_spec.bats` (+4) / `init_new_repo_spec.bats` (+2). Closes #604. Refs #172, #606.

### Tests
- **conf accessor + lib loader: dirty-input + error-path coverage (#689)** — closes the gaps surfaced by the adversarial test-quality audit, all behaviour-pinning (no production change; every predicted behaviour is an intentional contract). `conf_accessor_spec.bats` (5 -> 11): duplicate key within a section resolves last-wins; a reopened `[section]` later in the file keeps entries from both occurrences (header deduped); an inline `key = value # comment` keeps the `# comment` text verbatim (only LEADING `#` lines are stripped — inline comments intentionally unsupported); a header with internal whitespace `[ deploy ]` captures the spaced name (does NOT match `deploy`); an unterminated `[deploy` (no closing bracket) drops its keys with no crash; and `_conf_list_sorted` skips non-numeric list suffixes (`mount_x` / `mount_` / `mount_2b`), exercising the `^[0-9]+$` guard's reject path. `lib_spec.bats` (43 -> 45): `_load_env` round-trips a shell-hostile value (`$(...)` / backtick / spaces / single-quote) written with the same `%q` quoting `write_env` emits, asserting no command-substitution executes and no word-splitting (loader half of the write_env quoting contract); and `_load_env` on a present-but-nonexistent path aborts under `set -euo pipefail` with a `No such file` diagnostic (no `[[ -f ]]` guard — the missing-`.env.generated` race). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1982 -> 1990: unit 1897 -> 1905). Closes #689. Refs #625.

### Changed
- **CI double-run eliminated: `coverage` is the primary unit gate, `bats-unit` matrix replaced by a single plain `bats-fragile` job, coverage shards weight-balanced (#677)** — after #686 unified the coverage job onto the shared Alpine `test-tools` image, the 4-shard `bats-unit` matrix and the 4-shard `coverage` matrix ran the SAME ~1991 unit specs twice per PR (8 parallel jobs), differing only by `COVERAGE=1`. The only delta `bats-unit` covered over `coverage` was the kcov-fragile tests `coverage` SKIPS via `[ "${COVERAGE:-0}" = 1 ] && skip` (across 9 spec files). This restructure (plan A∘C): (1) the `coverage` matrix stays the PRIMARY unit gate — its codecov upload + the #615 / ADR-00000008 project gate are untouched; (2) the `bats-unit` matrix is replaced by a SINGLE `bats-fragile` job that runs ONLY those 9 fragile spec files in PLAIN mode (no `COVERAGE`), exercising exactly the tests coverage skips, so the delta is preserved with ZERO double-run — every unit test still runs SOMEWHERE (non-fragile under coverage/kcov, fragile under `bats-fragile`). The fragile set is computed at RUNTIME (`test.sh --bats-fragile` -> `_fragile_unit_files` greps a line-anchored `COVERAGE:-0 && skip` guard), so a NEW fragile-skip in a 10th file is picked up automatically (a `ci_spec` guard asserts the runtime set equals the anchored grep, in both directions, so no unit test can silently go unrun); (3) `_shard_unit_files` replaces round-robin with greedy bin-packing by per-spec `@test` count (heaviest-first into the lightest shard), so the slowest coverage shard drops from ~594 `@test` (round-robin, ~1.26x the average) to ~474 (within 3 of the 473 average). New plumbing: `test.sh --bats-fragile` (+ `BATS_FRAGILE` through `_run_via_compose`), `_run_bats_fragile` / `_fragile_unit_files` in `drivers/bats.sh`. The `bats-fragile` job uses the SAME probe-and-rebuild Obtain pattern (#697). `ci-rollup needs:` and `release needs:` swap `bats-unit` -> `bats-fragile`; `coverage` joins the `release` chain (it is now the primary unit gate). Unit-tested in `ci_spec.bats` (+6: `_fragile_unit_files` set-equality + inverse-completeness, `_run_bats_fragile` targets-only-fragile + plain-mode, `main --bats-fragile` dispatch, `_shard_unit_files` greedy balance) and `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (+1 net: `bats-fragile` single-job / invoke / no-matrix-remains, ci-rollup + release needs retargeted). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 2076 -> 2083: unit 1991 -> 1998; `ci_spec` 46 -> 52, `self_test_yaml_spec` 62 -> 63). Closes #677. Refs #686, #615, #377, ADR-00000008.
- **CI: kcov baked into the shared `test-tools` image; coverage no longer rebuilds its toolchain every run (#686)** — the coverage matrix was the last job NOT running on the pre-baked `test-tools` (Alpine) image: it ran on the upstream `kcov/kcov` (Debian) image and `_install_deps` apt-installed ~24 packages (perl, git, bats, openssl, ...) at runtime on EVERY shard of EVERY PR, needing `APT_MIRROR_DEBIAN` to dodge a flaky mirror. `kcov` is not packaged in any Alpine repo (main / community / edge-testing all lack it, verified), so it is now **compiled from source in a discardable `kcov-builder` stage** in `dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools` and the binary COPY'd into the final image, which `apk add`s only kcov's runtime shared libs (`libstdc++ libcurl libdw libelf zlib libgcc`), not the build toolchain. The `coverage` compose service now uses the SAME `test-tools` image as `ci` (only `COVERAGE=1` differs); the `coverage` GHA job obtains it via the identical 3-layer fallback (build-if-Dockerfile-touched else pull `ghcr:main`) the `bats-unit` job uses. **Removed:** the `kcov/kcov` Debian image reference, the entire `_install_deps` apt path (+ its `ci_apt_*` / `ci_bats_mock_clone_failed` die paths and 8 `_install_deps` unit tests), and `APT_MIRROR_DEBIAN` from the coverage hot path. **Spike result (recorded):** approach B (kcov-on-musl) was validated for accuracy + stability — line-rate 0.530 (Debian) vs 0.529 (Alpine) and per-file covered counts matched within <0.1% (codecov threshold is 1%) for shard 2/4, bit-stable across 3 runs with zero failures/segfaults; approach G (xtrace coverage) was assessed feasible but deferred (needs a cobertura converter + Codecov line-only validation) and is recorded for a later single-run review. **Hard constraints held:** the plain `bats-unit` job is unchanged (single-run unification deferred), Codecov still receives a valid cobertura report, the #615 / ADR-00000008 coverage PR gate is untouched, and the mawk+gawk cross-env awk parity stays in the image. `template_spec.bats` (+3: kcov-builder stage / kcov binary COPY / runtime-lib install; the make-guard retargeted to the final stage only since the builder legitimately uses make), `ci_spec.bats` (-8 `_install_deps` tests), `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (`git fetch origin` Obtain-step count 6 -> 7 for the new coverage Obtain step), `release-test-tools.yaml` smoke step (+`kcov --version`). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1982 -> 1977: unit 1897 -> 1892). README deferred to #656. Refs #686, #615, #576, #613, ADR-00000008.
- **`upgrade.sh` Step 5 healing extracted into a declarative Dockerfile-migration list `lib/dockerfile_migrate.sh` (#567, folds #579 facet B)** — upgrade.sh's Step 5 had become an accreting pile of one-off `sed`s, one per base contract change (the #348 lib-copy split, the #399 wrapper-copy move). It is now a **deep module behind a small interface**: the new `dist/script/docker/lib/dockerfile_migrate.sh` exposes `apply_migrations <dockerfile>` over an ordered, data-driven `_MIGRATIONS` table of `{detect, transform}` units, and Step 5 simply sources the lib and calls the dispatcher over the repo-root Dockerfile (+ sibling `script/entrypoint.sh`). Each migration auto-applies **idempotently** where its shape is detected and **SKIPs** (warn, never force-rewrite) where structure is missing / ambiguous — the inherited Step-5 apply policy. Seeded with the eight v0.41.0-fanout breakages, each an independently unit-tested unit: (1) wrapper-COPY shape A `COPY *.sh /lint/` + shape B `COPY .base/script/docker/*.sh /lint/` -> `COPY .base/script/docker/wrapper/*.sh /lint/`; (2) drop the retired `.base/dockerfile/setup` `${CONFIG_DIR}/pip/requirements.txt` install line; (3) drop redundant explicit hand-listed top-level lib/wrapper `/lint/` COPYs (single- and backslash-continued, multi-distro repos); (4) `_entrypoint_logging.sh` -> `runtime/logging.sh` rename (the Dockerfile COPY **and** the entrypoint source line); (5) the hadolint rules the slimmed `.hadolint.yaml` (#466) surfaces — DL3007 pin `:latest` helper tags / DL3046 `useradd -l` / DL3003 `cd /lint` -> `WORKDIR /lint` / DL3042 pip `--no-cache-dir` / DL4006 alpine lint-tools `SHELL` ash-pipefail / DL3006 inline ignore on parameterized `FROM`; (6) the noetic entrypoint `SC1091` -> `SC1090,SC1091` directive; (7, #579 facet B) `ARG USER` -> `ARG USER="${USER_NAME}"` (v0.41.0 compose/CI pass `USER_NAME`, a bare `ARG USER` builds the default `initial` user mismatching the `/home/${USER_NAME}/work` mount); (8, #579 facet B) bracket the entrypoint's `set -u` ROS `setup.bash` source with `set +u`/`set -u` (the unbound `AMENT_TRACE_SETUP_FILES` killed the container at start, failing `just run` while build-time CI smoke — which never starts the container — stayed green). Adding the next fanout breakage is a new `{detect, transform}` pair in the lib, not another branch in `upgrade.sh`. Compatible with ADR-00000006 (the frozen `.base` path contract is unchanged; this restructures the heal MECHANISM). Unit-tested in a new `dockerfile_migrate_spec.bats` (32, each migration in isolation + the dispatcher contract); integration `upgrade_spec.bats` retargeted from the old #348/#399 assertions to the new dispatcher behaviour end-to-end (announce pass / wrapper-copy heal staged into the commit / entrypoint nounset-guard / no-Dockerfile skip / idempotency; 17 -> 14). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1939 -> 1968: unit 1851 -> 1883, integration 88 -> 85). Refs #567, #579, #348, #399, #466, ADR-00000006.
- **relocated `init.sh` + `upgrade.sh` out of the subtree root into `dist/script/base/` (#654, epic #625, amends ADR-00000006, ADR-00000011 §8)** — the two scripts that backed the `base` namespace recipes (`just base init` / `upgrade` / `update`) were the last `.base/`-root eyesores; they now live at `.base/dist/script/base/{init,upgrade}.sh` alongside `justfile.base` + `completions.sh`. Because they no longer sit at the subtree root, each rewrote its self-location from `dirname $BASH_SOURCE` to a **walk-up to the subtree root** — the directory carrying the `.version` + `dist/` markers. `upgrade.sh` `basename`s that root for the `git subtree pull --prefix=` flag, so it still resolves to `.base` (NOT the script's own deep dir `base`, which would corrupt the repo); its `_lib.sh` source and Step-3 `init.sh` invocation (the symlink-resync AND `--gen-conf` delegations) were repointed to the deep paths in the same slice, per ADR-00000006's lockstep discipline. The **scope decision** (confirmed): the ADR-00000011 §4 "base=origin symlink unification" this issue was framed around is **N/A — there is no duplicated tooling to collapse** (the real docker tree is already single-copy at `dist/script/docker/`; `script/test` + `script/release` are base's OWN self-test/skeleton tooling); #654 is therefore purely the §8 relocation. Lockstep refs updated: `justfile.base` recipes, `drivers/{shellcheck,bats}.sh` lint/coverage paths, `self-test.yaml` (classify block-list globs + release-archive `cp` list, now covered by the `dist/` entry + the e2e job's `bash .base/...` bootstrap), `build.sh --reset-conf` dry-run command, `lib/gitignore.sh` + consumer `Dockerfile` comments, and the README literal bootstrap commands (the holistic README tree refresh stays deferred to #656). The **brand-new-consumer bootstrap** command changes to `bash .base/dist/script/base/init.sh` (steady-state users keep calling `just base ...`). Verified against a real git-subtree fixture: a new integration test (`upgrade_spec.bats`, +1) drives the relocated `upgrade.sh v0.9.7` through a `git` PATH-shim and asserts the captured `--prefix=.base` plus a clean pull (version bumped, no stray `base/` dir, payload under `.base/`); `init_spec` / `init_new_repo_spec` / `gitignore_sync_spec` fixtures place + invoke the scripts at the deep path. **Out of scope (untouched):** the harness hook `enforce_wrapper_first_upgrade.sh` (separate repo, raw-path regex) — updated by the main loop separately. `TEST.md` synced (integration upgrade_spec 16 -> 17). Refs #654, #625, ADR-00000006, ADR-00000011.
- **CI: kcov coverage sharded across a matrix + promoted to an enforced PR gate (#615, ADR-00000008, amends #377)** — #377 parallelised the normal test path but left the coverage path fully serial (a single `kcov ... bats unit/ integration/`, ~8-12 min) and sidestepped that cost by making `coverage` main-push-only and explicitly NON-gating (out of `ci-rollup`'s `needs:`, `codecov/project` not a required check, kcov never run on PRs). #613 first fixed the kcov-env test bugs that had made the job flaky, clearing the precondition to let coverage gate. This change: (1) **shards the kcov run** into a `strategy.matrix` (`shard: ['1/4'..'4/4']`, `fail-fast: false`) MIRRORING the `bats-unit` matrix — both select their slice via one shared primitive `_shard_unit_files` (round-robin over `find test/bats/unit -name '*_spec.bats' | sort`), so coverage shard *k* kcov's the identical unit slice the unit-test matrix runs, with the 87 integration specs on the last shard only (no slice kcov'd twice); each shard uploads its partial report under `flags: coverage-shard-<index>` and Codecov merges the uploads into one project figure. (2) **runs coverage on PRs** via `if: needs.classify.outputs.code_changed == 'true'` (was `push && ref == refs/heads/main`). (3) **enforces it as a gate**: `coverage` joins `ci-rollup`'s `needs:` (now 9 jobs) and the verifier consumes `needs.coverage.result` (SKIPPED-as-pass for doc-only PRs), so a kcov failure blocks PR merge; a coverage regression below the `.codecov.yaml` `project` threshold (now `informational: false`) is enforced as a required `codecov/project` branch-protection status. **Threshold decided explicitly:** `project` + `patch` both `target: auto, threshold: 1%` — `auto` tracks the PR base rather than a fixed % (a fixed patch target e.g. 80% would be flaky given the codebase's intentionally-uncovered bash branches, the #613-class brittleness this gate must avoid). New plumbing: `test.sh --coverage-shard N/T` (+ `COVERAGE_SHARD` env through `_run_via_compose`), `_run_coverage [<n>/<total>]` (full suite when no arg = local `just test coverage`; `just test coverage 1/4` runs one shard locally), and the coverage path now SKIPS the lint phase (the kcov/kcov debian image bakes in neither shellcheck nor hadolint — a latent bug that would have failed every coverage shard at `_run_hadolint`; lint is covered by the dedicated jobs). **Wall-time:** the added PR critical-path cost is ~one shard (~170s, measured locally for shard 1/4 via `just test coverage 1/4` producing a real cobertura report), parallel with `bats-unit`, replacing the ~8-12 min serial job; the precise CI before/after lands on the first CI run of this gate. Tested: `ci_spec.bats` (+10: `_shard_unit_files` partition exhaustive+disjoint+validation, `_run_coverage` shard/last-shard/non-last/full, `main --coverage-shard` env plumbing + coverage-mode guard, `--ci COVERAGE=1` skips lint); `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (+1: coverage now a PR-gated kcov matrix invoking `--coverage-shard`, in `ci-rollup` needs, verifier consumes its result); `template_spec.bats` retargeted (`coverage` recipe takes an optional shard arg). ADR-00000008 records the decision; `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1927 -> 1938: unit 1840 -> 1851). README deferred to #656. Refs #615, #377, #613, ADR-00000008.
- **split `setup_spec.bats` by concern to balance the CI shard floor (#377, #677)** — the unit suite's `setup_spec.bats` held 371 tests in ONE file (~20% of the unit suite). The bats-unit + coverage matrices shard round-robin BY FILE, so this single file was the per-shard floor (~239s) that no amount of extra shards could split. Pure test reorganization (ZERO behaviour change, same total test count): the file is split into five cohesive `*_spec.bats` files grouped by the section structure already in the source — `setup_spec.bats` (145: core detection / SSH X11 / INI parser / resolvers / image_name / drift / `main --lang` / template defaults), `setup_subcommand_spec.bats` (64: the `#49` git-style subcommand dispatcher — dispatch / set / show / list / add / remove / reset), `setup_emit_spec.bats` (75: `apply`-time emit + CLI flags — `.env.generated` cache #502 / runtime ENV-bake #503 / config/app #504 / i18n / `[build]` / `--quiet` #285 / `--gui` #338 / `#450` guards / S7 #507 / reconcile #569), `setup_section_validate_spec.bats` (29: per-section setup.conf parameter end-to-end coverage #202), and `setup_stage_override_spec.bats` (58: the per-stage override engine #215 / #220). All five `load` a new shared `test/bats/unit/setup_spec_helper.bash` (the common `setup()` / `teardown()`) so the preamble is single-sourced and cannot drift. The 371 `@test` blocks moved verbatim; the unit grand total is unchanged. `TEST.md` resynced (the single 371-test section replaced by five per-file sections summing to 371; grand total unchanged). Directly addresses the per-shard-floor follow-up noted in the 2->4 bats-unit shard entry below. Refs #377, #677.
- **hadolint folded into `just test` for local==CI single source of truth (#650, ADR-00000011)** — `just test` ran ShellCheck + Bats but NOT hadolint; hadolint was a CI-only job (`self-test.yaml`'s `hadolint` job, two `hadolint/hadolint-action@v3.1.0` steps), so a Dockerfile change could pass `just test` locally yet fail CI hadolint -- a local==CI blind spot. A new per-tool driver `script/test/drivers/hadolint.sh` (`_run_hadolint`, following the `drivers/shellcheck.sh` conventions: sourced library, no `main`, uses `${REPO_ROOT}` + `_log_*`/`_die`) is now the SINGLE source of truth for which Dockerfiles + which config the self-test lints (`dist/dockerfile/Dockerfile` + `dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools`, config `dist/.hadolint.yaml`). `test.sh` sources it next to the other drivers and runs `_run_hadolint` after `_run_shellcheck` in the full-`just test` container lint phase (the test-tools image bakes in the `hadolint` binary, so it runs in-container), so a Dockerfile regression now fails `just test` the same way it fails CI. Per ADR-00000011 #3 min->max, `just test lint` (no flag) runs all linters (ShellCheck + Hadolint) via the container; `just test lint --shellcheck` / `just test lint --hadolint` narrow to one. The host-only `--shellcheck-only` path (dedicated GHA shellcheck job) is unchanged, and a new `--hadolint-only` in-container primitive backs the driver. `self-test.yaml`'s `hadolint` job now obtains the test-tools image (same #317 P2 3-layer fallback as the bats jobs) and runs the SAME driver via `./script/test/test.sh --lint --hadolint` instead of the hadolint-action -- one source of truth, two callers, so local + CI can never drift; it stays a dedicated job (ci-rollup / release `needs: hadolint` unchanged). New event keys (`ci_no_hadolint` / `ci_lint_tool_without_lint` / `ci_unknown_lint_tool`) registered in `log-events.txt`. `ci_spec.bats` (+5) pins the driver (exists, sourced, `_run_hadolint` lives in the driver not `test.sh`, library-only, exact Dockerfile list + config, one invocation per file, host-missing-binary guard, failure propagation); `template_spec.bats` (+1) pins the `lint *args` forwarding; `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` retargeted to assert the hadolint job runs the driver (not the action) + its Obtain step. `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1921 -> 1927: unit 1834 -> 1840). **Deferred** (out of scope, follow-ups): actionlint local parity (the test-tools image lacks actionlint -- needs a test-tools release first) and integration-e2e local parity (too heavy for `just test`). README deferred to #656. Refs #650, ADR-00000011.
- **`script/test/test.sh` split into a dispatcher + per-tool drivers (#650, ADR-00000011 #5)** — the self-test runner was one monolith holding both the CI-container plumbing (`_install_deps` / `_run_via_compose` / arg parsing / `main` / `_die`) AND the per-tool execution. The per-tool logic now lives in sourced driver libraries under `script/test/drivers/`, 1:1 with the runner contract (`test.sh` = dispatcher, one driver per tool): `drivers/bats.sh` owns the bats runners (`_run_unit_tests` / `_run_integration_tests` / `_run_tests` / `_run_bats_path` / `_run_unit_shard` / `_run_behavioural` + the `_behavioural_setup`/`_behavioural_teardown` pair + `_BEHAVIOURAL_BUILDER`), the shared `_bats_args_with_label` helper, and `_run_coverage` (kcov wraps bats, so it lives with the bats driver); `drivers/shellcheck.sh` owns `_run_shellcheck` including the #565 flat-layout consumer-parity pass. `test.sh` keeps the dispatcher role and `source`s both drivers near the top, right after `_lib.sh` (so the drivers see `_log_*` / `_die` and the `REPO_ROOT` global). Each driver is a sourced library (no `main`). **Pure refactor: behaviour byte-identical** — every function name + body is unchanged, so all call sites in `main()` (the `--ci` dispatch, `--shellcheck-only`, `--bats-path`/`--filter`, `--coverage`, `--bats-unit-shard`, `--bats-integration`, `--behavioural`, the `lint`/`coverage`/`compose` modes) are untouched; the bats jobs (unit shards / integration / behavioural / bats-path / coverage) and the shellcheck phase produce identical results. `_run_shellcheck` additionally shellchecks the new `script/test/drivers/*.sh` (base-own self-test tooling, not shipped via `dist/`). `ci_spec.bats` (+6) pins the split (driver files exist, `test.sh` sources both, the runners moved out of `test.sh`, drivers are library-only, `_run_shellcheck` self-lints the drivers). `TEST.md` synced; README deferred to #656. Refs #650, #559, ADR-00000011.
- **tool-first `test/<tool>/<category>/` test layout for base's own suite (#650, ADR-00000012, supersedes ADR-00000004)** — base migrates its self-test tree from the category-first `test/{unit,integration,behavioural}/` shape (ADR-00000004) to the tool-first `test/bats/{unit,integration,behavioural}/` shape ADR-00000012 establishes: each test driver owns one subtree 1:1 with `script/test/drivers/<tool>.sh`, and the tool layer is always present so base and consumer trees share one shape. Pure structural move (`git mv`, history preserved): the 51 unit specs + their shared `test_helper.bash`, the 5 integration specs, and the behavioural spec move under `test/bats/`. Path references rewired in lockstep: `test.sh` (`_run_unit_tests` / `_run_integration_tests` / `_run_bats_path` / `_run_unit_shard` / `_run_coverage` / `_run_behavioural` bats targets, the `--bats-path` behavioural guard + its die-message, and the usage/docstring examples), `self-test.yaml`'s classify block-list (`test/bats/behavioural/**`), and the layout-asserting specs (`ci_spec` `--bats-path` examples, `template_spec` unit-dir existence check, `self_test_yaml_spec` block-list assertion). The integration specs' `load "${BATS_TEST_DIRNAME}/../unit/test_helper"` is unchanged: both dirs moved together under `test/bats/`, so the relative `../unit/test_helper` still resolves. The consumer `dist/test/smoke/` template is out of scope (the shipped consumer layout, migrated separately). **Retired `lint_mixed_test_layout.sh` (#495) + its spec (8 tests):** under tool-first a `test/<tool>/<category>/` dir can never hold two tools (the tool is the parent), so the WARN-only mixed-runner lint it guarded is moot; removed the script, its `_run_shellcheck` invocation + self-shellcheck line, and `lint_mixed_test_layout_spec.bats`. Self-test stays green at the same counts modulo the retirement: unit 1836 -> 1828 (-8), integration 87 unchanged. `TEST.md` resynced (paths + grand total 1923 -> 1915); README deferred to #656. Refs #650, ADR-00000012, supersedes ADR-00000004.
- **CI: bats-unit matrix sharded 2 -> 4 to cut the PR critical path** — the unit suite (~1840 tests) was the dominant PR job at ~280s on the slower of 2 round-robin shards. Splitting `strategy.matrix.shard` to `['1/4','2/4','3/4','4/4']` (the `_run_unit_shard` round-robin already handles any total; 51 spec files partition 12/13/13/13 with no overlap) drops each shard's wall time accordingly. `setup_spec.bats` (371 tests, one file) is the per-shard floor -- weight-by-test-count / intra-file sharding is a further follow-up (#377 note). `self_test_yaml_spec` updated to assert the 4-way matrix. — the five container-op wrappers (`dist/script/docker/wrapper/{build,run,exec,stop,prune}.sh`) repeated ~250 lines of cross-cutting boilerplate: the byte-identical `_msg` dispatcher, the `--lang` pre-pass (#222), and (build + run) the setup/drift orchestration (the `_run_interactive` TUI/setup.sh decision + bootstrap-vs-drift-regen branch + the #101-guarded subprocess `setup.sh check-drift`/`apply` + the defensive no-`.env` error). A new sourced module `dist/script/docker/lib/wrapper.sh` now owns those surfaces as three parameterised helpers: `_msg <category> <key>` (dispatches to the wrapper's own `_msg_<category>` tables), `_wrapper_lang_prepass <verb> "$@"` (seeds `_LANG`, threading the verb into `_sanitize_lang`'s warning tag), and `_wrapper_setup_sync <verb>` (the full build/run lifecycle, deriving the per-verb `[<verb>]` log tag + `<verb>_*` event names from its verb arg, reading `RUN_SETUP` / `SETUP_FORWARD_ARGS` / `FILE_PATH` / `_LANG` defensively per the lib unset-vars convention). `bootstrap.sh` sources `wrapper.sh` from its own `lib/` dir right after `_lib.sh`, so every wrapper that already calls `_bootstrap "$@"` gets the runtime for free; `_bootstrap` now locates the calling wrapper's `BASH_SOURCE` frame robustly (skipping bootstrap.sh's own frames). Each wrapper keeps its own file (the `just <verb>` -> `./script/<verb>.sh` symlink contract is unchanged) and shrinks to its verb-specific behaviour: build still does setup+drift+test-tools+compose-build+predecessor-prune, run still does setup+drift+image-gate+compose-up/exec/foreground, exec/stop/prune stay light (they deliberately do NOT call setup-sync — they expect the derived artifacts to already exist). **Pure refactor: observable behaviour is byte-identical** — the wrapper unit specs (`build_sh_spec` / `run_sh_spec` / `exec_sh_spec` / `stop_sh_spec` / `prune_sh_spec`) and the `wrapper_compose_dispatch_spec` integration test (the emitted `docker compose` invocations) stay green. Scoped to the 5 docker wrappers only; the runtime is designed to host the shared CLI lib + `--help`/`--lang` the test/release/base scripts adopt later (#655 / #656), but nothing outside the docker wrappers is pulled in now (ADR-00000011). New unit coverage in `test/unit/wrapper_lib_spec.bats` (18) exercises each helper in isolation INCLUDING the "called from each of the 5 wrappers" parameterisation; the `template_spec` grep-on-source guards for the setup.sh reference + subprocess check-drift were retargeted from the wrappers to `lib/wrapper.sh` (the #490 brittle-grep lesson). `TEST.md` synced; README deferred to #656. Also closes a local==CI shellcheck blind spot the refactor surfaced: `RUN_SETUP` is set in a wrapper but read in `lib/wrapper.sh`, so the consumer Dockerfile devel-test stage (which lints each wrapper with `shellcheck -S warning` WITHOUT `-x`) flagged SC2034 even though base's own `-x` lint followed the source and stayed green -- a CI-only failure. Fixed by marking RUN_SETUP `local -x` (exported -> shellcheck treats it as used-externally, robust across versions / flat-vs-tree layout / assignment sites), and `test.sh`'s lint phase now re-runs the consumer invocation (`shellcheck -S warning` on the shipped `wrapper/*.sh` + `lib/*.sh`, no `-x`) so `just test` catches this class locally. Refs #565, #559, ADR-00000011.
- **documented an optional runtime interactive-shell env-source pattern in the consumer Dockerfile template (#657)** — the minimal `runtime` stage does NOT inherit devel's `~/.bashrc` + `~/.bashrc.d/` wiring, so `docker exec -it <runtime> bash` (`just docker exec -t runtime bash`) gets none of the repo env (e.g. ROS `ros2`), and `docker exec` bypasses the ENTRYPOINT (which only sets up PID 1). The commented `runtime` block in `dist/dockerfile/Dockerfile` now documents the three-process-kinds rationale — (1) PID 1 / the app (non-interactive, exec'd directly) is sourced only by the ENTRYPOINT; (2) interactive `docker exec` shells get the env via bashrc / `~/.bashrc.d/`; (3) non-interactive ad-hoc `docker exec ... cmd` is reached by neither and inherently needs an explicit `source` / `bash -ic` — so entrypoint (PID 1) and bashrc.d (interactive) are COMPLEMENTARY, both needed (this is NOT a base or entrypoint bug). It then shows the lightweight OPT-IN fix for the interactive gap: a single `RUN echo 'source /opt/ros/$ROS_DISTRO/setup.bash' >> /etc/bash.bashrc` (commented; the ROS-specific line is the CONSUMER's since base is ROS-agnostic), keeping the minimal runtime minimal — explicitly NOT dragging the full `config/` COPY into runtime and NOT baking ROS env into Dockerfile `ENV` (arch- / python-version-dependent `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` / `PYTHONPATH` — fragile; sourcing is canonical). The entrypoint and non-interactive behaviour are unchanged. The actual per-repo ROS source lines live DOWNSTREAM in the ROS repos (tracking issues filed there). Unit-tested in `template_spec.bats` (+3: 3-process-kinds rationale marker, commented `/etc/bash.bashrc` source example, no-`ENV LD_LIBRARY_PATH`/`PYTHONPATH` fragility guard). `TEST.md` synced. Refs #657.
- **generalized the `COPY --from=test-tools-stage` toolchain pattern to all `-test` stages + opt-in `runtime-test` Bats smoke (#647)** — the consumer Dockerfile template (`dist/dockerfile/Dockerfile`) now documents the rule that EVERY `-test` stage stays `FROM <the real stage it tests>` (devel-test `FROM devel`, runtime-test `FROM runtime`) and brings the shared toolchain (ShellCheck / Hadolint / Bats) in via `COPY --from=test-tools-stage` from the pre-built, version-pinned, multi-arch test-tools image — the existing devel-test pattern, generalized. No `-test` stage is `FROM ${TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE}`: a `-test` stage must inherit the REAL environment of the artifact under test (else, for runtime-test, the fat tools base silently resolves a missing `.so` and reports a false green while the shipped minimal runtime is still broken — ros1_bridge#123). The `runtime-test` block gains a documented second smoke style (b): COPY Bats in from test-tools-stage and run a runtime-specific `.bats` suite INSIDE the genuine minimal runtime, alongside / replacing the bare ldd `RUNTIME_SMOKE_CMD` install-check (style (a)). The header also states that flavour-specific test tooling (e.g. ROS colcon test / ament_* / launch_testing) is the **consumer's** responsibility, installed in its own `-test` stage on top of the COPYed common toolchain — base ships only the common toolchain, NOT a ROS-flavoured test image (the originally-scoped `ros-test-tools` sibling image was dropped, scope decision 2026-06-23), and that `-test` stages may be heavier than shipped stages since they never reach users. Build-worker prerequisite landed: `build-worker.yaml`'s "Build runtime-test stage" step now forwards the pinned `TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE` build-arg (previously only devel-test did), so a runtime-test `COPY --from=test-tools-stage` resolves the cached image in CI instead of falling back to `test-tools:local` (pull-access-denied). Unit-tested in `template_spec.bats` (+3: commented Bats COPY shape, header rule + anti-pattern + consumer-owns-flavour-tools, build-worker forwards TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE twice). `TEST.md` synced. Refs #647.
- **consumer docker recipes become the `docker` namespace; `.base`-management moves to a new `base` namespace (#652, epic #625, ADR-00000011)** — zero-special-case slice: the consumer entry `mod?`s the docker module instead of importing it top-level, so `just build` -> **`just docker build`** (and run/exec/stop/prune/setup/setup-tui likewise). As a `mod?` module the docker file now owns its own `default` (`just docker` lists the verbs) and pins recipes to the repo root with `set working-directory := '../..'` (a module recipe's default cwd is the module dir; verified the symlinked-consumer `../..` resolves to the repo root, so `./script/<wrapper>.sh` still runs there). `upgrade` / `upgrade-check` are **not** docker ops -- they move out of the docker module into a new **`base`** namespace (`mod? base`): `just base upgrade [vX.Y.Z]` (apply the subtree pull) and `just base update` (apt-aligned: report whether a newer tag is available, the former `upgrade-check`). `init.sh` wires the `script/base/justfile.base` symlink alongside docker/template. (The heavier `base` work -- relocating `init.sh`/`upgrade.sh` to `script/base/`, the `init` recipe, completions, ADR-00000006 amendment -- lands in #653.) Specs updated: `justfile_spec` (docker module mods + owns default + no upgrade; base module declares upgrade/update; entry mods both), `justfile_user_spec` (executable `just docker <verb>` + `just base upgrade`/`just base update`), `upgrade_spec` integration (`just base update`). README (x4, command refs) + `TEST.md` synced. Build-arg / per-tool runner `build --stage` and the base-self docker namespace are deferred (need #654 symlink unification + generic build.sh). Refs #625, ADR-00000011.
- **base self-release namespace renamed `cd` -> `release` (#651, epic #625, ADR-00000011)** — second action-named-namespace slice. `script/cd/justfile.cd` -> `script/release/justfile.release`; the root `justfile`'s `mod? cd` -> `mod? release`, so base maintainers run `just release <recipe>`. The namespace stays a SKELETON (only `default`) -- the actual release/publish recipes remain in `.github/workflows/` and are deferred to a follow-up; this is the rename only. No README/TEST.md/spec references the namespace, so the change is the two file moves + the one `mod?` line + the skeleton's self-comment. Refs #625, ADR-00000011.
- **base self-test namespace renamed `ci` -> `test`; `script/ci/` -> `script/test/`, `ci.sh` -> `test.sh`, `justfile.ci` -> `justfile.test` (#650, epic #625, ADR-00000011)** — first slice of the ADR-00000011 command-model redesign (action-named namespaces). base maintainers now run **`just test`** (bare = the whole self-test; min->max per ADR-00000011 #3) and `just test lint` / `just test coverage` / `just test behavioural` / `just test clean` for sub-actions, via the root `justfile`'s `mod? test`. The dir/files move `script/ci/{ci.sh,justfile.ci,lint_*.sh}` -> `script/test/{test.sh,justfile.test,lint_*.sh}`; the namespace's `default` recipe runs the suite (the former `test:` recipe folds into it). Rewires every reference: `test.sh`'s self-shellcheck + lint-runner paths, `self-test.yaml`'s four `./script/ci/ci.sh` job invocations, `compose.yaml`'s `ci` / `ci-behavioural` service commands, `lint_bare_stderr.sh`'s scan root, `init.sh` / `Dockerfile.test-tools` / downstream `lib/{log,_lib,log-events}` comments, and the `script/cd/justfile.cd` working-dir note. Specs updated in lockstep (`ci_spec` / `lint_mixed_test_layout_spec` / `self_test_yaml_spec` / `template_spec` / behavioural `runtime_test_smoke_spec`); `template_spec`'s "has test recipe" check becomes "default recipe runs the suite". README (x4) + `TEST.md` command/path/test-name references synced. Pure rename + entry behaviour (bare `just test` runs all); the generic per-tool runner (dispatcher + drivers) and the tool-first `test/<tool>/<category>/` layout (ADR-00000012) land in follow-up slices under #650. Refs #625, ADR-00000011.
- **`just template new <name>` scaffolds repo-local command groups (#633, epic #625, ADR-00000010, closes #594)** — Phase-2. Adds the `template` namespace (`mod? template` in the entry): `just template new <name>` creates `script/local/<name>/justfile.<name>` + `<name>.sh` from `dist/script/template/skel/` (substituting `__NAME__`) and appends the group's `mod?` line to `script/local/justfile.local`, so the group is immediately usable as `just <name> <recipe>`; bare `just template` prints help. The scaffolder `new.sh` validates the name, refuses to clobber an existing group, and keeps the registry append idempotent. The `new` recipe runs `cd {{justfile_directory()}} && script/template/new.sh ...` -- `justfile_directory()` is the repo root in both a bare invocation and under `--working-directory` (unlike `[no-cd]` / `invocation_directory()`), so the scaffolder always runs from a known-good cwd. `init.sh` symlinks `script/template/{justfile.template,new.sh,skel}` (base-owned, flow on upgrade) and `ci.sh` shellchecks `dist/script/template/`. This closes #594's "extension point for repo-local recipes" -- superseding its original `import?`-a-justfile plan, which `just`'s duplicate-recipe-name hard error makes unworkable (namespacing via `mod?` is required). Tested: `template_new_spec` (7 unit, run `new.sh` directly), `justfile_user_spec` (executable: `just template new` -> group works, bare help), `init_new_repo` (template symlinks), `TEST.md`. README docs of the feature land with the #644 holistic refresh (4-lang lockstep). Refs #625, ADR-00000010. Closes #594.
- **repo-local just command-group registry: `script/local/justfile.local` (#632, epic #625, ADR-00000010)** — Phase-2. The consumer entry now `import?`s a **repo-owned** registry `script/local/justfile.local`; each repo-local command group is one `mod?` line in it (path relative to `script/local/`) and surfaces as a **top-level** sub-command `just <group> <recipe>` (verified: `mod?` lines inside an `import?`'d file create top-level namespaces, and a group recipe runs with cwd = its own `script/local/<group>/` dir so it can call sibling scripts). `init.sh` seeds the registry once via a new `_seed_local` (mirrors `_populate_config`): non-clobbering, a real committed file the repo owns, never overwritten by a subtree upgrade -- so a repo's group registrations survive upgrades. `import?` keeps a missing/empty registry from erroring. This is the wiring `just template new <name>` (#633) appends to. Tested: `init_new_repo` (seeded + no-clobber), `justfile_user_spec` (executable: a registered group resolves as a top-level namespace), `TEST.md`. Refs #625, ADR-00000010, #594.
- **layered consumer just entry: split into `dist/script/justfile` (entry) + `dist/script/docker/justfile.docker` (#631, epic #625, ADR-00000010)** — Phase-2 tracer bullet. The single downstream `justfile` becomes a thin **entry** that `import`s a **docker module**: the verb recipes (`build`/`run`/`exec`/`stop`/`prune`/`setup`/`setup-tui`/`upgrade`/`upgrade-check`) move to `dist/script/docker/justfile.docker` (imported TOP-LEVEL, so `just build` still works at the repo root), and the `default` recipe lives in the entry (the docker module carries no `default` -- `just` treats a duplicate recipe name across an import as a hard error). `init.sh` now wires the symlink chain `<repo>/justfile -> script/justfile -> .base/dist/script/justfile` plus `<repo>/script/docker/justfile.docker -> .base/dist/script/docker/justfile.docker` (the entry's `import 'script/docker/justfile.docker'` resolves relative to the repo root, the symlink location -- verified). This is the base for the namespaced extensions (#632 `local`, #633 `template`, #634 `ci`/`cd`). `justfile_spec` (static: entry vs module) + `justfile_user_spec` (executable: full chain fixture) + `init_spec`/`init_new_repo`/`gitignore_sync`/`upgrade_spec` fixtures + `TEST.md` updated; also folds in the P1.4 `TEST.md` smoke-path debt (`test/smoke/*` headers -> `dist/test/smoke/*`). **Deferred (still):** README ASCII tree + mermaid diagrams -- the `justfile -> .base/script/docker/justfile` mermaid edge is now stale and is part of the #644 holistic refresh. Refs #625, ADR-00000010.
- **base self-CI is now the `just ci` namespace; `justfile.ci` → `script/ci/justfile.ci` + base-own root `justfile` entry (#630, epic #625, ADR-00000010)** — fifth slice of the base/downstream split (base-own side). The root `justfile.ci` (invoked `just -f justfile.ci <recipe>`) moves to `script/ci/justfile.ci` and is reached via a new base-own root `justfile` entry that `mod?`s it as the **`ci`** namespace, so base maintainers run `just ci test` / `just ci lint` / `just ci coverage` / `just ci upgrade` (the `-f`-a-separate-file pattern was a Makefile-era holdover; `just` namespaces it). A `script/cd/justfile.cd` skeleton is added as the `cd` namespace (CI and CD kept separate; real release/publish recipes deferred). `set working-directory := '../..'` in both module files pins recipes to the repo root whether reached via the module or `just -f`. The base-own root `justfile` is distinct from the shipped consumer entry (`dist/script/justfile`, #631) and lands harmlessly at `.base/justfile` in a consumer. README (×4) + `CONTEXT.md` + `self-test.yaml` (comment + release-archive: `justfile.ci` → root `justfile`, `script/` already carries `script/ci/`) + `template_spec`/behavioural specs rewired to `just ci` / `script/ci/justfile.ci`. **Deferred:** the README ASCII structure-tree + mermaid diagrams (stale across the whole P1.x reorg) get a single holistic refresh after the dist/ tree settles (post-#634); the **harness** `CLAUDE.md` `just -f justfile.ci` references are a separate-repo doc update (pending owner consent). Refs #625, ADR-00000010.
- **shipped smoke templates + `.hadolint.yaml` relocated to `dist/` (#629, epic #625, ADR-00000010)** — fourth slice of the base/downstream split: base's shipped `test/smoke/` (the shared `test_helper.bash` + `display_env.bats` / `script_help.bats` templates) moves to `dist/test/smoke/`, and `.hadolint.yaml` to `dist/.hadolint.yaml`. base-own tests (`test/{unit,integration,behavioural}`) stay. Rewires `init.sh`'s `.hadolint.yaml` symlink **source** (the consumer's `<repo>/.hadolint.yaml` symlink + `<repo>/test/smoke/` overlay are unchanged), the consumer `Dockerfile`'s `COPY .base/test/smoke/` layer (the sibling `COPY test/smoke/` for the consumer's own smoke stays), and the base-own spec references (`smoke_helper_spec` load path, `template_spec` existence checks, `init_spec`'s `.hadolint` symlink-target fixture + assertions). `release-worker.yaml` is consumer-context (repo-root `test/smoke/` + `.hadolint.yaml`) so its archive list is unchanged. The base-own `self-test.yaml` is rewired: the dedicated `hadolint` job's `config:` → `dist/.hadolint.yaml` (CI-only, not in the local bats gate) and the `release` job's template-archive `cp -r` list now includes `dist/` and drops the relocated `.hadolint.yaml`/`config/` (the latter a P1.1 follow-up the local gate could not catch). No downstream-visible behaviour change until the fanout (#636). Refs #625, ADR-00000010.
- **consumer Dockerfile template relocated + renamed to `dist/dockerfile/Dockerfile` (#628, epic #625, ADR-00000010)** — third slice of the base/downstream split: `dockerfile/Dockerfile.example` moves to `dist/dockerfile/Dockerfile` (the `.example` suffix dropped — the `dist/` location now disambiguates it from the base-own `dockerfile/Dockerfile.test-tools`, which stays). Rewires `init.sh`'s new-repo `cp` source, the `self-test.yaml` hadolint/integration paths, and all spec/fixture path references (`init_spec`'s stub now `mkdir -p`s the `dist/dockerfile` parent). README (×4) + `CONTEXT.md` synced. Test titles/prose that name "the example Dockerfile" are left as-is (descriptive, not paths). No downstream-visible behaviour change until the fanout (#636). Refs #625, ADR-00000010.
- **base docker library/runtime/wrappers relocated to `dist/script/docker/` (#627, epic #625, ADR-00000010)** — second slice of the base/downstream split: `script/docker/{lib,runtime,wrapper}` move to `dist/script/docker/{lib,runtime,wrapper}` (the user-facing `justfile` stays at `script/docker/justfile` until #631). Rewires every consumer-side and base-own reference: `init.sh` wrapper-symlink sources + lib source + entrypoint copy; `upgrade.sh` lib source + **Region C** Dockerfile lint-stage auto-patch (`COPY .base/script/docker/lib` → `.base/dist/script/docker/lib`) in lockstep per ADR-00000006; `ci.sh` + `lint_*.sh` lib source + shellcheck scan roots; `bootstrap.sh` consumer `_lib.sh` lookup + the wrappers' bootstrap-locator loop + `build.sh`/`run.sh` sibling `setup.sh` invocation; `setup.sh` default-`base_path` BASH_SOURCE walk (now one level deeper) + its template-conf locator; `Dockerfile.example` lint-stage `COPY`s; the `self-test.yaml` classify block-list; and all `# shellcheck source=` directives. `_lib.sh`'s sub-lib sourcing is `BASH_SOURCE`-relative so the lib tree is self-consistent after the move. README (×4) + `CONTEXT.md` wrapper glossary synced. Affected unit/integration specs + fixtures updated. No downstream-visible behaviour change until the fanout (#636). Refs #625, ADR-00000010, ADR-00000006.
- **base config default-layer relocated to `dist/config/` (#626, epic #625, ADR-00000010)** — first slice of the base/downstream directory split: base's shipped config tree (the `Dockerfile` layer-1 default layer + the `--gen-conf` source + `setup.sh`'s template baseline) moves from `config/` to `dist/config/`, so consumers read it from `.base/dist/config/`. Rewires the consumer `Dockerfile.example` layer-1 COPY, `init.sh`'s `--gen-conf` source, `setup.sh`'s template-baseline locator (`../../../config` → `../../../dist/config`), `ci.sh`'s base-own shellcheck/test paths, and — per ADR-00000006's frozen-`.base`-path discipline — `upgrade.sh` Region B config-drift detection (`${TEMPLATE_REL}/config` → `${TEMPLATE_REL}/dist/config`) in lockstep, so the drift warning keeps firing. The consumer-side `<repo>/config/` overlay path is unchanged; there is no downstream-visible behaviour change until the fanout (#636). Affected unit/integration specs updated to the new shipped location. Refs #625, ADR-00000010, ADR-00000006.
- **`log.sh` resolves TTY-ness once at startup via `_LOG_IS_TTY` (#605, ADR-00000007)** — single-sink dispatch (#438) chose text-vs-JSON and colour-vs-plain from a *live* `test -t <fd>` on every call. A transcript tee on fd1 (the upcoming #606 wrapper transcript) makes `test -t 1` false mid-run, which would silently flip the live terminal to JSON / no-colour. A new `_log_is_tty <fd>` helper prefers a startup-cached `_LOG_IS_TTY` (a return code: `0` = tty, non-zero = not) and falls back to live `test -t <fd>` when unset; the auto-format branch of `_log_dispatch` and `_log_color_enabled` now route through it. So once the run's real interactivity is cached before the tee, format/colour survive the tee; standalone sourcing (ci.sh, a bare `source log.sh`, `_LOG_IS_TTY` unset) is byte-identical to before. Explicit `LOG_FORMAT=text|json` still short-circuits and never consults the cache. #605 ships only the read + fallback; #606 is the producer that sets `_LOG_IS_TTY`. ADR-00000007 records the cache rationale, the transcript-tee-over-single-sink layering, and the best-effort mixed-format posture under `LOG_FORMAT=json` (no dual-render); `CONTEXT.md` gains the glossary terms "Single-sink dispatch" and "Run trace id". Unit-tested in `log_spec.bats` (+12). Refs #438, #605, #606.
- **`integration-e2e` now runs on a native arm64 + amd64 matrix (#603, #579 A2)** — the self-test runnability gate (#579) only exercised the `init.sh -> just build -> just run -d -> assert -> just stop` contract on `ubuntu-latest` (amd64). It now runs as a static 2-entry `strategy.matrix` (`linux/amd64` -> `ubuntu-latest`, `linux/arm64` -> `ubuntu-24.04-arm`) with `fail-fast: false`, verifying the full A1 assertion set (configured user, container running, `/entrypoint.sh`, writable `~/work`, teardown) on **both** arches via native runners — no QEMU — mirroring the platform->runner convention of build-worker / publish-worker / release-test-tools (#587). The job `runs-on: ${{ matrix.runner }}` and the test-tools Obtain step pulls `:main` for `${{ matrix.platform }}` (multi-arch post-#587) so the arm64 shard resolves the arm64 variant rather than a hardcoded amd64. `ci-rollup` aggregates the matrixed job through `needs.integration-e2e.result` unchanged (all shards must succeed). Guarded structurally by `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (+3) + `actionlint`; the live arm64 run is exercised by the job itself. Closes #603. Refs #579, #587.

### Fixed
- **setup.conf validators now reject embedded whitespace/newlines (compose YAML injection) and `apply` drops a malformed `restart:` policy (#687)** — an adversarial test-quality audit found four value-bearing validators in `lib/_tui_conf.sh` accepted dangerous input that the sibling `_validate_log_local_path` (`#328`) already rejected. `_validate_env_kv` and `_validate_additional_context` matched only the first line of a multi-line value (bash `=~` `.*` is single-line), so a real newline in the value passed the shape check and would inject an extra `environment:` / `additional_contexts:` line into the generated `compose.yaml`; both now reject any embedded `\n`. `_validate_mount` (used by `volumes.mount_*` AND `devices.device_*`) only checked that the colon-split parts were non-empty, so it accepted both an embedded newline (YAML injection into the `volumes:` list) AND an embedded space (a `/my data:/work` mount word-splits in `docker run -v /my data:/work` into two argv tokens); it now rejects ALL whitespace (`[[:space:]]` — space, tab, newline) up front, mirroring `local_path`. No legitimate mount/env/context value contained whitespace (verified across the unit + integration fixtures), so this is a tightening with no behaviour change for valid input. Separately, the `apply` path performs NO schema revalidation (validation lives only on the `set`/`add` write paths), so a hand-edited `restart = sometimes` in `setup.conf` flowed verbatim into `compose.yaml` as `restart: sometimes`, which `docker compose up` rejects with a cryptic error; `_emit_restart_line` now runs the value through `_validate_restart` and drops the line for any invalid policy (apply-time trust-boundary guard) — valid policies (`always` / `on-failure:N` / ...) are unaffected. Unit-tested in `schema_spec.bats` (+1: newline-reject row for env_1 / context_1 / mount_1 / device_1), `tui_spec.bats` (+2: `_validate_mount` / `_validate_env_kv` newline reject), `tui_mount_assembler_spec.bats` (+1: space-bearing path round-trips then is rejected), and `setup_section_validate_spec.bats` (+1: apply drops a malformed `restart:` line); the apply trust boundary is also documented by two locking tests (a `$(...)` env value is not executed at apply time and stays on a single line). Closes #687. Refs #328, #625.
- **`setup` apply/emit trust-boundary + metacharacter/quoting hardening (#688)** — the adversarial test-quality audit confirmed five untested seams where a hand-edited `setup.conf` value (which `apply` ingests without re-validation) or a metachar-bearing name reaches a generated artifact unquoted; four were real bugs. (1) `_upsert_conf_value` wrote `set`/`add` values via a literal `printf '%s = %s\n'` with no newline guard, and the line-anchored scalar validators (`_validate_env_kv` etc., `.*$` matches up to a newline) accept an embedded newline — so `set environment.env_1 $'A=b\nstray'` validated and split into an orphan, un-keyed line that corrupted the INI on the next read; the writer sink now refuses a newline-bearing key/value (protecting set/add/TUI/WS_PATH) and `_setup_set`/`_setup_add` propagate the refusal instead of printing a false success. (2)/(3) `_generate_runtime_dockerfile` baked each `[environment]` entry as `ENV K="<v>"` with no escaping, so a `"` emitted the broken line `ENV MSG="say "hi""` (docker build rejects it) and a `$(...)`/backtick would be subject to Dockerfile ENV expansion / shell substitution at build time — the asymmetric sink versus the `%q`-quoted `deploy.sh` generator; the value is now backslash-escaped (`\`, `"`, `$`) at the bake (after cross-ref expansion) so it lands verbatim in a balanced double-quoted token. (4) `_generate_deploy_sh` inlined `IMAGE` / `CONTAINER_NAME` into the launcher heredoc as a bare double-quoted default expansion, so a `"`/`$(...)` name broke the assignment (ShellCheck error) or command-substituted at field run time; both defaults are now `printf %q`-quoted and emitted as the unquoted default of the parameter expansion (`IMAGE=${DEPLOY_IMAGE:-<q>}`), keeping the launcher ShellCheck-clean and the literal name intact. (5, coverage-only) `_setup_ssh_x11_cookie`'s `xauth_rewrite_failed` middle branch (the `nlist|sed|nmerge` pipe itself exiting non-zero) had no test; a regression that swallowed a failing `nmerge` would emit a bogus `XAUTHORITY` into `.env` — now locked by an xauth stub whose `nmerge` exits 1. No behaviour change beyond the four hardening fixes; the `deploy.sh` skeleton test's expected shape is updated to the `%q` default form. Unit-tested in `setup_subcommand_spec.bats` (+1), `setup_emit_spec.bats` (+2), `setup_spec.bats` (+1), `deploy_spec.bats` (+1). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1982 -> 1987: unit 1897 -> 1902). Closes #688. Refs #625.
- **`lint_bare_stderr.sh` scanned an empty tree (path drift) + two real bare-stderr violations it had been masking; plus an error-path/edge test-coverage sweep (#692)** — the bare-stderr lint's `find` roots were the now-empty `script/docker` + `script/test`, while its exclusion list already used the relocated `dist/script/docker/...` paths, so the lint walked an empty directory and **passed vacuously** — real violations shipped undetected. Fixes: the find root is corrected to `dist/script/docker`, the exclusion-list path drift (`runtime/logging.sh`, `runtime/smoke.sh`, `wrapper/setup_tui.sh`) is healed, and the pre-sourcing `cannot find lib/bootstrap.sh` wrapper guard is allowlisted (it fires before `lib/log.sh` is available). Two genuine violations the corrected scan surfaced are migrated to the logger (`lib/log.sh` is sourced before both via `_lib.sh`): `hook.sh`'s not-executable error and `conf.sh`'s `_upsert_conf_value` file-missing guard now use `_log_err` (new registered events `hook_not_executable`, `conf_upsert_file_missing`). Separately, `dockerfile_migrate.sh`'s logging-rename `detect` grepped ONLY the Dockerfile, so a partial migration (Dockerfile hand-fixed to `runtime/logging.sh`, sibling entrypoint still sourcing the retired `_entrypoint_logging.sh`) returned non-zero, `apply` never ran, and the entrypoint stayed broken; `detect` now also fires when the sibling entrypoint still references the old name (matching the `sc1090` / `nounset` detect convention). The rest is pure test coverage of error/edge branches with no behaviour change: a new `lint_bare_stderr_spec.bats` (5: fixture-tree violation / clean / allowlist + a live-tree clean guard that would fail on the path-drift bug); `init_spec` (+5: `_bootstrap_just` installer-failure, `_call_setup` warn/skip/success, `_gen_setup_conf` missing-template); `ci_spec` (+8: `main` unknown-option / `--hadolint`-standalone / unknown-`LINT_TOOL`, `_shard_unit_files` no-slash / non-numeric / empty-shard + strengthened out-of-range, `_behavioural_setup` no-docker-socket / no-docker-cli); `completions_spec` (+2: exit-2 unknown-arg / missing-action); `issueref_lint_spec` (+3: 2-digit + 4-digit accept boundaries through `_run_issueref` and the every-engine `_ISSUEREF_AWK` loop); `gitignore_spec` (+3: documented CRLF double-append / `..`-traversal / space-path dirty-input constraints); `runtime_smoke_spec` (+2: ldd-non-zero swallow, multi-`.so` `_exit` accumulation). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1982 -> 2011: unit 1897 -> 1926). Closes #692. Refs #625, #576, #677.
- **`issueref` lint awk made portable across mawk / busybox-awk / gawk so the kcov coverage shard detects refs again (#576)** — the `_ISSUEREF_AWK` detector in `script/test/drivers/issueref.sh` matched a ref with the chained-optional regex `#[0-9][0-9]?[0-9]?[0-9]?`. Debian's mawk 1.3.4 (the `awk` in the `kcov/kcov` coverage image) does NOT extend those `?` repeats greedily — its `match()` capped `RLENGTH` at a hash plus two digits, so a 3-4 digit ref like the failing fixtures matched only its first two digits and the trailing digit landed in the `after` char, which the `after !~ /[0-9]/` guard then read as "more digits follow" and silently exempted. Detection therefore no-op'd ONLY under Debian mawk: the four positive-detection specs in `issueref_lint_spec.bats` passed under the alpine/busybox `just test` gate (a local==CI blind spot) but failed the `coverage (2/4)` kcov shard, where the spec runs under mawk. The regex is now the greedy `#[0-9]+` (the digit run is captured whole and identically across busybox-awk, mawk 1.3.4, and gawk), the redundant `after` guard is dropped, and the 2-4 digit window is enforced directly by `length(digits) >= 2 && length(digits) <= 4` — exact original semantics, no exemption weakened. Blind spot closed two ways: (1) the test-tools image now installs `mawk` + `gawk` alongside busybox awk and two new portability specs run `_ISSUEREF_AWK` under EVERY available engine asserting identical detect/exempt results, so a portability regression fails the fast `just test` loop; (2) the Debian-mawk-specific build is additionally gated by the existing local kcov path `just test coverage 2/4`. Unit-tested in `issueref_lint_spec.bats` (+2 cross-engine parity specs, 12 -> 14). `TEST.md` synced (grand total 1980 -> 1982: unit 1895 -> 1897). Refs #576.
- **`just run -t <stage> <cmd>` override on a non-`devel` one-shot stage now runs the ENTRYPOINT and replaces the default CMD, instead of `up -d` + `exec` (#679)** — the non-`devel` foreground CMD path (`run.sh`) was `compose up -d <SERVICE>` + `compose exec <SERVICE> <CMD>`. For a one-shot app target whose ENTRYPOINT sets up the environment (e.g. ROS sourcing) and whose default CMD *is* the app, that was wrong twice over: (1) `compose exec` bypasses the container ENTRYPOINT (same root cause as the interactive-exec gap #88 / #657), so a documented command like `ros2 launch …` failed with `exec: "ros2": executable file not found in $PATH`; and (2) `up -d` had already started the default CMD as PID 1, so the exec'd command ran *alongside* it (double-launch / device contention) rather than replacing it. The branch now dispatches `compose run --rm <SERVICE> <CMD…>`, which runs the service ENTRYPOINT (env set up) **and** replaces the default CMD (no double-launch) — the correct `docker run`-style override semantics. The `devel` path is unchanged (`up -d` + `exec`, intentional: join the idling container with an interactive shell, #458 / #88), and the non-`devel` no-CMD path stays foreground `compose up` so `container_name:` takes effect. Container-name note: `compose run` ignores `container_name:` and appends a `-run-<hash>` suffix (the #458 concern), which is acceptable here because the override container is ephemeral (`--rm`), foreground, and never re-attached to by name. This unblocks the downstream-documented `just run -t runtime ros2 launch <pkg> <launchfile> <args…>` pattern (e.g. lowering a camera profile for a USB 2.x link). Unit-tested in `run_sh_spec.bats` (non-`devel` + CMD -> `compose run --rm`; `devel` + CMD still `up -d` + `exec`; #679 repro shape; #580 exit-code propagation via the `run` path) + `template_spec.bats` (structural dispatch guard retargeted). Closes #679. Refs #88, #657, #458.
- **wrapper self-test no longer flakes on dry-run stream interleaving, nor leaks a `log/` tree into the checkout (#624, #622)** — two parallel-suite test-infra fixes. (#624) `prune.sh --worktree-orphans --dry-run prints plan, no real rmi` asserted `[dry-run] docker rmi` against the merged stream; the plan is printed to **stdout** by `_dry_run_cmd` while scan progress (`_log_info`) goes to **stderr**, so under the parallel suite a stderr line could interleave between the plan's `[dry-run]` and ` docker rmi` chunks and break the substring assertion — the test now uses `run --separate-stderr` so the assertion sees stdout only (order-independent). (#622) specs that run a wrapper `main()` teed a transcript `log/<verb>/` tree into the mounted `/source` checkout (root-owned, gitignored but polluting the working tree), because `_transcript_repo_root` resolves to `FILE_PATH`/`REPO_ROOT`/`PWD` which point at the checkout. `_transcript_enabled` now honors a `WRAPPER_TRANSCRIPT` env override (true/false wins over `[logging] wrapper_transcript`), and the self-test runner (`test.sh`) exports `WRAPPER_TRANSCRIPT=false` so no spec writes `log/`; `transcript_spec` clears the override to keep exercising the conf logic (+2 env-override unit tests). Verified: full suite green with zero `log/` written to the checkout. Closes #624, #622. — the main-push-only `coverage` job (`ci.sh --coverage`) had been red on every push to main since ~2026-06-15, masked from PRs because it is not in `ci-rollup`'s `needs:` and its codecov upload uses `fail_ci_if_error: false`. The failures were **test-only**, manifesting solely under the kcov runner (debian `kcov/kcov`: bash 5.2, no `just`, set -x/PS4 instrumentation) while the normal alpine gating jobs (bash 5.3, `just` present) stayed green. A full local kcov run surfaced 12 failures in 3 clusters: (A) the `-vv` bash-trace assertion `[[ "${stderr}" == *"+ "* ]]` in `build_sh_spec` / `exec_sh_spec` / `run_sh_spec` / `stop_sh_spec` — kcov's instrumentation keeps the `+ ` xtrace prefix off stderr; (B) `justfile_user_spec` teardown `[ -n "${TMP_REPO:-}" ] && rm -rf` exiting non-zero on the skip path (`just` absent → `TMP_REPO` unset), which bats turns a clean skip into a teardown failure (7 tests); (C) `upgrade_spec`'s `_get_latest_version` set-e safety test tripping `BASH_SOURCE: unbound variable` in its inner `set -u` shell under kcov. Fix is test-only: clusters A + C skip under `COVERAGE=1` (the behaviours stay fully asserted in the normal non-kcov gating job), cluster B's teardown is guarded with an `if`-block so a skip never produces a non-zero teardown. Verified: kcov run 12 failures -> 0; normal run unchanged (the skipped specs run + assert as plain `ok`). Closes #613. Refs #377, #615.
- **`publish-worker.yaml` multi-platform publish now produces a true multi-arch manifest list (#602)** — the `publish` job was a per-platform matrix where every shard ran `push: true` with a shared `tags: ${{ steps.tags.outputs.tags }}`, so a 2-platform call left a **last-shard-wins single-arch tag** (whichever shard finished second overwrote the tag with its single architecture) — despite the docstring claiming "each platform is published as a separate manifest entry". A consumer pulling the tag on the losing arch got the wrong architecture. The fix mirrors the #587 release-test-tools pattern: each shard now pushes its single-arch image **by digest** (`outputs: ...,push-by-digest=true,name-canonical=true,push=true`, no tag) and uploads its digest as an artifact, and a new `merge` job downloads the digests and assembles the tagged manifest list via `docker buildx imagetools create`. The tag-resolution logic (version tag from `github.ref_name` + `tag_suffix`, plus `:latest${suffix}` when `is_latest`) moves intact from the per-shard step into the merge job, so tags are applied exactly once. All `workflow_call` inputs and the caller invocation are unchanged; single-platform (default `linux/amd64`) publishes are unaffected. Guarded by `test/unit/publish_worker_yaml_spec.bats`. Closes #602. Refs #587.
- **Interactive shells no longer print `groups: cannot find name for group ID N` (#589)** — compose `group_add:` injects the host's numeric device GIDs (e.g. the `render` / `video` group for `/dev/dri`) so the non-root container user can reach those device nodes; those GIDs have no named group inside the image, so every interactive shell emitted a cosmetic `getgrgid()` warning (device access already worked — membership is by GID). A new `config/shell/bashrc.d/30-name-host-groups.sh` drop-in names each nameless supplementary GID as `hostgrp<gid>` via passwordless sudo. It lives in `bashrc.d/` (sourced by `~/.bashrc`), so it fixes both `just run` (entrypoint + bashrc) and `just exec` (compose exec + bashrc, which bypasses the entrypoint); it is idempotent and does not touch the (correct) `group_add` device-access behaviour. Closes #589.
- **`detect_ws_path` final fallback now mounts the repo itself, not its parent (#586)** — when neither workspace layout matched (not a `docker_*` dir with a sibling `*_ws`, no `*_ws` ancestor), `detect_ws_path` fell back to `dirname(base_path)`. base-based repos keep the docker scaffolding at the repo root, so for a self-CI checkout (the GitHub Actions `_work/<repo>/<repo>` convention) this mounted one level too high — the in-container repo landed at `~/work/<repo>/`, breaking relative test paths and the baked `PYTHONPATH`. Consumers had to pin `WS_PATH` in their workflow as a workaround (e.g. `isaac#146`). The fallback now resolves to `base_path` itself, so a base repo that is its own workspace root mounts correctly with no override. Strategies 1 & 2 (the `*_ws`-wrapped layouts) are unchanged; only the final fallback flips from parent to self. Closes #586.
- **`release-worker.yaml` archive step no longer copies root wrappers that no longer exist (#558)** — the archive `cp -r` listed `build.sh` / `run.sh` / `exec.sh` / `stop.sh` / `setup_tui.sh` at the repo root, but those were relocated to `script/` (symlinks into `.base/`) and `init.sh` no longer creates root copies. `cp -r` aborts non-zero on the first missing operand, so the **first `v*` tag push of every standard-layout downstream repo** failed at "Create release archive" (latent until a repo cuts its first release). The five root names are dropped from the list; the wrappers still ship via `script/` (already copied), so `cp -r` resolves the symlinks and the archive keeps runnable wrappers. Guarded by `test/unit/release_worker_yaml_spec.bats`. Closes #558.
- **`just run` no longer flags a clean interactive exit as a recipe failure (#580)** — leaving a no-CMD foreground session (`just run`, or a one-shot stage's foreground `compose up`) carried out the exit status of the *last* command the user ran, not `run.sh`'s own success: an interactive bash exits with `$?` of its last command, so a Ctrl-C-cleared line (`$?=130` = 128 + SIGINT) rode out on the following Ctrl-D / `exit`, `run.sh` propagated `130`, and `just` printed a scary `recipe \`run\` failed ... exit code 130` on a perfectly normal exit. A new `_normalize_interactive_rc` helper normalizes the clean-exit codes (`0` and `130`) to `0` on the two no-CMD foreground paths (devel attached shell + one-shot stage `compose up`), while any other code (e.g. `127`) still propagates so genuine breakage surfaces, and command mode (`just run <cmd>`) keeps propagating the real exit code unchanged for scripting. Unit-tested in `test/unit/run_sh_spec.bats` (6 new specs spanning both facets across devel / non-devel and interactive / command mode). Closes #580.
- **GUI `XAUTHORITY` mount no longer materializes a confusing duplicate `~/workspace` tree in the container HOME (#582)** — the generated `compose.yaml` bind-mounted the X11 cookie at its absolute host path on both sides (`${XAUTHORITY:-/dev/null}:${XAUTHORITY:-/dev/null}:ro`), and `XAUTHORITY` resolves to a deep path like `<...>/workspace/docker/app/<repo>/.docker.xauth`. To host that single file, Docker auto-created the intermediate dirs inside the container, leaving a stray `~/workspace/docker/app/<repo>/` tree next to the real `~/work` mount that looked like it should be the workspace but held only the empty cookie path. The GUI block now mounts the cookie at a fixed neutral target — source stays the host `XAUTHORITY`, target is `/tmp/.docker.xauth` (`${XAUTHORITY:-/dev/null}:/tmp/.docker.xauth:ro`) — and the in-container `XAUTHORITY` env is repointed to that target so X11 clients still find the cookie. Applies to both compose emit sites (devel baseline + per-stage services) and works for the local and SSH-X11 (`#321` ffff-rewritten cookie) cases alike. Unit-tested in `test/unit/compose_gen_spec.bats` (+ golden master + `display_env` smoke updated). Closes #582.

### Added
- **`CONTEXT.md` domain glossary (#570, architecture-review follow-up)** — adds a root `CONTEXT.md` that fixes base's domain vocabulary so future work and architecture reviews stop re-coining terms. Pure glossary (no implementation detail): domain terms (`base` / downstream repo / `.base` subtree contract / wrapper / stage / baseline stage), schema and configuration terms (setup.conf / setup.conf schema / schema registry / per-stage override / env vs workload parameter boundary / field deploy / managed `.gitignore` block), and the architecture seams named by the 2026-06-11 review (resolved-config seam #563, conf accessor handle #564, wrapper runtime #565, per-service compose emitter #566, Dockerfile-migration list #567), plus a relationships section, an example dialogue, and flagged ambiguities. Decisions stay in `doc/adr/`, rationale in the issues. Doc-only. Closes #570. Refs #559.
- **schema registry drift guards (`schema_coverage_spec.bats`, #562, schema epic #559 phase 3)** — a coverage spec that fails CI on registry drift instead of letting it surface at runtime: every `SCHEMA_VALIDATOR` validator name must resolve to a function defined in `_tui_conf.sh`; `SCHEMA_SECTIONS` must equal the `setup.conf` template `[section]` headers in file order (a section added to one but not the other fails); every `SCHEMA_EMPTY` key must be a registered validator key (no dead policy entries); and every registered key must be reachable by walking `SCHEMA_SECTIONS` + `_schema_section_keys` (no key stranded under an unlisted section). Runs automatically as a `test/unit/` spec (no extra wiring). **Scoped down from the original #562:** the i18n-in-all-locales / `kind` / `default` coverage needs a registry i18n-index column that the epic deferred as a future follow-up; that assertion is tracked in a separate issue and is not part of this guard set. Refs #562, #559, #560, #561.
- **conf accessor merge-aware toolkit + first adopter (#563, builds on #564)** — extends the opaque accessor layer with the two verbs the setup.sh merge-based readers need: `_conf_load_merged <template_file> <repo_file> <handle>` loads the template+repo **section-replace** merge (a section the repo defines replaces the template's wholesale; omitted sections keep template values) into a queryable handle, and `_conf_list_sorted <handle> <section> <prefix> <outvar>` returns `<prefix>_N` values in numeric-suffix order (skipping empty opt-outs) — the handle equivalent of `_get_conf_list_sorted`. Adds the `setup.sh` adapter `_setup_conf_handle <base> <handle>` (resolves the SETUP_CONF override / template+repo paths in one place) and migrates `detect_image_name` to read `[image] rule_*` via `_setup_conf_handle` + `_conf_list_sorted` instead of `_load_setup_conf` + `_get_conf_list_sorted` — the first real accessor adopter (behaviour-preserving; all 13 `detect_image_name` specs green), making the #564 seam real. The `_resolve_deploy_context` resolve-once + both-renderer dedup (the #563 core) and the remaining list-reader migrations follow. Unit-tested in `test/unit/conf_accessor_spec.bats`. Refs #563, #564.
- **`conf.sh` opaque accessor interface (#564, architecture deepening)** — new read-side accessor verbs hide `conf.sh`'s internal parallel-array representation and the `<section>.<key>` namespacing rule so callers query setup.conf without knowing the data model: `_conf_load <file> <handle>` tokenizes a file once into a named handle; `_conf_get <handle> <section> <key> [default]` returns a value (last occurrence wins) or the default; `_conf_sections <handle>` lists section names in first-appearance order; `_conf_list <handle> <section>` lists a section's keys in file order (for iterating list sections like `volumes mount_*` / `environment env_*`). The existing `_ini_tokenize` / `_parse_ini_section` / `_load_setup_conf_full` readers are unchanged (the accessors are a thin opaque layer over the tokenizer). Caller adoption lands incrementally starting with the resolved-config seam (#563), which restructures the prime adoption site (`_resolve_deploy_context`) — migrating those call sites now would be churn #563 immediately rewrites. Unit-tested in `test/unit/conf_accessor_spec.bats`. Refs #564, #563.

### Changed
- **self-test `integration-e2e` hardened into a real runnability gate (#579)** — the job ran `init.sh -> build -> run -d -> exec -> stop` but only *executed* the steps without *asserting* results (e.g. it ran `exec whoami` and discarded the output), so the v0.41.0 user-args regression — `whoami` printing `initial` instead of the configured user — stayed green. It now (A3) drives build / run / exec / stop through the documented `just` entry points instead of raw `script/*.sh` (a broken container-ops `justfile` is caught; `just` is installed via `extractions/setup-just`), and (A1) asserts the runnability contract: the in-container user equals `USER_NAME` from `.env.generated` (not `initial` / `root`), the detached container is still running (catches the entrypoint `set -u` insta-exit class), the wired ENTRYPOINT is `/entrypoint.sh`, the `~/work` mount is present and writable, and `just stop` removes both the container and the compose project network. The container / network names are derived at runtime by filtering on the deterministic `e2e_test` image name rather than reconstructing the host-detected compose name. The A2 native-arm64 matrix for this job is deferred (same file, sequenced after this lands). Guarded structurally by `self_test_yaml_spec.bats` (+2) + `actionlint`; the live behaviour is exercised by the e2e job itself. Closes #579.
- **`release-test-tools.yaml` arm64 leg moved off QEMU to a native-runner matrix with push-by-digest manifest merge (#587)** — the test-tools publish workflow emulated arm64 via `docker/setup-qemu-action` on a single `ubuntu-latest` runner (`platforms: linux/amd64,linux/arm64`, one `push: true` producing a correct-but-slow manifest), while `build-worker.yaml` / `publish-worker.yaml` had already moved to native runners. It now builds each arch on its native runner (`linux/amd64` -> `ubuntu-latest`, `linux/arm64` -> `ubuntu-24.04-arm`) via a `compute-matrix` -> `build` -> `merge` pipeline and drops the QEMU step. **Crucially, the multi-arch contract is preserved correctly:** each `build` shard pushes its single-arch image *by digest* (`outputs: ...,push-by-digest=true`, no tag), and a `merge` job assembles the tagged manifest list with `docker buildx imagetools create`. This deliberately does **not** mirror `publish-worker.yaml`'s pattern of each shard pushing the *same tag* — that leaves a last-shard-wins single-arch tag, which for the test-tools image (consumed by every downstream `FROM ${TEST_TOOLS_IMAGE}`) would break CI on the losing arch. (`publish-worker.yaml` carries that latent single-arch bug; tracked separately.) Verified structurally by `release_test_tools_yaml_spec.bats` (+4) and `actionlint`; the live multi-arch publish is exercised by the actual workflow run. Closes #587.
- **setup.conf section list made a single source in `lib/schema.sh` (`SCHEMA_SECTIONS` + accessors, #561, schema epic #559 phase 2)** — phase 1 (#560) unified per-key *validation*; the *section list* was still duplicated as hardcoded alternations (`setup.sh`'s `_setup_known_section` case and `setup_tui.sh`'s `main` CLI direct-jump subcommand list). This adds `SCHEMA_SECTIONS` (the ordered 14-section list, matching the `setup.conf` template header order) plus two accessors to `schema.sh`: `_schema_is_section <section>` (membership predicate) and `_schema_section_keys <section> <outarray>` (a section's registered keys, derived from `SCHEMA_VALIDATOR` by canonical-key prefix). `_setup_known_section` now derives from `_schema_is_section` (keeping the per-service `[logging.<svc>]` shape as the one explicit special case the registry does not model), and `setup_tui.sh`'s CLI subcommand recognition routes through a new `_tui_known_subcommand` (any schema section, plus the `ports` pseudo-section) -- so adding a section to the registry makes it known to `set`/`add` and usable as `setup_tui.sh <section>` automatically. This also closes the latent gap where `lifecycle` / `additional_contexts` / `logging` had editors but were unreachable as direct subcommands. **Deliberately out of scope** (kept explicit): the TUI's four grouped menu dispatches stay per-menu (they are #221 UX grouping guards, not a global section list -- the `*_no_longer_dispatches` regression tests enforce that each menu only acts on its own sections), and `_resolve_deploy_context`'s section-load stays a deliberate 10-section subset (a full `SCHEMA_SECTIONS` loop would change which sections the deploy resolver reads). Full menu-metadata-ization (group / order / label columns) is a future follow-up. `_schema_section_keys`'s first non-test consumer is the #562 coverage test. Behaviour-preserving (full gate green); new tests in `schema_spec.bats` (+10), `setup_spec.bats` (+2), `tui_flow.bats` (+4). Refs #561, #559, #560.
- **setup.conf validation unified behind a single registry (`lib/schema.sh` + `_schema_validate`, #560, schema epic #559 phase 1)** — validation for "is this `<section>.<key> = <value>` valid?" was wired twice: setup.sh's `_setup_validate_kv` (a per-section `case`) and the TUI's per-call-site validator names threaded through `_edit_list_section` / inline scalar editors. The two could (and did) drift. This introduces `script/docker/lib/schema.sh` -- a flat dotted-key registry (`SCHEMA_VALIDATOR` mapping a canonical `<section>.<key>` to the validator function name that still lives in `_tui_conf.sh`; `SCHEMA_EMPTY` for the empty-value policy) plus one dispatcher `_schema_validate <section> <key> <value>` that normalises per-service `[logging.<svc>]` sections and numbered list keys (`port_3` -> `port_`) before lookup. Both paths now route through it: `_setup_validate_kv` collapses to a one-line adapter, and the TUI's `_edit_list_entry` / `_edit_list_section` drop their validator parameter while every scalar / logging editor calls `_schema_validate` keyed by `(section, key)`. schema.sh joins the `_lib.sh` chain (and setup_tui.sh's). **Behavioural consequence -- the rule sets are now the union:** keys the TUI already validated but setup.sh's `set` / `add` historically accepted as free-form are now rejected by `set` / `add` too: `build.target_arch`, `build.network`, `build.arg_*`, `deploy.gpu_runtime` (+ legacy `runtime` alias), `network.network_name`, `devices.device_*`, `security.cap_add_*` / `cap_drop_*`. Only previously-invalid values are affected; a valid `setup.conf` is unchanged, and compose / `.env` output is byte-identical (full gate green: 1676 unit + 74 integration). The registry's `kind` / `default` / `i18n` columns and section enumeration are deferred to phase 2 (#561), where dispatch / menu / section-load derivation will consume them. New `test/unit/schema_spec.bats` (14). Refs #560, #559.
- **per-service compose emitter extracted from the `generate_compose_yaml` god-function (#566, architecture deepening)** — the ~1015-line `generate_compose_yaml` (29 positional args, ~16 nested emitter closures, and the per-stage service loop all in one scope) is split into a thin orchestrator plus testable seams.
